# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **HKDF-SHA256 key derivation for encrypted archives (`KeyFile::key_derivations`)** — optional `BTreeMap<String, Derivation>` sibling to the existing `keys` map in `rustgate_core::encrypted::KeyFile`. Operators holding a single high-entropy root secret (KMS blob, HSM output, another rustgate deployment's master key) can now derive multiple archive subkeys deterministically without re-provisioning raw hex. Each entry takes the shape `{ "kdf": "hkdf-sha256", "ikm": "<hex>", "salt": "<hex>", "info": "<utf8>" }`; the `tag = "kdf"` envelope leaves room for future Argon2id variants for passphrase-derived keys, which is explicitly out-of-scope for this change (HKDF adds no work factor and is unsuitable for low-entropy passphrases). `KeyRing::from_bytes` evaluates derivations after raw entries, inserting the 32-byte result into the same in-memory map so derived keys are indistinguishable from raw keys at use time — they can be the active key, a historical decrypt key, and carry a `key_expirations` entry identically. An id appearing in both `keys` and `key_derivations` is rejected before any decoding or derivation, because silently preferring one entry would hide the operator typo. IKM is floored at 16 bytes (HKDF adds no entropy — a trivially-short IKM produces a trivially-short-security subkey), empty salt follows RFC 5869 § 3.1 (maps to `Hkdf::new(None, ikm)`), and intermediate decoded IKM lives inside `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` so it is wiped the moment HKDF's extract stage is done with it. A new `EncryptError::KeyDerivation { id, reason }` variant surfaces per-entry failures without leaking IKM or salt bytes into the error string. Thirteen new tests pin the wiring: RFC 5869 Test Case 3 exact vector (empty salt + empty info, the canonical case where the spec's info octets align with UTF-8), info-separation independence, derived-key end-to-end round-trip through `EncryptedWriter` + `decrypt_file`, mixed raw-and-derived key files, id-overlap rejection, IKM floor, invalid hex IKM, empty-salt acceptance, derived-only configuration, the updated "not found in keys or key_derivations" error message, derivations honoring `key_expirations`, unknown-KDF rejection at parse time (so an older binary reading a newer key file with `argon2id` fails loudly rather than silently ignoring the entry), and the `serde` round-trip with `skip_serializing_if` so pre-KDF key files remain byte-identical. Adds the `hkdf = "0.12"` crate to the workspace (RustCrypto, small, depends on already-present `hmac` + `sha2`). (Refs #883)
- **MQTT post-route egress fan-out to NATS / federation / HTTP sinks** — `rustgate-mqtt` PUBLISH hot path now threads the same three-way egress fan-out the WebSocket transport has carried since #890, so operators who front their deployment with MQTT clients get scale-out via the NATS bridge, server-to-server delivery via the federation forwarder, and webhook delivery via the HTTP publisher without having to bridge through a second transport. The three callback type aliases (`OnNatsPublish`, `OnFederationForward`, `OnHttpForward`) are promoted from `rustgate-websocket::server` (where they originated) to a new `rustgate-core::egress` module so any future session-level transport (WebTransport, QUIC, raw TCP) can reuse the same shapes without depending on a sibling transport crate; `rustgate-websocket` re-exports them through `server` for backward compatibility with existing call sites. A new `MqttForwardingHooks` bundle (`nats_publish`, `federation_forward`, `http_forward`) sits alongside the existing `MqttSessionHooks` on `MqttServerConfig`; both use independent `Option<Arc<dyn Fn>>` slots so each sink can be disabled individually and the `None`-bundle case stays at zero runtime cost. On the session side, `handle_publish_v5` and `handle_publish_v4` now call `router.route_for_egress(msg).await` (the Send-PEP-aware cousin of the old `route()` that returns `None` when the router drops the message via dedup / TTL / Send-authz deny / oversize) and only fire the three callbacks when the router returns `Some(Arc<RoutableMessage>)` — so a dedup'd or denied publish acks the client but does not fan out. A new `fire_forwarding` helper is called *before* the PUBLISH admission observer (`on_publish`) so callbacks see every routable message including those that never make it to the metric; the PUBACK path is lifted into `ack_publish_v5` / `ack_publish_v4` helpers to keep the two versions consistent. The server crate's `start_mqtt` entry point is refactored into a `MqttSetup<'a, P>` struct (mirroring the existing `WebSocketSetup`) so the call site threads `nats_publisher`, `fed_forward`, and `http_forward` by reference, each leg feature-gated behind `#[cfg(feature = "nats|federation|http")]`; the `MqttSetup` struct carries the narrow `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` justification the old function did. Six new tests pin the contract: four inline `config::tests` (default-all-None, Debug-None rendering, Debug-Some-each-field rendering, and Arc-strong-count-sharing under Clone — mirroring the existing `MqttSessionHooks` suite) and two new `mqtt_integration.rs` tests (`publish_fires_all_three_forwarding_callbacks_once` — mTLS-authenticated PUBLISH fires all three sinks exactly once with the same post-route payload + the publisher's cert-derived `alpha` group on the federation leg; `publish_with_no_forwarding_hooks_does_not_panic` — `None`-bundle QoS 1 PUBLISH still returns a clean PUBACK). (Closes #903)
- **NATS request/reply pattern (`NatsRequestClient`, `NatsRequestResponder`)** — new `crates/rustgate-nats/src/request.rs` module adds bidirectional query support over the existing NATS connection, separate from the streaming pub/sub bridge. `NatsAdapter::request_client()` wraps `async_nats::Client::send_request` with a 5-second default per-call timeout (override via `with_timeout` or `request_with_timeout`) and maps the `async-nats` error taxonomy into a rustgate-owned `RequestError` enum with four explicit variants: `Timeout { subject, timeout }`, `NoResponders { subject }`, `HandlerError { subject, message }`, and `Client { subject, source }` — so callers can pattern-match the common failure modes without depending on the `async-nats` crate directly. `NatsAdapter::request_responder(handler)` returns `Option<NatsRequestResponder>` — `Some` only when the new `NatsConfig::request_subject: Option<String>` is set, making the responder strictly opt-in. The responder subscribes to the configured subject, dispatches each request to the caller-supplied handler on an independent `spawn_background` task (slow or panicking handlers cannot block the loop), and publishes the handler's result on `msg.reply`; handler errors are relayed via a new `NATS-Rustgate-Error` reply header that the client side decodes into `RequestError::HandlerError` to distinguish "handler rejected the request" from "no responders" or "timeout". The handler signature is `Arc<dyn Fn(Bytes) -> Pin<Box<dyn Future<Output = Result<Bytes, String>> + Send>> + Send + Sync>` (aliased as `NatsRequestHandler`) — the same async-callback shape used elsewhere in the workspace (see `rustgate_enrollment::contacts::ContactsProvider`). Nine new config validation tests reject wildcards, control characters, whitespace, empty strings, and non-ASCII in `request_subject`, and confirm the default is `None` (zero behaviour change for existing deployments). Five unit tests in `request.rs` pin the error-display format, the `ERROR_HEADER` constant value, the default timeout, and that the handler alias accepts idiomatic async closures. Five integration tests (all gated on `require_nats!()`) cover the happy-path round trip with an echo handler, `NoResponders` on an unrelated subject when a responder is running elsewhere, `HandlerError` propagation via the reply header, `Timeout` when the handler sleeps past the per-call budget, and the "no `request_subject` → `request_responder` returns `None`" opt-in contract. Closes #907b.
- **NATS consumer-side subject filtering (`NatsConfig::subject_filters`)** — optional `Vec<String>` on the NATS bridge that narrows which subjects the JetStream pull consumer asks for at the wire level, instead of receiving every `{subject_prefix}.>` message and filtering after receipt in `extract_group_from_subject`. Empty (the default) preserves the existing `{prefix}.>` wildcard behavior, a single entry is passed as JetStream's `filter_subject`, and two or more entries go to `filter_subjects` (requires server 2.10+, which is now enabled on the workspace `async-nats` dependency via the zero-cost `server_2_10` feature flag). NATS wildcards (`*` single-token, `>` tail) are accepted inside each filter so operators can subscribe to narrow slices like `points.ALPHA.>` or `points.*.update.v2`. Validation enforces that every entry starts with `{subject_prefix}.` — a filter that escapes the stream's subject space would bind to an always-empty consumer and silently drop all traffic — and rejects empty strings, control chars, whitespace, DEL, and non-ASCII the same way `subject_prefix` itself is guarded. `build_consumer_config` owns the dispatch between the singular and plural wire fields and leaves the unused one at its default (`""` / `vec![]`) because JetStream treats both-populated as a consumer-create-time error. Fifteen new unit tests (ten in `config.rs` covering the round-trip, multi-pattern, wildcard-allowance, out-of-prefix rejection, exact-prefix rejection, empty-entry, control-char, space, DEL, and non-ASCII paths, plus validation-ordering against a bad `subject_prefix`; five in `subscriber.rs` pinning empty-falls-back-to-wildcard, single-entry-uses-singular, multi-entry-uses-plural, insertion-order preservation, and the explicit `{prefix}.>` round-trip). No behavioural change for existing deployments — the field defaults to an empty vec and the wire stays on `{prefix}.>` until an operator opts in. (Refs #907)
- **Circuit breaker `OnStateChange` observer hook + HTTP publisher auto-drain trigger point** — `rustgate-resilience::circuit::CircuitBreaker` gains a lock-free observer callback set once at construction time via the new `.with_on_state_change(cb)` builder. The callback alias `pub type OnStateChange = Arc<dyn Fn(u8, u8) + Send + Sync>` receives `(old_state, new_state)` on every transition using the newly-promoted `pub const STATE_CLOSED / STATE_OPEN / STATE_HALF_OPEN: u8` constants so callers can pattern-match instead of hardcoding 0/1/2. The observer is stored in a `OnceLock` — a second `with_on_state_change` call silently no-ops per OnceLock semantics, and the zero-observer path costs a single atomic pointer check per transition (no extra work on the hot `allow_request` path). Transitions fire the observer inside the existing `prev != STATE_*` guards in `close()` / `trip()` / `try_half_open()`, so redundant same-state writes do not spam subscribers (pinned by `on_state_change_does_not_fire_on_identical_state_writes`). The struct's derived `Debug` is replaced with a manual impl that renders each atomic/counter via `Ordering::Relaxed` loads and prints a boolean `configured` flag for the callback field via `finish_non_exhaustive()` — matching the workspace pattern used by `HttpPublisher`, `SpiffeHooks`, `AuthState`, and `JwtAuthority` (a `dyn Fn` cannot derive `Debug`). Downstream, `rustgate-http::publisher::build_target` now attaches a per-target observer via `build_circuit_observer(&tc.name)` that fires only on `Open → HalfOpen` (the cooldown-elapsed "target probably recovered" moment the DLQ auto-drain needs); the other transitions already emit `http_circuit_tripped` / `http_circuit_recovered` at the `record_failure` / `record_success` call sites in `publish_with_retry` and firing the observer there too would double-log. A new `audit::http_circuit_probing(target)` event labels the probe-window entry for the forensic trail, and the Prometheus `rustgate_http_circuit_state{target}` gauge now reaches value `2` (half-open) during the probe window so dashboards can distinguish "target is being re-tested" from "target is healthy" and from "target is tripped". Seven new breaker-level tests pin the full recovery cycle (Closed→Open→HalfOpen→Closed in order), the set-once silence contract, the None-by-default zero-cost path, and each of the four transition arms individually; four new HTTP publisher tests pin direct-invocation, unrelated-transition no-op, end-to-end gauge behaviour via a real breaker, and the `Send + Sync` bounds on the callback alias. No behavioural change for operators who never configured the observer — the zero-cost path is the default. Refs #881 (DLQ auto-drain on target recovery — the observer wiring is the infrastructure piece; the replay of dead-lettered messages back through the publisher channel is the follow-up now that the probe-window trigger exists).
- **`rustgate cert-info` and `rustgate crl-info` diagnostic subcommands** — two offline inspection tools that parse PEM-encoded X.509 certificates and CRLs without having to start the server, addressing Findings F-178 and F-183 of issue #900. Both live under the new `crates/rustgate-server/src/diagnostics/` module. The `cert-info` command emits `CertReport` with 22 stable fields (subject CN, issuer CN, serial hex, `notBefore` / `notAfter` in RFC 3339, `valid_now` / `expired` booleans, `seconds_until_expiry` signed i64, SHA-256 fingerprint, organization, country, groups, SPIFFE ID, SAN DNS / URI / IP / email, key usage, extended key usage, `is_ca`, signature algorithm). Groups extraction exactly mirrors `rustgate_transport::extract::extract_groups` — splits OU values on `,` with a JSON-array `[...]` fallback — so the diagnostic view stays consistent with what the runtime router sees. The `crl-info` command emits `CrlReport` (issuer CN, `thisUpdate` / `nextUpdate`, `stale` boolean, `seconds_until_next_update`, revoked count, and a `Vec<RevokedEntry>` with serial hex + revocation date + optional RFC 5280 reason). Both subcommands take a PEM file path and an optional `--json` flag; the JSON shape is the public contract, pinned by 20 new unit tests (11 `cert_info` + 9 `crl_info`) that cover happy-path fields, IPv4 / IPv6 / unknown-length IP SAN formatting, expired-cert detection, missing file, garbage input, CRL freshness, multi-entry CRLs, and stable JSON layout. The existing `rustls-pki-types` + `x509-parser` deps are promoted from `optional` to unconditional in `rustgate-server/Cargo.toml` (both are already transitive through `rustgate-transport` so this adds no build cost); `time = { workspace = true }` is added for RFC 3339 rendering. The dispatch in `main.rs` runs before tokio/tracing/config init so the commands are usable in break-glass scenarios on hosts that cannot otherwise launch the server. Human-readable output is deliberately *not* a contract and may evolve.
- **Offline reader for encrypted DLQ files (`rustgate_http::dlq_reader`)** — companion to the writer in `crates/rustgate-http/src/dlq.rs`. Decrypts a `.dlq.enc` artifact with the operator's `KeyRing` and yields typed `OwnedDlqEntry` records for forensic review or replay. Two entry points: `read_dlq_file(path, key_ring) -> Vec<OwnedDlqEntry>` for the common "load it all" case and `for_each_dlq_entry(path, key_ring, cb)` for streaming consumers that want to stop on first callback error (e.g. a future replay driver that halts on the first undeliverable record). Decryption lands in a `tempfile::NamedTempFile` scoped to the function call so plaintext never persists to disk beyond the reader's lifetime. The parser is a `serde_json::Deserializer::into_iter` stream, so concatenated JSON objects with no delimiter (the current writer shape) and any future whitespace-delimited variant both parse correctly. Errors are categorised as `DlqReadError::{Decrypt,Io,Json,PayloadDecode}` so operators can distinguish AEAD integrity failures (wrong key, torn write, tampered chunk, unknown key id) from schema regressions, I/O issues, or malformed base64 payloads. Ten inline tests pin the contract: end-to-end round-trip against the writer (multi-entry, binary payload, empty payload), sealed-but-empty file acceptance, wrong-key rejection, unterminated-file rejection (simulated truncation after writer drop), missing-file error shape, early-abort on callback error, binary-payload base64 round-trip, invalid-base64 rejection, concatenated-JSON parsing without delimiters, and strict-required-field deserialization. (Refs #881)
- **HTTP DLQ per-target total-bytes cap with drop-oldest FIFO eviction** — `crates/rustgate-http/src/dlq.rs` gains an optional `dlq-max-target-bytes` config field that bounds the total on-disk DLQ footprint for a single target across every rotated file. When the sealed-tail footprint exceeds the cap after a rotation, the oldest `.dlq.enc` segments are deleted until the footprint fits; the currently-active segment is always preserved so the live `SharedEncryptedWriter`'s in-memory state cannot be corrupted. The sweep hooks into the rotation path (not every write) so the I/O cost is amortised across rotations, and a new `rustgate_http_dlq_evicted_total{target}` Prometheus counter labels each eviction by target name. Config validation rejects `dlq-max-target-bytes < dlq-max-bytes` (a per-target cap smaller than a single rotated file would leave windows with zero sealed files after rotation) and accepts `0` as the documented "unlimited" sentinel. The `DLQ_FILE_SUFFIX` constant pins the scanner's glob so a future refactor cannot silently desynchronise the suffix between writer and evictor. Closes part 1 of #881 (unbounded DLQ growth during extended outages).
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport::ocsp` stapler wrapper (concrete return type, `Send + Sync + 'static` bounds, per-inner independence, idempotent preload)** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/ocsp.rs` is the ~70-line OCSP-stapling seam that wraps any `Arc<dyn ResolvesServerCert>` (typically `CertReloader`) with `ocsp_stapler::Stapler` so the rustls server config can hand stapled responses to clients without them having to contact the issuing CA's responder. Before this PR the `#[cfg(feature = "ocsp")]`-gated inline suite had exactly one test — `stapler_delegates_resolve_to_inner` — which constructed the wrapper and called `.stop().await` but pinned no contract on the *shape* of the return value, the auto-traits that rustls depends on, or the idempotence of `preload()`. Four additional contract clauses are added in a single batched `wrap_resolver_and_preload_contract_batch` test (intentionally a single `#[tokio::test]` to amortize the ~55s tokio-runtime teardown cost — each `Stapler` spawns a background fetch loop whose shutdown cannot be short-circuited from the test body, so splitting the four clauses into four tests would multiply the runtime-teardown delay by four with no isolation benefit): (1) **concrete `Arc<Stapler>` return type** — a refactor that narrowed the signature to `-> Arc<dyn ResolvesServerCert>` would silently break `stapler.preload(...)` and `stapler.stop()` call sites in `TlsConfig::build_reloadable_server_config` where the concrete type is load-bearing; (2) **`Send + Sync + 'static` bounds survive on both the concrete and the trait-object-erased form** via a `fn assert_bounds<T: Send + Sync + 'static>(_: &T)` compile-time self-check, so a regression that introduced an `Rc`, a non-`Send` `dyn Fn`, or a non-`'static` borrow inside `Stapler` fails test-compile rather than at the first transitive `rustls::ServerConfig` call site; (3) **distinct inner resolvers produce independent staplers** via `!Arc::ptr_eq(&stapler_a, &stapler_b)`, pinning that `wrap_resolver` is not secretly coalescing into a shared global singleton (a regression would be invisible to single-reloader tests and surface only in multi-SNI-leaf deploys that serve several leaves from one process); (4) **`preload()` is idempotent under the same certified key** — two successive `preload(&stapler, ckey)` / `preload(&stapler, ckey_second)` calls with `Arc::clone`d handles to the same `CertifiedKey` must not panic or double-enqueue a fetch, pinning the leaf-cert-fingerprint dedup the upstream stapler relies on. A helper `build_reloader_with_tempdir(cn)` returns `(Arc<dyn ResolvesServerCert>, TempDir)` so each clause gets an independent backing tempdir without duplicating the `TestCa` → `issue_server_cert` → `CertReloader::new` boilerplate, and the tuple return keeps the tempdir alive for the duration of the test. No behavioural change to the module under test. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for the `rustgate-federation` REST API request/response envelope types** — `crates/rustgate-federation/src/api/types.rs` defines the on-the-wire schema for the federation management API: `CreateFederateRequest` with its `default_true` helper and `#[serde(default)]`-annotated fields (`groups`, `is_outbound`, `routing_mode`), `UpdateFederateRequest` with the `Option<Option<T>>` double-option shape on `inbound_groups` / `outbound_groups` / `trust_cert_cn`, `SetRoutingModeRequest`, and the `pub(crate)` `SuccessResponse<T>` / `ErrorResponse` envelopes every handler returns. Before this PR the inline suite had only three tests — all `deny_unknown_fields` rejection checks — leaving the default-value plumbing, the `Option<Option<T>>` two-state contract, the serialize shape of both envelopes, and the `default_true` helper itself completely unpinned. A silent refactor that (a) flipped `default_true` to `|| false` (dormant-by-default peers), (b) widened `#[serde(default = "default_true")]` to bare `#[serde(default)]` (`bool::default()` is `false` — same observable effect), (c) collapsed `Option<Option<Vec<String>>>` to a single `Option<Vec<String>>` on `UpdateFederateRequest` (silently shifting the storage layout — any call site that pattern-matches on `Some(Some(_))` / `None` would break), (d) renamed the `data` / `error` envelope keys or switched to `finish_non_exhaustive()`-style Debug output on the request types (breaking every REST consumer and every request-log field enumeration), or (e) changed the `RoutingMode` default from `Matrix` to `Broadcast` (silently fanning out every federated event to every peer) could all ship green. Eleven new inline tests close those gaps: `create_federate_request_enabled_defaults_true` and `create_federate_request_enabled_explicit_false_overrides_default` bracket the `default_true` helper on both the omission path and the explicit-false override path; `create_federate_request_is_outbound_defaults_false`, `create_federate_request_groups_defaults_empty` (also asserts `inbound_groups`/`outbound_groups` stay `None`, not `Some(vec![])`), `create_federate_request_routing_mode_defaults_matrix`, and `create_federate_request_trust_cert_cn_defaults_none` pin every remaining default field; `update_federate_request_double_option_is_two_state_under_stock_serde` pins the actual two-state contract across all three double-Option fields (omitted → outer `None`, JSON `null` → outer `None` identically — no `deserialize_with` hook is wired up so null and omitted collapse together — and value present → `Some(Some(v))`), documenting the gotcha that the PATCH endpoint cannot "clear" a previously-set field by sending `null` under stock serde. If someone later adds a custom deserializer to distinguish null from omitted, the null-case assertion flips from `None` to `Some(None)` and this test fails loud, forcing the PR to explicitly migrate the contract and think about whether the downstream store-update path handles `Some(None)` as "clear"; `update_federate_request_groups_is_single_option_not_double` and `update_federate_request_scalar_fields_single_option` pin the deliberate asymmetry where `groups`, `address`, and `is_outbound` use a single `Option<T>` rather than the double pattern — a "normalize the schema" refactor would change the observable state-space of the surrounding fields and is caught here; `success_response_serializes_as_data_wrapper` and `error_response_serializes_as_error_string` pin the envelope shapes that Helm chart probes and every REST consumer depend on (the envelopes are `pub(crate)`, which is why the pin lives in the same-crate test module rather than an integration test); `request_types_debug_shape` verifies `Debug` starts with the struct name and does not carry `..` (forbids a drift to `finish_non_exhaustive()` on types the API layer logs at validation-failure time); and `default_true_helper_returns_true` pins the helper directly so a regression localizes to the helper rather than forcing triangulation through a deserialize diff. Fourteen tests total (three existing + eleven new), zero changes to any production API. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-server::archive_rotation` derive, sweep ordering, and accounting invariants** — `crates/rustgate-server/src/archive_rotation.rs` owns the size- and time-based retention policy for the CoT event archive (the `run_pass(dir, max_bytes, max_age, active_path)` free function called from the background rotation task and the `RotationConfig` / `RotationStats` types it closes over). Before this PR the inline suite pinned five behaviours — oldest-first size eviction with five files, max-age eviction, the fully-unbounded no-op path, active-segment exclusion from both accounting and deletion, and the missing-directory short-circuit — but left six contract-shaped invariants unpinned that a refactor could silently break without tripping the tracing logs or the integration layer. Nine new inline tests close those gaps: `rotation_stats_default_is_zero` pins `RotationStats::default()` returning `bytes_after = 0, files_deleted = 0` so the early-return short-circuits in `run_pass` (missing directory) keep the documented "nothing happened" shape; `rotation_stats_is_copy_and_clone` exercises both derives explicitly — pinning Copy by binding to a second variable *after* moving the original (a Clone-only type would fail to compile) so a future `#[derive]` edit that drops `Copy` fails test-compile instead of silently forcing the background loop's `match join { Ok(Ok(stats)) => …, stats.bytes_after, stats.files_deleted }` double-field read onto a moved-from local; `rotation_config_is_cloneable` drives every field of `RotationConfig::clone()` with a fully populated struct (PathBuf, three u64/Duration scalars, `Option<Arc<CotArchiver>>` = None) so the Clone derive keeps the `spawn(cfg.clone())` dispatch contract working even if a future field is added without an explicit Clone bound; `rotation_skips_subdirectories` creates a sibling `backup/` subdir containing a 4 KiB file under the archive root, runs a sweep with a 1 KiB cap, and asserts `files_deleted = 0`, `bytes_after = 512` (only the top-level file counts) and `subdir.is_dir()` survives — pinning the `meta.is_file()` guard inside `collect_entries` that prevents the sweep from attempting `fs::remove_file` on a directory (which would leak a warn log and, worse, miscount the cap — if the 4 KiB subdir file were counted, `bytes_after` would be 4608 and the sweep would spuriously trigger); `rotation_empty_dir_returns_zero_stats` is the complement to the existing missing-dir test — the directory exists but contains no files, and both sweeps must be no-ops (distinguishes "directory not yet created" from "directory drained to empty by the previous pass"); `rotation_size_sweep_stops_once_under_cap` pins the `while total > max_bytes` loop termination — three 1 KiB files with a 2 KiB cap must delete exactly the oldest one (bringing total to 2 KiB, == cap, ending the loop), *not* continue deleting out of zeal — a regression that changed the condition to `>=` would delete two files instead of one and fail this test immediately; `rotation_age_sweep_runs_before_size_sweep` builds a scenario where the size sweep alone is a no-op (three files totalling 512 bytes with a 1 KiB cap) but an ancient 256-byte file still exits via the age sweep — pinning the documented order (age first, then size) so a reversal would leave the ancient file in place and fail the test; `rotation_combined_age_and_size_hits_both_sweeps` layers four 1 KiB files where the ancient one is removed by age and then the size sweep removes the next-oldest survivor to bring the total under a 2 KiB cap — pins the deletion counter accumulates across both passes (`files_deleted == 2`) and that the size pass picks the oldest-remaining file after the age pass; `rotation_bytes_after_matches_disk_reality` writes three files totalling 900 bytes, runs an unbounded sweep, and asserts `bytes_after == 900` — pins the arithmetic that feeds the `rustgate_archive_bytes` Prometheus gauge in the background task's success arm, so a future refactor that accidentally reported `total_before` (pre-sweep) or the sum including the active segment would fail loudly. Coverage grows from 5 to 14 tests; zero changes to any production code path — the existing rotation helpers and config struct are untouched, only the `mod tests` block grows. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-federation::discovery::etcd` backend key-value decode contract, defaults, `parse_kvs` ordering, constructor side-effect-freedom, and `Debug` redaction** — `crates/rustgate-federation/src/discovery/etcd.rs` is the etcd v3 peer-discovery backend gated behind the `peer-discovery-etcd` sub-feature; the reconciler calls `EtcdDiscovery::discover` on every tick to rescan the configured key prefix (default `rustgate/peers/`) and decode each key-value pair into a `PeerConfig`. Before this PR the inline suite had five tests (plain `host:port` decode, JSON `PeerConfig` decode, empty-value rejection, invalid-JSON rejection, `parse_kvs` bad-entry skip) that covered the happy-path branches but left ten defensive invariants unpinned — the kinds of contracts a future refactor could silently break without a single wire-level integration test failing. Ten new inline tests close the gap: `decode_entry_with_key_not_matching_prefix_uses_full_key_as_id` pins the `key.strip_prefix(prefix).unwrap_or(key)` fallback — an operator who flips the configured prefix mid-flight, or a future etcd quirk that returns a key past the `with_prefix()` filter, must still get a usable peer id (the full key) rather than a silently-dropped entry; `decode_entry_with_key_equal_to_prefix_returns_none` pins the empty-id-after-strip guard — an accidental `etcdctl put rustgate/peers/ 10.0.0.1:9000` at the bare prefix must surface as a warning + `None` rather than silently producing a peer with an empty id that would collide with every other prefix-exact mistake in the federation manager's peer map; `decode_entry_treats_whitespace_only_value_as_empty` pins the `value.trim().is_empty()` routing so a value like `"   \n\t  "` takes the empty-value warning branch instead of the plain-`host:port` branch where it would produce a peer with an empty address; `decode_entry_trims_surrounding_whitespace_from_plain_host_port` pins the same `trim()` on the success path so operators who accidentally store `"  10.0.0.1:9000\n"` via a shell pipeline that preserved a trailing newline still get a valid peer config; `decode_entry_plain_mode_applies_documented_defaults` exhaustively pins the 11 hardcoded fields in plain mode (`groups=["__ANON__"]`, `inbound_groups=None`, `outbound_groups=None`, `enabled=true`, `is_outbound=true`, `trust_cert_cn=None`, `routing_mode=RoutingMode::default()`, `protocol_version=2`, `jwt_token=None`, `region=None`, `zone=None`, `priority=100`) so a silent flip — an `enabled=false` during a "disable peers by default" refactor, or a `protocol_version` bump to 3 — fails a focused unit test rather than surfacing as a production federation outage; `decode_entry_json_id_is_taken_from_body_not_key_suffix` pins that the JSON branch hands the serialised `PeerConfig` straight to `serde_json::from_str` and does **not** override the `id` with the key suffix — operators who store `id: "fleet-a"` in the JSON body under `rustgate/peers/node-42` get a peer whose id is `fleet-a`, and a future refactor that "helpfully" took the key suffix as the id would be caught here; `parse_kvs_preserves_order_of_valid_entries` pins the `filter_map` iteration-order contract — the reconciler's `FederationManager::swap_peers` diff depends on stable ordering to avoid bogus remove/add churn every tick when the etcd contents are unchanged, and a non-deterministic reordering would silently increase churn; `backend_name_is_stable_etcd_token` (async via `#[tokio::test]`) pins both the exact `"etcd"` token operators grep for in reconciler log lines and the `'static` lifetime (no allocated `String`) via a `&dyn PeerDiscovery` coercion; `new_stores_endpoints_and_prefix_without_connecting` pins that the constructor is side-effect-free with an unreachable endpoint (`http://10.255.255.1:2379`) — the connect is deliberately deferred to the first `discover` call so a transient etcd cluster outage at rustgate startup cannot prevent the process from launching, and an eager connect would either hang the test on the connect future or fail with a connection error; `debug_impl_redacts_client_slot_via_finish_non_exhaustive` pins the manual `Debug` impl so `tracing::debug!(?backend, …)` renders `endpoints` + `prefix` but elides the `client: Mutex<Option<Client>>` slot via `finish_non_exhaustive()` — a regression that swapped to `#[derive(Debug)]` would leak the etcd gRPC client's internal connection state into logs. Total suite grows from 5 to 15 tests; no behavioural change to the module under test. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport::client_ocsp` — `FETCH_QUEUE_CAPACITY = 256` constant pin, `cache_key` SHA-256 identity across five dimensions (determinism, single-bit-flip avalanche, reissued-cert differentiation, interleaved-call functional purity, FIPS 180-4 empty-input reference vector, fold-free full-DER key derivation), `CachedStatus` `Copy + Clone + Debug` compile-time bounds and Debug-variant rendering, `OcspFetcher` trait `Send + Sync + 'static` bounds, `OnCacheHit` / `OnCacheMiss` callback alias `Send + Sync + Clone` bounds, `FetchFuture<'_>` alias `Pin<Box<dyn Future + Send>>` shape, `ClientOcspVerifier` custom-Debug redaction (renders only `cache_entries` count, must not leak `FakeFetcher` / `WebPkiClientVerifier` / inner subject-hint bytes), `new(None, None)` smoke test, `cache_len()` accessor agreement with background-fetcher inserts, `schedule_fetch` silent drop on saturated 256-capacity channel (using `futures::future::pending` to starve the drain task), `verify_client_cert` three-path coverage (cached-`Good` accept, cached-`Revoked` reject with error message naming revocation, inner-verifier rejection propagates via custom `AlwaysReject` impl without cache consult), `on_hit` callback fires exactly once on a `Good` cache hit, `on_miss` callback fires exactly once on cold cache and schedules a background fetch, `root_hint_subjects` / `supported_verify_schemes` delegation to the inner verifier (compared via `DistinguishedName::as_ref().to_vec()` because the type lacks `PartialEq`), and background fetch-task lifecycle via `Arc::downgrade` (spawned task must exit cleanly when the owning verifier `Arc` is dropped)** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/client_ocsp.rs` is the Tier 1 client-certificate OCSP verifier that sits between every `rustls`-accepted handshake and the router's session admission path: the `WebPkiClientVerifier` inside validates chain-of-trust and path construction, then `ClientOcspVerifier` consults a SHA-256-keyed revocation cache and fires an async OCSP fetch into a bounded `mpsc::Sender<CertificateDer>` when the cache is cold. Before this PR the inline suite (three tests) covered `cache_key` consumer-visible determinism, a single cached-`Good` accept case, and the `FETCH_QUEUE_CAPACITY` numeric value — but it left the entire defensive invariant surface (custom Debug redaction, callback alias bounds, trait-object Send/Sync contracts, saturation drop, revoked-reject error messaging, errored-fetch cache-empty guarantee, unknown-key-shape fallback, empty-issuer no-intermediate edge, and background-task lifecycle) untested at unit level, any of which could silently regress without a single integration test noticing until a deployed mTLS listener started serving revoked certs or leaking cert bytes into `tracing::warn!(?verifier, …)` log output. Twenty-four new inline tests close those gaps. Cache-key coverage: `cache_key_is_deterministic` re-derives the same key twice from the same input and asserts equality (a regression that folded in a random nonce or `HashMap` iteration order would fail here); `cache_key_is_sha256_of_full_der` cross-checks against the external `sha2::Sha256::digest` of the same input bytes to pin the specific hash function (a silent swap to `Blake3` or `SHA-3` would produce a valid-looking but different digest and fail against the reference implementation); `cache_key_of_empty_input_matches_sha256_empty_vector` is the FIPS 180-4 empty-input regression guard (digest must equal `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855`); `cache_key_differs_on_single_bit_flip` flips the lowest bit of one byte in a 512-byte test vector and asserts Hamming distance ≥ 16 of 32 bytes differ (this is the SHA-256 avalanche property — a regression to a length-only or truncated hash would fail here); `cache_key_is_purely_functional_on_interleaved_calls` calls `cache_key(a); cache_key(b); cache_key(a);` and asserts the first and third outputs agree byte-for-byte (pinning no hidden mutable state — a regression that memoised through a static `OnceLock` with `HashMap` key collisions would fail here); `cache_key_differs_for_reissued_cert` uses `TestCa::issue_client_cert` twice for the same CN and asserts the resulting DERs hash to different keys (pinning the "reissuance rotates the cache key" property the revocation freshness guarantee depends on — a regression that keyed on subject-plus-serial rather than full-DER-bytes would silently share cache entries between issuances and defeat the OCSP freshness contract). Type-surface pins: `cached_status_is_copy_clone_debug` uses `fn assert_bounds<T: Copy + Clone + Debug>()` parameterised on `CachedStatus`; `ocsp_fetcher_trait_is_send_sync_static` pins the trait-object bounds (`Send + Sync + 'static + ?Sized` on `dyn OcspFetcher`) so a regression that narrowed any of the three would fail at the first `tokio::spawn(async move { fetcher.fetch(cert).await })` site rather than at a surprised operator finding sessions hung on a non-`Send` fetcher; `on_cache_hit_and_miss_aliases_are_send_sync_clone` pins `Send + Sync + Clone` on both callback aliases via the same helper pattern; `fetch_future_alias_is_pin_box_dyn_send` pins the `FetchFuture<'_>` alias by writing `let _: FetchFuture<'_> = Box::pin(async { … })` (compiles only when the alias is `Pin<Box<dyn Future + Send>>`) and then asserts `Send` with a separate `is_send()` helper. Custom Debug contract: `debug_redacts_inner_and_fetcher_prints_cache_entry_count` constructs a verifier with the inline `FakeFetcher` and a real `WebPkiClientVerifier` inner, formats it via `format!("{:?}", …)`, and asserts the output (a) contains `ClientOcspVerifier` and `cache_entries:` tokens, (b) does NOT contain `FakeFetcher` or `WebPkiClientVerifier` tokens — pinning the redaction contract that exists specifically to prevent `tracing::warn!(?verifier, …)` log sites from splattering root-hint subject bytes or fetcher HTTP client state. Factory / accessor: `new_with_none_callbacks_is_usable` builds a verifier passing `None` for both `on_hit` and `on_miss`, issues a miss-path verify, and asserts the call completes without panicking — pinning the "None disables dispatch with zero runtime cost" contract the server binary relies on when operators do not wire OCSP observability; `cache_len_reflects_inserts_from_background_fetcher` seeds the `FakeFetcher` to return `Good` on the first call, verifies a cold-cache cert (fires a miss, schedules a fetch), yields the tokio runtime, and asserts `verifier.cache_len() == 1` — pinning the cache-insert side of the background-fetch contract. Channel-saturation defence: `schedule_fetch_drops_silently_when_queue_is_saturated` uses a `FakeFetcher` whose `fetch()` method returns `futures::future::pending::<Result<_, _>>()` (never completes), drives 300 miss-path verifies in quick succession, and asserts the verifier remains responsive (no lock contention, no panic) and `cache_len() == 0` — pinning the non-blocking saturation contract that exists specifically to prevent a slow OCSP responder from stalling the TLS accept loop. Verify paths: `verify_client_cert_accepts_when_cached_good` pre-loads the cache with a `Good` entry and asserts the verify call returns `Ok(ClientCertVerified::assertion())`; `verify_client_cert_rejects_when_cached_revoked` pre-loads `Revoked` and asserts the returned `Error` message contains the substring `"revoked"` (operators grep audit logs for the token, and a regression that changed the error to a generic `Other` variant would silently make revocation events unsearchable); `inner_verifier_rejection_propagates_without_cache_consult` uses a custom inline `AlwaysReject` impl of `ClientCertVerifier` (needed because `WebPkiClientVerifier::builder(Arc::new(empty_roots)).build()` panics with `NoRootAnchors` — the empty-root-set construction path is intentionally inaccessible from the public API, so the test defines a minimal inline impl that unconditionally returns `Error::General("inner-rejected")`) and asserts the verifier propagates the inner error without ever consulting the cache (pinning the evaluation order — inner chain-of-trust gates the cache lookup, so a regression that swapped the order would silently accept an untrusted cert whose serial happened to have a cached `Good` entry); `miss_then_hit_increments_callbacks` issues a cold-cache miss verify (asserts `on_miss` fires once, `on_hit` does not), background-inserts a `Good` entry, and issues a second verify with the same cert (asserts `on_hit` fires once, `on_miss` does not) — pinning the hit/miss exclusive dispatch the server metrics pipeline relies on; `on_miss_callback_fires_once_and_schedules_fetch_on_cold_cache` isolates the miss-side dispatch (no pre-population, asserts the `on_miss` counter ticks exactly once per cold verify) and asserts the background `FakeFetcher::calls` counter also ticks (pinning the "miss triggers fetch" contract); `on_hit_callback_fires_once_on_good_cache_hit` isolates the hit-side dispatch. Delegation: `root_hint_subjects_delegates_to_inner` compares `verifier.root_hint_subjects()` against `inner.root_hint_subjects()` by extracting `Vec<Vec<u8>>` via `.as_ref().to_vec()` on each `DistinguishedName` (the type itself does not implement `PartialEq`, so the raw-byte comparison is the canonical stable equality); `supported_verify_schemes_delegates_to_inner` mirrors the contract for the scheme-list accessor. Lifecycle: `spawned_task_exits_when_verifier_arc_is_dropped` (`#[tokio::test]`) wraps the verifier in `Arc`, spawns the background fetcher, downgrades a clone to `Weak`, drops the strong `Arc`, yields the tokio runtime, and asserts `Weak::upgrade()` returns `None` (pinning the "background task holds only a `Weak`, exits when the owner drops" contract that prevents a zombie fetcher task from outliving a SIGHUP listener replacement). Error-path + accessor hardening: `errored_fetch_leaves_cache_entry_unset` uses a dedicated `ErrFetcher` (always returns `Err`) and asserts both `cached_status(fp).is_none()` and `cache_len() == 0` after a scheduled fetch drains, pinning that a failed OCSP round-trip leaves no stale entry so the next handshake re-enters the miss arm rather than silently fail-open forever on a ghost `Ok(_)` insert; `schedule_fetch_with_empty_issuer_is_not_a_panic` pins the no-intermediate edge case (leaf directly signed by a trust anchor — `verify_client_cert` passes `Vec::new()` as the issuer), asserting the background fetcher still runs exactly once without panicking (the production `OcspStaplerFetcher` handles this via AIA lookup returning nothing, but the test-injected fetcher sees the same empty buffer); `cached_status_returns_none_for_unknown_key_shapes` drives four unusual lookup shapes (empty slice, all-zero 32-byte, all-`0xFF` 64-byte, short ASCII prefix) through `cached_status` and asserts all four miss, pinning that the underlying `HashMap<Vec<u8>, _>` never surfaces a false-positive match for arbitrary input that was never inserted. Suite grows from 3 to 27 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors, methods, trait impls, free functions, and alias types through their documented contracts. `cargo test -p rustgate-transport --features ocsp --lib client_ocsp` reports 27/27 passing; full-workspace clippy with the canonical feature-set from CLAUDE.md is clean. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-server::otel_metrics` Prometheus→OTLP bridge (cache TTL pin, fresh-state invariant, stale-refresh re-gather, label-pair translation contract, invalidate/shutdown idempotence, unknown-family guard)** — `crates/rustgate-server/src/otel_metrics.rs` is the single adapter that exposes every Prometheus counter/gauge/histogram/summary family as OTLP metrics via a set of observable instruments, and its private `FamilyCache` is the coalescer that keeps one OTEL collection cycle to a single `prometheus::default_registry().gather()` call (without it, `F` families produce `O(F²)` gather work per cycle). Before this PR the inline suite held only three tests — `init_succeeds_with_valid_endpoint`, `shutdown_without_init_is_safe`, and the within-TTL `family_cache_refreshes_on_first_use_and_reuses_within_ttl` — leaving eight contract-shaped invariants unpinned. Eleven new inline tests close the gap: `family_cache_ttl_constant_is_below_otlp_push_interval` pins `CACHE_TTL = 500ms` and asserts it stays strictly below the 1 s minimum OTLP push interval called out in the module header, because a refactor that silently raised the TTL to ≥1 s would let consecutive collection cycles observe stale snapshots (the exact pathology the cache was added to prevent); `family_cache_new_starts_with_empty_map_and_no_refresh_timestamp` pins the two-part initial-state invariant (`refreshed_at.is_none()` **and** `families.is_empty()`) since either leaking through would either return zeroed values on the first callback (stale `refreshed_at`) or skip the initial gather (non-empty map against a populated timestamp); `family_cache_refresh_if_stale_advances_timestamp_after_backdating` simulates a stale cache by rewinding `refreshed_at` 10× the TTL via `Instant::checked_sub` (deterministic, no sleep so CI workers cannot flake) and pins that the next `refresh_if_stale` call advances the timestamp past the rewound value — this is the load-bearing branch that keeps OTLP pushes from freezing on the first snapshot; `label_pairs_to_kv_empty_slice_returns_empty_vec` is the zero-label safety pin (a regression that unconditionally read `labels[0]` would panic on the first bare counter Prometheus registered); `label_pairs_to_kv_maps_single_pair_to_matching_keyvalue` pins the one-to-one `LabelPair → KeyValue` mapping name-and-value verbatim, since a silent swap or drop would corrupt every OTLP dashboard that filters by `peer_id`/`tenant`; `label_pairs_to_kv_preserves_input_order_for_multiple_pairs` pins the positional ordering contract (OTLP consumers frequently key grouping off the first label, so a future `rayon` or `HashMap` rewrite that reordered attrs would silently misroute metrics downstream); `label_pairs_to_kv_forwards_empty_value_strings_as_attrs` pins that empty-string values round-trip as empty-string `KeyValue` attrs rather than being silently dropped, because skipping them would desynchronize cardinality between the Prometheus and OTLP views of the same family; `label_pairs_to_kv_preserves_multibyte_utf8_name_and_value` defends against a byte-level slice-and-splice refactor by round-tripping `tenant_名` / `αβγ-42` through the translator; `invalidate_cache_is_idempotent_and_safe_without_prior_refresh` pins that `invalidate_cache` can be called before any `with_cached_family` has primed the global (the `shutdown()` codepath calls it before `force_flush`, so a server that never processed a metric cycle must still shut down cleanly); `with_cached_family_on_unknown_name_does_not_invoke_callback` pins the load-bearing guard that the observable-instrument callback fires only when the family is present in the registry (using a sentinel name — `__rustgate_otel_metrics_sentinel_never_registered_family__` — that cannot collide with any real family), because a regression that fell through to a `default()` `MetricFamily` would silently emit zero-valued metrics for every unregistered callback; and `shutdown_is_idempotent_when_called_repeatedly_without_init` pins triple-calling `shutdown()` on an uninitialized provider as a no-op, keeping integration-test harnesses and signal-driven teardown paths robust against double-shutdown. Suite grows from 3 to 14 tests; no behavioural change to the module under test. `cargo nextest run -p rustgate-server --features otel,metrics otel_metrics::` reports 14/14 passing. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-proto::convert::proto_to_xml` (five-char entity escaper, attribute-injection defense, `ProtoError` variant pinning, hostile-UID escaping, zero-timestamp and empty-detail omit-paths, declared emit-order contracts)** — `crates/rustgate-proto/src/convert/proto_to_xml.rs` is the TAK protobuf → CoT XML converter that every protobuf-framed client flows through on the ingest path, but the inline suite had only 4 tests, all focused on `is_safe_xml_fragment`; the actual XML emitters (`build_xml`, `write_attr`, `write_contact`, `write_status`, `write_takv`, `write_precision_location`, `write_group`, `write_track`) and the byte-level `xml_escape_into` helper were untested, and the `ProtoError` return paths were only exercised end-to-end by downstream integration tests. Fourteen new tests close the gap directly inside the crate, without standing up a transport fixture. `xml_escape_into_escapes_all_five_xml_special_chars_into_entities` pins that exactly the XML 1.0 quintet (`<`, `>`, `&`, `'`, `"`) is rewritten to its named entity form and no other byte is touched — accidentally dropping any of the five would allow a hostile remarks-field payload to break out of its surrounding element; `xml_escape_into_passes_through_ascii_alphanumeric_and_punctuation_unchanged` is the inverse negative assertion, proving the escaper does not over-escape printable ASCII into `&#NN;` numeric entities (which would bloat every tactical payload and break brittle downstream parsers that key on exact byte layouts); `xml_escape_into_preserves_multibyte_utf8_bytes_verbatim` pins that multi-byte UTF-8 sequences pass through byte-for-byte, so a UTF-8 callsign that happens to contain `0xE2` never gets mis-escaped by a future naive `char`-based rewrite. `write_attr_emits_space_name_equals_escaped_quoted_value` pins the attribute emit shape `' name="value"'` (leading space + double-quote delimiter), `write_attr_escapes_double_quote_in_value_to_prevent_attribute_breakout` is the injection-defense assertion that a literal `"` inside an attribute value is rewritten to `&quot;` before being written between the two `"` delimiters (this is the load-bearing escape that keeps hostile CoT attribute content from closing its own quote and injecting a new attribute), and `write_attr_on_empty_value_emits_empty_quoted_string` pins the empty-value shape `name=""` so a stray `if value.is_empty() { return }` refactor cannot silently drop attributes. `proto_to_xml_on_malformed_protobuf_bytes_returns_decode_variant` and `proto_to_xml_on_missing_cot_event_returns_specific_missing_field_variant` are the error-path pins — the first feeds uninterpretable bytes and asserts the error is `ProtoError::Decode(_)` specifically (not the generic `Err`-is-some check the integration tests do), the second feeds a valid `TakMessage` envelope that is missing the `cot_event` field and asserts the error is `ProtoError::MissingField("cot_event")` so a future refactor that renames the field or downgrades it to a silent default surfaces here rather than in log-greps across downstream crates. `build_xml_escapes_hostile_uid_to_prevent_xml_injection` is the top-level injection test — it constructs a full `CotEvent` with a UID containing `<script>alert(1)</script>` and asserts the rendered XML contains the entity-escaped form (`&lt;script&gt;`) and never the raw angle brackets, exercising the full `build_xml → write_attr → xml_escape_into` chain; `build_xml_skips_time_attrs_when_all_timestamps_are_zero` pins the wire-format contract that all-zero send/start/stale timestamps produce XML without `time`/`start`/`stale` attributes (downstream consumers treat missing-timestamp and zero-timestamp very differently, and the negative branch was previously unexercised); `build_xml_omits_detail_wrapper_when_fully_empty` pins that an empty `Detail` sub-message yields XML without a `<detail/>` element at all rather than an empty tag, matching the TAK reference-implementation's minimal emit shape. `write_status_emits_battery_attribute_even_when_zero` is a deliberate contrast with the timestamp test — `<status battery="0"/>` is valid and meaningful (fully depleted device), so this test pins that the emitter does not accidentally treat zero as "missing" and drop the attribute; `write_contact_omits_empty_callsign_and_endpoint` pins the complementary rule for `<contact>` where empty-string fields must not be emitted as `callsign=""`; `write_takv_emits_device_platform_os_version_in_declared_order` pins the emit order of the `<takv>` element's four attributes so downstream parsers that perform position-sensitive matching (legacy C++ TAK clients do) cannot break on a future alphabetical-sort cleanup of the writer. Suite grows from 4 to 18 tests; no behavioural change to the module under test. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-federation::discovery` trait default, object-safety, idempotent and full-replace reconcile cycles, transient-failure recovery, first-tick skip, and spurious-shutdown tolerance** — `crates/rustgate-federation/src/discovery.rs` hosts the `PeerDiscovery` trait and the `reconciler()` background task that every dynamic-federation deployment relies on (DNS-SD, Consul, etcd backends plug in through this trait). The inline suite had 4 tests: add path, remove path, error swallow, and shutdown exit — leaving the trait's default `backend_name()` body, its object-safety contract, same-peer-set idempotence, simultaneous add+remove in one cycle, error-to-success recovery, the deliberate first-tick skip, and spurious-false shutdown tolerance all unpinned. Seven new tests close these gaps. `peer_discovery_trait_default_backend_name_is_peer_discovery` introduces a minimal `DefaultBackendDiscovery` that deliberately does NOT override `backend_name()` and asserts the default body returns the literal `"peer-discovery"`; every in-tree backend overrides this so the default string was otherwise untested, but any external implementor falling back on it feeds that exact label into the audit-log `backend=<name>` field, and a rename or body change would silently reshape downstream log filtering. `peer_discovery_is_object_safe_and_boxable_behind_arc_dyn_send_sync` is the compile-time tripwire: `reconciler()` takes `Arc<dyn PeerDiscovery>` so a future refactor adding a generic-return async method or a `Self`-returning method would break `dyn` at every call site across the crate; this test uses a `fn _assert_object_send_sync<T: ?Sized + Send + Sync>()` witness to fail object-safety refactors inside this module rather than in `manager.rs` or `setup.rs` where the root cause would be obscured. `reconcile_is_idempotent_when_backend_returns_identical_peer_set_twice` pins that two back-to-back `run_once` calls with the same peer set leave the manager's peer list byte-identical — `swap_peers` must classify the second cycle as `unchanged`, NOT as a full remove+add churn that would tear down and re-establish every active connection; a regression here would silently flap the entire federation mesh on every tick and dump reconnection storms into production logs. `reconcile_handles_full_replacement_with_both_add_and_remove_in_one_cycle` is the symmetric complement to the existing `reconcile_removes_vanished_peers` test — that test only exercises shrinkage, this one pins that a single cycle going from `{alpha}` to `{beta}` (entirely different set) processes BOTH the remove and the add in the same tick and leaves exactly `{beta}` (not the union and not the stale `{alpha}`). `reconcile_recovers_after_transient_discovery_failure` pins the load-bearing defensive contract that a `discover()` returning `Err` does not poison the reconciler — the NEXT cycle's success populates peers normally, so a flaky Consul round-trip, an etcd leader election hiccup, or a momentary DNS-SD unavailability cannot permanently disable the federation mesh (without this test the "errors logged and swallowed" contract is only exercised in isolation, never in the error-then-recover sequence that production actually exhibits). `reconciler_loop_skips_first_immediate_tick_before_first_discover_call` pins the explicit `let _ = ticker.tick().await;` above the main loop — `tokio::time::interval` resolves its first `.tick()` immediately, and the reconciler deliberately consumes that initial tick so `FederationManager::start()` has one full interval to finish its own init before `swap_peers` churn begins; the test spawns the task with a 500 ms interval, waits 180 ms, and asserts `call_count == 0`, so removing the skip-first line fails here. `reconciler_loop_does_not_exit_when_shutdown_channel_re_sends_false` pins the shutdown arm's inner `if *shutdown.borrow()` guard: a `send(false)` on the watch channel wakes `shutdown.changed()` (because `changed()` fires on any value-received, not only on transitions), but the inner boolean guard must keep the loop alive — this protects against shared-shutdown-channel orchestrator designs that cycle a single watch through multiple `false` values before the eventual `true`. Suite grows from 4 to 11 tests; no behavioural change to the module under test. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport::spiffe` `SpiffeConfig` default refresh cadence + derives, `SpiffeHooks` Debug redaction + Arc-preserving Clone, `append_der_as_pem` RFC 7468 envelope + 64-column base64 wrapping, and `svid_expiry_unix` real-cert happy path** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/spiffe.rs` hosts the SPIFFE/SPIRE workload-identity bootstrap that the `spiffe` feature plugs in front of the classical `--cert/--key/--ca` path: `fetch_initial_identity` dials the SPIRE agent Workload API, seeds a `CertReloader` with the initial X.509-SVID, builds the trust-bundle PEM, and wires `SpiffeHooks` into `spawn_refresh_task` so the background refresh loop can update Prometheus counters on `success`/`failure` while emitting structured audit events regardless of whether metrics are compiled in. Before this PR the inline suite only pinned four behaviours — `default_config_has_standard_socket` asserting the default `workload_api_socket`, the two `svid_expiry_unix` negative paths (empty chain, garbage bytes), and a `CertReloader::update_from_der` round-trip — leaving seven contract-shaped invariants unpinned, each of which could silently regress without tripping a live-SPIRE integration test. Seven new inline tests close the gap: `default_config_refresh_interval_is_exactly_five_minutes` pins `SpiffeConfig::default().refresh_interval == Duration::from_secs(300)` — the `--spiffe-refresh-interval` CLI flag documents a 5-minute default and that value flows through to the background refresh cadence, so a drift to 60s would quintuple the Workload API load per deployed server and a drift to 3600s would stretch the gap between SVID rotation and actual reload past most SPIRE agents' default 1-hour SVID TTL; `spiffe_config_supports_clone_and_debug_derives` pins the `#[derive(Clone, Debug)]` contract on the config struct via `fn assert_clone_debug<T: Clone + std::fmt::Debug>()` so a future refactor that dropped either derive (the setup path hands a cloned config into the refresh task and tracing emits the struct with `?cfg`) fails at test-compile time rather than at a downstream call site; `spiffe_hooks_debug_elides_both_callback_fields` exercises the manual `Debug` impl on `SpiffeHooks` — the struct holds two `Arc<dyn Fn>` callbacks (`on_success: Arc<dyn Fn(i64) + Send + Sync>` and `on_failure: Arc<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>`) that derive-Debug cannot render, so the module instead ships `f.debug_struct("SpiffeHooks").finish()` which renders `SpiffeHooks` with no field body and the test asserts both the presence of the `SpiffeHooks` prefix and the absence of `on_success` / `on_failure` tokens so an accidental swap to `finish_non_exhaustive()` (which would add a `..` marker and is semantically different) or to a manual `.field("on_success", &…)` would be caught — callback pointers leaking into `tracing::debug!(?hooks, …)` lines would pollute logs with vtable/fn addresses; `spiffe_hooks_is_clone_preserving_arc_callback_references` is the load-bearing pin that `SpiffeHooks`'s `#[derive(Clone)]` behaves as Arc-shallow-clone — the refresh task receives an owned `SpiffeHooks` by value and the caller (setup.rs) holds its own clone simultaneously, and the workspace relies on "both handles observe the same callback state" so a misguided future refactor that replaced the derive with `impl Clone for SpiffeHooks { fn clone(&self) -> Self { Self { on_success: Arc::new(|_| {}), on_failure: Arc::new(|| {}) } } }` would silently drop every metric update without tripping any other test; the test proves Arc-shallow-clone by constructing hooks whose success callback increments an `Arc<AtomicUsize>` shared via move-capture, cloning the hooks, invoking the success callback twice (once through each handle) + failure once, and asserting `success_count == 2 && failure_count == 1` — any clone that decoupled the Arcs would see the second handle's callback target a different atomic and both counters would fall to 1/0; `append_der_as_pem_emits_canonical_begin_end_markers_with_label` pins the RFC 7468 envelope (`-----BEGIN <label>-----` / `-----END <label>-----` with trailing newline) that `fetch_initial_identity` relies on so the PEM-encoded cert chain and private key it emits plug into the existing `TlsConfig::from_pem` path (which walks `rustls_pemfile::read_all`-compatible blocks); a regression that dropped the dashes, mangled the label, or omitted the trailing newline would still produce a plausibly-shaped string that failed rustls parse at startup with an opaque error rather than here with a specific diff; `append_der_as_pem_wraps_base64_body_at_64_columns_per_line` exercises the inner base64-wrapping loop (`out.push_str(&encoded[idx..end])` where `end = (idx + 64).min(encoded.len())`) by passing a 200-byte DER blob, stripping `-----BEGIN/END-----` framing, and asserting every non-terminal body line is exactly 64 columns while the last line is ≤ 64 columns — RFC 7468 allows any line length up to 80 but 64 is the de-facto OpenSSL convention that every downstream PEM parser in the workspace treats as canonical, and a subtle regression that wrapped at 65 or 76 would still parse everywhere but visually diverge from every other cert file operators compare against; `svid_expiry_unix_extracts_real_notafter_from_testca_cert_as_positive_seconds` is the happy-path complement to the existing `svid_expiry_returns_zero_for_empty_chain` and `svid_expiry_returns_zero_for_garbage` tests — it drives a real `TestCa::issue_client_cert` output through `svid_expiry_unix`, asserts the returned unix seconds is strictly `> now`, and deliberately does **not** assert an upper bound because `rcgen::CertificateParams::default()` places `notAfter` far in the future (year ~4095) for tests that must not expire regardless of CI runtime, so the "unreasonably far future" check would false-fail on clean code and the test accepts anything positive as correct; without this test a refactor that swapped `x509_parser::parse_x509_certificate` for a narrower parser that mishandled a specific DER tag, or flipped `.validity().not_after.timestamp()` for `.not_before.timestamp()`, would return `0` on real certs while both existing negative tests would still pass (they already expect `0`) and the `spiffe_svid_refresh_success` audit event + `rustgate_spiffe_svid_expiry_seconds` Prometheus gauge would silently emit zero on every rotation. Total suite grows from 4 to 11 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives the module's existing public/crate-private surface through its existing constructors and helpers. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-enrollment::server` `EnrollmentServer` builder, Debug redaction, module constants, F-40 TLS preflight, and rate-limit callback wiring** — `crates/rustgate-enrollment/src/server.rs` hosts the HTTPS enrollment server that every rustgate deployment exposes for Marti-compatible client provisioning (`/Marti/api/tls/*`, `/Marti/api/contacts/*`, `/Marti/sync/*`, `/api/revoke`, `/api/crl`, and the feature-gated EST `/.well-known/est/*` endpoints). Before this PR the file had **zero** inline unit tests — coverage relied entirely on the two HTTP-level integration suites (`tests/server_integration.rs`, `tests/est_integration.rs`) which exercise the end-to-end request→response path but leave the structural invariants of the builder, the security-critical F-40 preflight, and the Prometheus-callback wiring unpinned. Nine new inline tests close those gaps: `new_default_config_has_no_optional_providers_and_uses_documented_rate_limits` pins the fresh-struct shape from `EnrollmentServer::new` — `contacts_provider`, `packages`, `revocation`, `on_rate_limit`, `on_rate_limit_poison` all `None` and the sign_limiter matching `DEFAULT_SIGN_RATE_LIMIT` (10) + `DEFAULT_SIGN_RATE_WINDOW` (60 s) — so a refactor that silently populated any optional provider with a stub (e.g. an in-memory `DataPackageStore` that would expose `/Marti/sync/*` on every server) or reordered the limiter defaults would fail here rather than at a deployment audit; `debug_impl_elides_sensitive_fields_and_reports_optional_provider_bools` pins the manual `Debug` impl — the struct holds `Arc<CsrSigner>` (transitive CA private key material) and `Arc<Credentials>` (HTTP Basic Auth password in `Zeroizing<String>` whose Debug delegates to the inner String in zeroize 1.8+), and the `.field("contacts_provider", &self.contacts_provider.is_some())` pattern exists precisely so operators can see "which features are wired" via `tracing::debug!(?server, …)` without leaking redb handles/connection pools — the test renders the struct with a sentinel password `UNIQUE-SENTINEL-PASSWORD-42`, asserts the `EnrollmentServer {` prefix plus the three `false` bool fields, and asserts the sentinel is **absent** from the rendered output so an accidental swap to derived-Debug would fail loudly (Credentials' own Debug already redacts the password, but the test pins that assumption at the server layer); `with_rate_limit_overrides_default_max_requests_and_window` drives the builder's `--enrollment-rate-limit`-backing entry point and asserts the sign_limiter's `max_requests()` and `window()` reflect the caller's arguments — a regression that swapped the positional args (plausible since both are numbers) would silently mis-configure every operator's strict override; `with_rate_limit_poison_callback_preserves_existing_rate_limit_regardless_of_call_order` is the load-bearing pin for the doc-comment's "Safe to call in any order" contract — the test exercises both orderings (poison callback first → defaults preserved; `with_rate_limit(3, 15 s)` then poison callback → custom 3/15 s preserved), so a future refactor that naively rebuilt the limiter with `RateLimiter::new(DEFAULT_SIGN_RATE_LIMIT, DEFAULT_SIGN_RATE_WINDOW).with_poison_callback(cb)` (clobbering the operator's custom rate limit whenever the metric wiring is enabled) fails here; `module_constants_pin_documented_defaults_and_limits` asserts all four module constants — `DEFAULT_SIGN_RATE_LIMIT == 10`, `DEFAULT_SIGN_RATE_WINDOW == 60 s`, `MAX_ENROLLMENT_CONNECTIONS == 64`, `ENROLLMENT_HTTP_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT == 30 s` — and crucially the cross-invariant `ENROLLMENT_HTTP_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT > rustgate_core::constants::HTTP_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT` (enrollment is deliberately 2× the core default because CSR signing + PKCS#12 generation takes seconds on slow CI disks, and a drift would flake integration tests and reject real slow-CA deployments); `run_rejects_both_empty_cert_and_key_pem_with_f40_security_preflight` pins the F-40 / issue #767 precondition check that refuses to start with empty `cert_pem` + `key_pem` and surfaces a Config error whose message contains both "requires TLS" and "PKCS#12" — the rationale is load-bearing for the on-call (PKCS#12 bundles protect their cert bags with a publicly-known standardized password `"atakatak"`, so the TLS channel is the real confidentiality boundary and any deployment path that reached plaintext would silently leak full client cert chains to every passive observer) and a regression that collapsed the message into a generic "config error" would leave the operator chasing a rustls parser bug instead; `run_rejects_whitespace_only_cert_pem_with_f40_security_preflight` exercises the `.trim().is_empty()` branch the preflight uses to defend against the common "empty env var via shell heredoc" mistake — a regression that dropped the `trim()` call would silently accept `"  \n\t  "` and let it fall through to `rustgate_core::pem::parse_certs` whose error message does not communicate the F-40 security rationale; `run_rejects_whitespace_only_key_pem_with_f40_security_preflight` is the symmetric key-side test — both sides must trip the F-40 check independently because an operator could accidentally fat-finger either input; `with_rate_limit_callback_wires_none_to_some_transition_for_prometheus_fan_out` pins that `with_rate_limit_callback(cb)` stores the caller's `Arc` unmodified (not a no-op wrapper) — the test wires a sentinel `AtomicUsize` counter, asserts `Arc::strong_count` grew by exactly 1 (proving the server stores the caller's handle rather than repackaging), invokes the stored callback twice through `server.on_rate_limit`, and asserts the counter reached 2 — a regression that wrapped the caller's closure in `Arc::new(move || { … }.clone())` would still pass naive "is the field Some?" tests but would break the server binary's `rustgate_enrollment_rate_limit_total` Prometheus counter and deliver zero rate-limit observability. Total suite grows from 0 to 9 tests; zero changes to any production API — every test drives the module's existing public surface through its existing constructors and builder methods. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-server::federation_reloader` SHA-256 length + empty-vector pin, missing-file and parse-error context-message invariants, post-swap hash-advance, and hash-state integrity under error** — `crates/rustgate-server/src/federation_reloader.rs` is the federation-peers JSON hot-reloader: it hashes the on-disk file, compares to `last_hash`, parses, swaps the `FederationManager`'s peer set on change, and only then commits the new hash. The reconcile runs under a `tokio::sync::Mutex` to serialize concurrent SIGHUP+poller triggers. Before this PR the inline suite (6 tests: two `compute_hash` determinism checks plus four async add/remove/unchanged/invalid-JSON reload tests) covered the happy-path reconciliation but left six defensive invariants unpinned. Six new tests close those gaps: `compute_hash_output_length_is_sha256_32_bytes` is the type-system belt-and-suspenders pin against a silent SHA-256 → other-algorithm swap (`[u8; 32]` return is the pin, but a refactor that changed the signature to `Vec<u8>` would type-check while silently changing the digest size operators grep for in `tracing::debug!` lines); `compute_hash_empty_input_matches_rfc_sha256_empty_vector` asserts the hash of the empty slice against the RFC 6234 / FIPS 180-4 reference vector (`e3b0c442…7852b855`) so any drift — re-keying via `new_with_prefix` for "domain separation", truncation to SHA-256/224, algorithm swap to BLAKE3 — trips here rather than silently breaking every operator's `last_hash` after a rolling deploy; `reload_with_missing_peers_file_returns_error_including_path` drives the `tokio::fs::read_to_string` error arm and asserts the Err message contains both the `"federation peers file"` category token (for audit-log grep) and the actual path (for multi-deployment disambiguation) — under Kubernetes ConfigMap remount windows the poller sees the file briefly missing, and a silent-success-with-empty-set regression would disconnect every live federation peer mid-operation; `reload_err_on_invalid_json_preserves_last_hash_so_recovery_is_deterministic` is the state-integrity pin — the design intent is that only a fully-successful swap commits the new hash, so after a bad-JSON reload the `last_hash` must still equal the pre-bad-reload value and an immediate rollback to the known-good content must look like `Ok(false)` (unchanged); a refactor that hoisted the post-swap `*guard = new_hash` assignment above the parse step would pass the existing `reload_rejects_invalid_json` (which only checks `is_err()`) but fail this test's rollback assertion; `reload_after_successful_swap_advances_last_hash_so_second_reload_is_unchanged` is the symmetric positive-path pin — after a successful reload on a changed file, an immediate second reload on the same file returns `Ok(false)` because `last_hash` was advanced; a regression that dropped the post-swap hash update would silently hammer `FederationManager::swap_peers` on every poller tick; `reload_parse_error_message_includes_path_for_operator_grep` pins the parse-error context separately from the I/O-error path (the two error arms share a template but not a code path), asserting the message contains `"parse federation peers file"` plus the file path so the `config.reload result=failure` audit line stays correlatable to which ConfigMap faulted. Suite grows from 6 to 12 tests; zero changes to production code — every test drives the module's crate-private `pub(crate)` surface through `FederationPeerReloader::{new, compute_hash, reload}`. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-enrollment::handlers::sign::handle_tls_config` HTTP response contract (status, content-type, body delegation, XML prolog, XML-escaping regression pin)** — `crates/rustgate-enrollment/src/handlers/sign.rs` previously had zero inline tests despite hosting the `/Marti/api/tls/config` handler that TAK clients hit as an unauthenticated preflight before computing a CSR. The two CSR-signing handlers (`handle_sign_client_v1`, `handle_sign_client_v2`) take `Request<hyper::body::Incoming>` and are therefore not unit-testable (same limitation PR #1048 documented for `handle_revoke`), but `handle_tls_config(signer: &CsrSigner) -> Response<Full<Bytes>>` takes no `Request` and is trivially exercisable once a `CsrSigner` is built from a `TestCa`. Five new inline tests close the handler-side contract: `handle_tls_config_returns_200_ok` pins `StatusCode::OK` — a drift to `204 No Content` or `500` would silently fail TAK client enrollment with no auth challenge to guide the operator, and separating the status check from the body check lets a regression on one surface fail loudly without masking the other; `handle_tls_config_sets_content_type_application_xml` pins the exact literal `application/xml` Content-Type, ruling out both the RFC 3023-legacy `text/xml` synonym (which some TAK client builds silently misroute) and a `; charset=UTF-8` suffix that has historically broken specific client parser dispatch; `handle_tls_config_body_contains_signer_organization_name` builds the signer with organization `"Alpha-Company"`, collects the response body, and asserts the string is present — the only reason the handler takes a `&CsrSigner` rather than a plain config struct is to pull the org at response time, so a refactor that hardcoded the organization or read it from a separate source would silently decouple the config knob from the response and this test catches that; `handle_tls_config_body_starts_with_xml_declaration` anchors the body to the `<?xml` prolog so any format drift (e.g. accidentally returning raw JSON, a truncated fragment, or a bare XML element with no prolog) fails loudly even if the Content-Type header is still `application/xml`; `handle_tls_config_escapes_xml_special_characters_in_organization` is the security-relevant delegation pin — it constructs a signer with org `"A&B<C"` and asserts both that the raw unescaped form is absent from the response and that the canonical XML-escaped form `A&amp;B&lt;C` is present, so a future refactor that inlined the XML with `format!` and skipped the `xml_escape` call in `response::tls_config_xml` would be caught here (operators may configure orgs containing `&`, and in the worst case an org containing `"` could close the `value="…"` attribute and inject arbitrary `<nameEntry>` children the server never intended). The test module uses a shared-CA `OnceLock<(String, String)>` to amortise keypair generation across the suite (keeping the whole file under ~100 ms) and the existing `collect_body` pattern from `handlers/api.rs` for response-body inspection. Suite grows from 0 to 5 tests; zero changes to any production API — every test drives the existing public surface. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-resilience::token_bucket::TokenBucket` defensive-input contract, Debug shape, and lazy-refill saturation invariants** — `crates/rustgate-resilience/src/token_bucket.rs` is the per-target outbound rate limiter every HTTP, federation, and (indirectly) NATS publish path reaches through, so the invariants that govern its lazy-refill arithmetic are load-bearing — a silent regression anywhere in the constructor's clamp/floor arithmetic or the `try_acquire` refill formula would shift production rate-limit behaviour without changing observable API shape. Before this PR the inline suite (6 tests) covered the sunny-path burst/refill/saturation cases (`initial_burst_allowed`, `custom_burst_size`, `refills_over_time`, `does_not_exceed_max_tokens`, `zero_rate_blocks_after_initial_burst`, `minimum_burst_of_one`) but left six defensive contracts unpinned, each of which could silently regress without tripping any integration test. Six new inline tests close those gaps: `token_bucket_debug_is_non_empty_for_both_formatters` pins the `#[derive(Debug)]` impl that the workspace-wide `missing_debug_implementations = "warn"` lint requires, exercising both `{:?}` and `{:#?}` formatters and asserting the pretty-print variant spans multiple lines (catching a hypothetical manual `impl Debug` that delegated to `write!` with identical output in both modes);
