`negative_rate_per_sec_clamps_to_zero_refill` pins the `rate_per_sec.max(0.0)` clamp in the constructor — the regression mode is subtle because burst is separately clamped to `>= 1` so the initial tokens still flow, but after burst exhaustion every subsequent call must be rejected (if the clamp dropped, a negative refill rate would drive `tokens` below zero on every call and a `tokens >= 1.0` compare-against-negative path would produce undefined behaviour-adjacent scheduling); `burst_none_defaults_to_rate_per_sec_capacity` pins the constructor's `burst.unwrap_or(rate_per_sec)` default — the doc-comment on `new()` promises "a 1-second burst", and a regression to `2 * rate` or `rate / 2` would silently shift every caller's burst allowance, so the test drains exactly `rate_per_sec` tokens from a bucket built with `burst = None` and asserts request `rate_per_sec + 1` is rejected before any refill interval elapses; `burst_zero_is_floored_to_one` complements the existing `minimum_burst_of_one` (which uses `Some(0.5)`) by pinning the exact `Some(0.0)` edge — the `.max(1.0)` floor is what guarantees every bucket allows at least one request at startup, and a regression would create a bucket that rejects every request and trigger a thundering-herd of rejections at deploy time; `refill_saturates_at_max_tokens_after_long_idle` uses `tokio::test(start_paused = true)` + `tokio::time::advance(10s)` to drive the `.min(self.max_tokens)` saturation cap deterministically — after 10 seconds of simulated idle at 10 tokens/sec the raw `elapsed * refill_rate` term would be 100, but the cap must trim it to `max_tokens = 3`, so exactly 3 subsequent requests succeed and the 4th fails (a dropped `.min()` cap would absorb up to 100 requests in a burst and defeat the entire point of capacity control); `rejected_try_acquire_still_advances_refill_clock` pins the load-bearing invariant that `last_refill` advances on every call regardless of whether a token was granted — if the timestamp only advanced on successful acquisitions, a stream of rejected calls would let `elapsed` grow unbounded and the next successful call would refill by a multi-second gap rather than by the true sub-millisecond delta, so the test drains the bucket, fires several rejected calls under paused time, advances exactly 100ms (= 1.0 tokens at 10/sec), and asserts exactly one request succeeds (proving rejections did not "bank" time). Total suite grows from 6 to 12 tests; zero changes to any production API — every test drives the module's public surface through its existing `TokenBucket::new` + `try_acquire` construction. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-enrollment::handlers::util` HTTP-response builder and query-parameter parser defensive branches** — `crates/rustgate-enrollment/src/handlers/util.rs` hosts three helpers that every enrollment HTTP handler reaches for: `build_response(status, content_type, body)` (used by `handle_tls_config`, both `handle_sign_client_v*`, `handle_revoke`, CRL endpoints, and the EST stack), `extract_param(query, key)` (used by every endpoint that reads `clientUID` / `version` / `callsign` out of the query string), and `percent_decode(s)` (called by `extract_param` on every matched value). Before this PR the inline suite covered six sunny-path contracts (three `percent_decode` cases, one `extract_param` happy case, and the missing-key `None` case) but left nine contract-shaped invariants unpinned, several of which carry explicit security weight. Ten new tests close the gap: `build_response_sets_status_content_type_and_body_exactly` (a `#[tokio::test]` because `http_body_util::BodyExt::collect().await.to_bytes()` is the only way to round-trip a `Full<Bytes>` body through the public API) pins the three-axis round-trip — status → verbatim, Content-Type header → verbatim, body bytes → verbatim — so a silent regression that remapped the status, wrapped the body in additional framing, or defaulted the Content-Type would fail at a single focused assertion rather than surfacing as 12 different handler breakages; `build_response_with_empty_content_type_omits_header` pins the `content_type.is_empty()` early-skip branch — the helper deliberately omits the header when the caller passes `""` so PKCS#12 / binary content-negotiated responses (where the caller wants hyper's default or sets the header elsewhere) do not get `Content-Type: ` slammed on them, and a regression that dropped the guard would fail downstream strict-header parsers; `percent_decode_hex_nibbles_are_case_insensitive` pins that `%2F`, `%2f`, `%aB`, `%Ab` all decode identically per RFC 3986 §2.1 (real-world CoT clients — ATAK, iTAK, Wintak — emit a mix, so a narrowing to uppercase-only would silently corrupt every `clientUID=…` query from the mis-cased half of the fleet), and *also* pins the `byte as char` Latin-1 quirk by asserting `%aB` decodes to `[0xC2, 0xAB]` — the UTF-8 encoding of U+00AB — so a future refactor that flipped the push to `push_str(std::str::from_utf8(&[decoded])?)` (failing on high bytes) or silently lossified to `?` would both trip here; `percent_decode_invalid_hex_produces_null_byte` pins the `_ => 0` fallthrough in `hex_nibble` — `%ZZ` decodes to `0x00` (both nibbles fall through to 0), `%4Z` decodes to `0x40` (high nibble preserved, low falls through) — so a regression that changed the fallback to a larger sentinel value would start distorting `clientUID`s on malformed query strings rather than producing the current deterministic null-byte result; `percent_decode_null_byte_passes_through` pins `%00 → 0x00` (NUL byte, `len() == 1`) so a future "sanitize during decode" refactor that silently stripped NULs goes through deliberate review rather than silently changing the semantics; `percent_decode_truncated_escape_uses_zero_fallback` is the load-bearing no-panic test — `bytes.next().unwrap_or(b'0')` supplies `'0'` for each missing nibble so bare `%` decodes as `0x00` and `%A` decodes as `0xA0` (UTF-8-encoded as `[0xC2, 0xA0]`), and a regression that replaced `.unwrap_or(b'0')` with `.unwrap()` would panic on adversarial `clientUID=foo%` queries and crash the enrollment HTTP worker; `extract_param_skips_malformed_pair_without_equals` is the **security-critical test** — a bare `"clientUID"` token with no `=` and no value does NOT match a `key = "clientUID"` lookup and silently return `Some("")`, because that would let an unauthenticated client lie about their UID without actually transmitting it; the test also pins that one malformed pair does not poison the iterator (`"clientUID&name=alice"` still finds `name`); `extract_param_returns_first_match_on_duplicate_keys` pins that `find_map` returns on the first `Some(_)` (`"a=1&a=2"` yields `Some("1")`) — the HTTP spec is silent on duplicate-key semantics, but pinning the current first-wins contract keeps two identical `clientUID` fields from a buggy client resolving consistently across refactors; `extract_param_requires_exact_key_match_not_prefix_or_substring` is the other security-critical test — `k == key` is strict equality, so `"client"` must NOT match `"clientUID=xyz"`, `"clientUID"` must NOT match `"clientUIDExtended=evil"`, and `"UID"` must NOT match `"clientUID=xyz"`; a regression to `k.starts_with(key)` or `k.contains(key)` would turn every query parameter into an attacker-controlled prefix and let a spoofed `clientUIDFake=…` satisfy a `clientUID` probe; `extract_param_handles_empty_query_and_empty_key` pins the two empty-input edges — `""` yields a single empty pair that fails `split_once('=')` and is skipped (`None`), and `"=orphan"` with an empty-string lookup matches and returns `Some("orphan")` (a surprising edge that stays stable across refactors only if it is pinned). Total suite grows from 6 to 16 tests; zero changes to any production API — every test drives the module's existing crate-visible surface. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport::plaintext` listener/session getters, Debug/Clone derives, CN disambiguation, and plaintext-identity propagation invariants** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/plaintext.rs` previously had four inline tests (`default_identity_config`, `bind_and_accept`, `accepted_socket_has_tcp_nodelay_set`, `custom_identity_config`) covering the sunny accept path and the F-151 `TCP_NODELAY` regression, but left a significant amount of load-bearing behaviour unpinned: the `Default` delegation to the canonical `rustgate_core::constants::{ANONYMOUS_IDENTITY, PLAINTEXT_GROUP}` values (inline literals would silently desynchronise the plaintext listener from router/ACL/audit code that keys on the exact strings `"anonymous"` / `"__plaintext__"`), the `Clone` semantics of `PlaintextIdentityConfig` (cloned into the listener at bind time — a future switch to a shared `Arc` would create aliasing across sessions), the `Debug` derives on both `PlaintextIdentityConfig` and `PlaintextSession` (operators rely on them when logging listener lifecycle), the manual `Debug` impl on `PlaintextListener` (a derive regression would render the struct opaquely in `tracing::debug!`), the `PlaintextSession::peer_addr()` accessor contract (audit code keys off this to attribute events to the correct client; an inverted value would point every event at the listener itself), the documented "fingerprint is the empty string" invariant on plaintext `Identity` (router and audit code use `fingerprint.is_empty()` to distinguish TLS from non-TLS sessions — a sentinel string like `"plaintext"` would silently defeat those checks), the one-group-exactly invariant on the accepted identity (adding an extra group would widen the ACL surface of every plaintext session), the CN disambiguation invariant (two concurrent clients must receive distinct CNs, otherwise audit logs and dedup caches conflate them), the `into_split()` contract (returning unusable halves would silently break every caller), the `local_addr()` ephemeral-port reporting after `bind(0)` (service discovery and integration tests rely on this), and listener reusability across multiple sequential accepts. Twelve new inline tests close that gap: `default_identity_config_matches_core_constants` compares `cfg.cn` / `cfg.default_group` to the re-exported `ANONYMOUS_IDENTITY` / `PLAINTEXT_GROUP` constants rather than to inline string literals so a constant rename fails fast; `identity_config_clone_produces_independent_copy` mutates the source after cloning and asserts the clone retains the original values; `identity_config_debug_exposes_fields` pins that both field values appear in the derived `Debug` output; `listener_debug_includes_type_name_and_config` validates the custom `PlaintextListener::fmt` impl renders both `"PlaintextListener"` and `"identity_config"`; `session_derives_debug` asserts the `PlaintextSession` derive still produces the type name and `identity` field marker; `session_peer_addr_matches_client_local_addr` captures the client's `stream.local_addr()` from a spawned task and asserts it equals `session.peer_addr()` while also being `!=` the listener bind address (regression guard for the never-attribute-events-to-the-server invariant); `session_identity_fingerprint_is_empty_string_for_plaintext` asserts both `== ""` and `.is_empty()` so a sentinel rewrite is caught either way; `session_identity_groups_contain_only_default_group` pins `groups.len() == 1` and exact group membership, preventing accidental multi-group plaintext identities; `session_cn_is_unique_for_concurrent_connections` spawns two clients from distinct ephemeral ports and asserts the resulting CNs are `!=` (the `{cn}-{peer_addr}` format is the whole disambiguation mechanism); `session_into_split_round_trips_payload` round-trips a five-byte payload end-to-end through `into_split()` to prove the halves are usable; `listener_local_addr_reports_ephemeral_port` asserts `addr.port() > 0` after `bind(0)` so service discovery advertisements work; `listener_accepts_multiple_sequential_connections` loops three accepts against a live listener to pin the reusability invariant. Sixteen tests total (four existing + twelve new), zero changes to any production API. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-http::metrics` Prometheus metric-name schema, label ordering, fallback constructors, arity contract, and accessor idempotence** — `crates/rustgate-http/src/metrics.rs` previously had six inline tests (`accessors_return_without_init`, `publishes_can_increment`, `errors_can_increment_with_reason`, `circuit_state_can_set`, `queue_depth_can_set`, `dropped_can_increment_with_reason`) covering the sunny fallback-accessor happy path but leaving the Prometheus-contract surface entirely unpinned. The eight fully-qualified metric names (`rustgate_http_publishes_total`, `rustgate_http_errors_total`, `rustgate_http_request_duration_seconds`, `rustgate_http_circuit_state`, `rustgate_http_retries_total`, `rustgate_http_filtered_total`, `rustgate_http_queue_depth`, `rustgate_http_dropped_total`) are hard contracts with external Grafana dashboards, alerting rules, and scrape-aware tooling; a silent rename would break dashboards without any in-tree compile failure. The per-metric variable-label ordering is even more brittle because the Prometheus timeseries identity is `(metric_name, label_set)` — renaming `target`→`service` (or reordering `target,reason`→`reason,target`) forks the series and breaks `rate()` continuity on every dashboard. The four `fallback_*` helpers (`fallback_counter_vec`, `fallback_gauge`, `fallback_gauge_vec`, `fallback_histogram`) abort the process on error and had no coverage of their sunny paths. The `get_metric_with_label_values` arity contract — which production code relies on via `if let Ok(child)` guards — had no pin that the API returns `Err` (rather than panicking) on wrong-arity label slices. The `OnceLock::get_or_init` idempotence invariant (every accessor returns the same `&'static` on every call, preventing duplicate timeseries in scrape output) had no coverage. And the prometheus `HELP` lines — which render in `/metrics` exposition and Grafana tooltips — had no non-empty guard. Eleven new inline tests close those gaps: `metric_names_match_documented_schema` pins all eight `fq_name` values via `Collector::desc()`; `label_schema_is_stable_per_metric` extracts `variable_labels` via `Desc` and asserts the exact ordered label-name vector for every label-bearing metric (including the zero-label metrics `queue_depth` and `request_duration` that must stay zero-label); `wrong_label_arity_returns_error_not_panic` drives four cases through the two-label `errors` vector — 0, 1, 3, and 2 labels — and asserts `Err` on the three wrong-arity cases and `Ok` on the correct-arity case, pinning the `Result` contract production guards rely on; `fresh_label_pair_starts_at_zero` probes a never-before-seen label combination (`"fresh-label-start-zero-0x7d4e"`) and asserts `.get() == 0` — the Prometheus baseline invariant without which `rate(counter[5m])` silently shifts; `fallback_counter_vec_produces_callable_counter` direct-calls the helper, binds a child, asserts fresh-zero, increments, re-asserts; `fallback_gauge_starts_at_zero_and_is_signed` pins the documented `IntGauge` behaviour (reads zero, accepts negative values) that differentiates it from a counter — a regression that swapped the helper to an `IntCounter` flavour would fail here; `fallback_gauge_vec_returns_callable_vec` mirrors the counter-vec shape for the gauge-vec helper (used by `circuit_state`); `fallback_histogram_observes_count_and_sum` drives `observe(0.5)` and `observe(1.5)` and asserts `get_sample_count() == 2` and `get_sample_sum() ≈ 2.0`; `accessors_are_idempotent_reference_same_static_instance` uses `std::ptr::eq` across two back-to-back accessor calls for all eight metrics to pin the `OnceLock` invariant against any regression that created fresh collector instances per call (which would duplicate timeseries); `request_duration_observes_sample_count_and_sum` complements the helper-level test via the production accessor, capturing baseline + delta to be resilient against parallel-test interference; `metric_help_text_is_non_empty_for_every_accessor` iterates all eight metrics and asserts `!desc.help.is_empty()` so an accidental `"".to_string()` help would not sneak through review. Seventeen tests total (six existing + eleven new), zero changes to any production API. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-enrollment::ratelimit` constants, getters, Debug redaction of the poison callback, builder-pattern replacement semantics, Send+Sync trait bounds, rejected-request non-append invariant, off-by-one boundary, IPv4/IPv6 quota separation, and zero-`max_requests` shutdown behaviour** — `crates/rustgate-enrollment/src/ratelimit.rs` is the per-IP sliding-window rate limiter that gates every enrollment HTTP endpoint (`/enroll`, `/reenroll`, the EST endpoints) against burst abuse, and the module owns two load-bearing operational constants (`SWEEP_THRESHOLD = 1024`, `MIN_SWEEP_INTERVAL = 60s`), a custom `Debug` impl that deliberately projects the `PoisonCallback` `Arc<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>` to a boolean so `tracing::debug!(?limiter, …)` cannot leak the closure, a `with_poison_callback` builder method that replaces rather than stacks callbacks, and a `check()` control flow where the distinction between "rejected → do NOT record timestamp" and "allowed → record timestamp" is the single invariant that prevents a hostile client from pinning a legitimate IP into permanent rejection by spamming. Before this PR the inline suite (7 tests) covered the sunny-path under-limit / over-limit / separate-IPs / window-expiry / inline-sweep / poison-fail-closed (with and without callback) behaviours but left the surface enumerated above unpinned. Twelve new tests close the gap: `constants_match_documented_values` pins `SWEEP_THRESHOLD` and `MIN_SWEEP_INTERVAL` so a silent edit to either (both of which operators tune memory/CPU expectations around) fails here rather than surfacing as a post-deploy memory-footprint drift; `getters_return_configured_values_verbatim` pins that `max_requests()` and `window()` round-trip the constructor arguments unchanged, including zero values (no clamping, no defaulting, no rounding); `debug_format_renders_on_poison_as_bool_not_closure` pins the custom `Debug` impl's `on_poison: true`/`false` projection so a refactor that accidentally switched to `#[derive(Debug)]` on the `Option<PoisonCallback>` field would fail at compile time (because `Arc<dyn Fn()>` is not `Debug`) or at test time (because the rendering would change), and additionally pins that `max_requests` and `window` remain visible in the Debug output for forensics; `with_poison_callback_replaces_prior_callback` is the subtle builder-semantics test — a second `with_poison_callback` invocation overwrites the first via `self.on_poison = Some(cb)`, it does not compose into a callback list, and the test installs two callbacks with distinct atomic counters, poisons the mutex, and asserts `first_count == 0 && second_count == 1` so a future refactor that switched to a `Vec<PoisonCallback>` "stack-all-callbacks" model would have to be an explicit API change rather than a silent behaviour shift; `rate_limiter_and_poison_callback_are_send_sync` is the compile-time structural assertion via `fn assert_send_sync<T: Send + Sync>()` parameterised on both `RateLimiter` and the `PoisonCallback` type alias — the limiter is placed behind `Arc` and shared across tokio tasks by every enrollment HTTP handler, so Send+Sync is a structural requirement that must never silently narrow; `rejected_requests_do_not_append_timestamps` is the CRITICAL security-invariant test — fills to the limit with 2 allowed calls, issues 5 rejected calls, then peeks inside `state.map.get(&ip).unwrap()` and asserts the deque length is still exactly 2 (not 7), pinning that the implementation only calls `push_back(now)` on the allow path so a regression cannot turn the limiter into an always-closed gate that a hostile client can pin into permanent rejection by spamming; `boundary_allows_exactly_max_requests_then_rejects` pins the `timestamps.len() >= self.max_requests` comparison at the exact boundary (5 successful calls with `max=5`, 6th rejected) so a regression from `>=` to `>` would let 6 through before rejecting at 7 — a silent doubling of the burst ceiling on short windows that no integration test would catch; `ipv4_and_ipv6_maintain_separate_quotas` pins that `HashMap<IpAddr, …>` keys by enum discriminant so `10.0.0.1`, `::ffff:10.0.0.1` (IPv4-mapped IPv6), and `2001:db8::1` each carry their own quota, making the documented NAT caveat and the operational consequence (a dual-stack client can double their quota) part of the test contract — any future normalization that collapses IPv4-mapped IPv6 into IPv4 would break this test and therefore require a paired documentation update; `zero_max_requests_rejects_every_request` pins that `max_requests = 0` rejects even the first request (lower-boundary correctness), supporting the operational use case of sealing the endpoint via config reload rather than redeploy; `fresh_limiter_has_empty_state_map` pins that the constructor does not pre-seed the IP map; `single_successful_check_records_exactly_one_timestamp` is the regression guard against an accidental double-push inside the allow path; `distinct_ips_create_distinct_map_entries` pairs with the existing `separate_ips_have_separate_limits` — that test checks allow/reject behaviour across IPs, this one checks the internal shape (three distinct IPs create three distinct map entries with no accidental coalescing). Total suite grows from 7 to 19 tests; no behavioural change to any production code path. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport::verifier` dynamic-root-store rebuild contract, generation-change semantics, hint-list refresh, provider call-count instrumentation, CRL ingestion, and Send+Sync+object-safety trait-bound pins** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/verifier.rs` is the TLS client-cert verifier that sits between rustls's `ClientCertVerifier` trait and rustgate's root-store hot-reload path: it wraps a `RootStoreProvider` (`Box<dyn Fn() -> (u64, RootCertStore) + Send + Sync>`) that returns the current `(generation, RootCertStore)`, caches the last-built `WebPkiClientVerifier` keyed by generation, and exposes an `ArcSwap<&'static [DistinguishedName]>` of hint subjects that rustls uses to populate the `certificate_authorities` extension of the `CertificateRequest` handshake message. Before this PR the inline suite had three sunny-path tests (`new_builds_initial_verifier`, `generation_change_triggers_rebuild`, `debug_format`) but left ten contract-shaped invariants unpinned, each of which could silently regress without tripping a live TLS handshake test: the `Send + Sync` trait bounds on both `RootStoreProvider` and `DynamicCertVerifier` (a refactor that narrowed either to `Send` without `Sync` would only surface when a future `spawn` call site failed to compile), the `Debug` impl's documented projection (CRL count — not CRL contents — plus current generation), the post-rebuild `Debug` rendering after a provider generation bump (to pin that the cached generation is actually refreshed and not just the cached verifier), the `Arc::ptr_eq` cache-identity preservation on same-generation reads (rustls calls `current_verifier()` on every handshake, so a regression that rebuilt on every call would cost a `RootCertStore` clone + `WebPkiClientVerifier::builder().build()` per connection), the rebuild-on-*decrease* branch (the generation comparison is `!=`, not `<`, so a rollback from gen=5 back to gen=3 must also rebuild — a naive `<` spelling would silently keep stale roots after an operator reverted a bad CA rotation), the provider call-count instrumentation via a shared `AtomicU64` (proving the provider is invoked exactly once per `current_verifier()` call is the load-bearing performance pin), the hint-list `DistinguishedName` count matching the cert count (three CAs in → three entries out), the non-empty CRL constructor path through `rustls_pki_types::pem::PemObject::pem_slice_iter` decoding the `generate_crl(&[])` PEM output, the lock-free `root_hint_subjects` read path (the `ArcSwap` load must not acquire the internal mutex the rebuild path takes, so a test that holds the mutex and reads hints proves the decoupling), and the `Arc<DynamicCertVerifier>`-to-`Arc<dyn ClientCertVerifier>` upcast (which listener code relies on when wiring the verifier into the rustls `ServerConfig`). Eleven new inline tests close those gaps: `root_store_provider_and_verifier_are_send_sync` uses `fn assert_send_sync<T: Send + Sync + ?Sized>()` parameterised on both `RootStoreProvider` and `DynamicCertVerifier`; `debug_format_reports_crl_count_not_crl_contents` pins that `Debug` surfaces the CRL *count* integer but never renders raw CRL bytes; `debug_reflects_current_generation_after_rebuild` bumps the provider generation, calls `current_verifier()` to force the rebuild, and asserts the new generation appears in the `{:?}` rendering; `same_generation_does_not_rebuild_verifier` takes two successive `current_verifier()` calls on an unchanged provider and asserts `Arc::ptr_eq` to prove cache-identity is preserved; `generation_decrease_triggers_rebuild_not_just_increase` flips the provider generation from 1→5→3 and asserts `!Arc::ptr_eq` across the decrease step; `provider_is_called_once_per_current_verifier_invocation` wraps the provider with an `AtomicU64::fetch_add` counter and asserts the count is exactly `1 + N` (one construction call + N reads) after N `current_verifier()` invocations; `initial_hint_subject_count_matches_root_store_cert_count` issues three CAs via `TestCa`, composes them into one `RootCertStore`, and asserts the `root_hint_subjects()` length equals 3; `new_with_non_empty_crls_constructs_successfully` decodes `ca.generate_crl(&[])` PEM via `rustls_pki_types::pem::PemObject::pem_slice_iter` into `Vec<CertificateRevocationListDer<'static>>` and passes it through `DynamicCertVerifier::new` (the previous test suite could only exercise the empty-CRL path because `generate_crl` returns PEM — the plumbing the production path actually uses); `root_hint_subjects_does_not_acquire_cached_mutex` takes a `parking_lot::Mutex` that wraps the cached verifier state and, while the test thread holds the lock, calls `root_hint_subjects()` from a spawned thread with a 500ms timeout — if the hint accessor ever acquired the mutex, the spawned thread would deadlock and the timeout would fire; `verifier_usable_as_dyn_client_cert_verifier_via_arc` builds an `Arc<DynamicCertVerifier>`, upcasts to `Arc<dyn ClientCertVerifier>`, and exercises `offer_client_auth()` and `client_auth_root_hint_subjects()` through the trait-object boundary to prove the upcast compiles and the forwarding methods land correctly. Suite grows from 3 to 14 tests (17 total after counting a separate `supported_verify_schemes_not_empty`, `root_hint_subjects_keeps_old_list_on_rebuild_error`, and `root_hint_subjects_refreshes_after_ca_rotation` that each pin adjacent invariants). Zero changes to any production API — every new test drives the module's public surface through its existing constructors and methods. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-enrollment::est` RFC 7030 constants, `handle_cacerts`/`handle_csrattrs` response shapes, `decode_pkcs10_to_pem` defensive branches, `extract_csr_cn` whitespace tolerance + multi-RDN walk, and `build_response_with_www_auth` header contract** — `crates/rustgate-enrollment/src/est.rs` is the RFC 7030 Enrollment-over-Secure-Transport handler module: `handle_cacerts` serves the CA certificate bag to every first-time client at bootstrap, `handle_simpleenroll` signs initial CSRs, `handle_simplereenroll` rotates certificates while enforcing identity continuity (CSR subject CN must match the authenticated username), and `handle_csrattrs` reports CSR attribute guidance. Before this PR the inline suite (6 tests) covered only `extract_csr_cn` on the happy path, the empty-subject path, two "invalid input" rejection paths, and the `eq_ignore_ascii_case` arithmetic used by re-enrollment — leaving every module-level constant (three content-type tokens + `OID_COMMON_NAME` + `MAX_CSR_BODY`), the entire `decode_pkcs10_to_pem` base64-to-PEM wrapper, both `handle_cacerts` and `handle_csrattrs` response builders, and the `build_response_with_www_auth` 401-response helper without any direct test. The `Request<hyper::body::Incoming>`-typed handlers (`handle_simpleenroll`/`handle_simplereenroll`/`validate_and_read_body`/`read_body`) are deliberately left to integration-test coverage — `hyper::body::Incoming` cannot be built directly in unit tests (confirmed by the existing comment in `handlers/api.rs`) and forcing the seam open would bring in a layer of mock machinery that the rest of the crate's unit tests avoid. Fourteen new inline tests close the gaps on the purely-functional surface. Constants (3 tests): `content_type_constants_pin_rfc7030_wire_tokens` pins all three RFC 7030 content-type strings exactly — `application/pkcs7-mime; smime-type=certs-only` (§4.1.2 + §4.2.3 response type), `application/csrattrs` (§4.5.2), `application/pkcs10` (§4.2.1 request type) — a silent edit to any of these would still round-trip between rustgate binaries but break wire-level interop with iTAK, ATAK, and third-party EST clients; `oid_common_name_is_2_5_4_3_per_rfc4519` pins `OID_COMMON_NAME` to 2.5.4.3, the load-bearing discriminator inside `extract_csr_cn`'s RDN walk — a regression that silently pointed it at, say, 2.5.4.11 (OU) or 2.5.4.10 (O) would make re-enrollment accept every CSR regardless of subject CN and defeat the identity-continuity check, but wouldn't trip the existing `extract_cn_from_csr` happy-path test because rcgen emits the CN under the correct OID on the *encoding* side (the regression is on the *lookup* side); `max_csr_body_is_64_kib_matching_marti_sign_client_limit` pins `MAX_CSR_BODY = 65_536` — a silent bump to 1 MiB would open a DoS amplification window where an unauthenticated IP could force per-request 1 MiB allocations before the 401 surfaces (rate-limit fires before auth, but the hyper `Limited` cap is applied regardless of later auth result). Response shapes (3 tests): `handle_cacerts_returns_200_pkcs7_certs_only_with_ca_bag` uses `TestCa` + `CsrSigner::from_pem` to build a real signer, drives `handle_cacerts(&signer)`, and asserts status 200, `Content-Type: application/pkcs7-mime; smime-type=certs-only`, and that the body decodes cleanly as ASCII base64 (no PEM headers, no whitespace) — a regression that emitted raw DER or an empty body would still pass a status-only check but break every RFC 7030 client; `handle_csrattrs_returns_204_with_empty_body_and_no_content_type_header` pins the RFC 7030 §4.5.2 shape — 204 No Content, **no** `Content-Type` header (the `build_response` helper's `!content_type.is_empty()` guard is the load-bearing branch that produces this behaviour, and an accidental refactor that always set Content-Type would still "work" but violate the spec). `decode_pkcs10_to_pem` defensive branches (5 tests): `decode_pkcs10_to_pem_wraps_clean_base64_in_pem_headers` pins the success path — a real CSR's base64 goes in, PEM headers come out with the original base64 preserved between them; `decode_pkcs10_to_pem_rejects_empty_body_with_csr_error` pins the empty-body rejection and the exact `EnrollmentError::Csr` variant + "empty" substring in the message (so downstream log-grep rules can key off it); `decode_pkcs10_to_pem_rejects_whitespace_only_body_as_empty` pins the subtle branch where after RFC 8951 whitespace stripping a whitespace-only input is indistinguishable from an empty input — the test feeds `b" \t\r\n \t"` and asserts the same empty-body error, proving the `is_empty()` check fires after the strip, not before; `decode_pkcs10_to_pem_rejects_malformed_base64_with_csr_error` pins the early `b64::decode` validator — `!!!not-base64!!!` must surface an error with "invalid base64" in the message before any PEM wrapping runs so the downstream `CsrSigner` never sees garbage; `decode_pkcs10_to_pem_strips_whitespace_per_rfc_8951` pins the RFC 8951 tolerance contract — a base64 body with embedded CR+LF+tab+space must come out with all four stripped and the PEM body exactly equal to the un-wrapped base64 (a regression that failed to strip any one character would leave it embedded and break the round-trip). `extract_csr_cn` additional coverage (2 tests): `extract_csr_cn_tolerates_whitespace_in_body_per_rfc_8951` pins the same whitespace tolerance on the identity-extraction path — `extract_csr_cn` has its own independent strip (not shared with `decode_pkcs10_to_pem`) and a regression in one would silently diverge from the other; `extract_csr_cn_finds_cn_in_multi_rdn_subject` builds a CSR with `O=TestOrg, OU=Ops, CN=bob` in that order so the RDN walk has to iterate past two non-CN attributes before reaching the CN — a regression that stopped after the first RDN would miss the CN and return `None`, breaking re-enrollment identity continuity for any client whose CSR tooling emits subjects in non-CN-first order (OpenSSL's default is CN-first, but several commercial CA tools ship CN-last). `build_response_with_www_auth` contract (2 tests): `build_response_with_www_auth_sets_basic_realm_est_header` pins both the presence of the `WWW-Authenticate` header on a 401 (RFC 7235 requirement) and its exact value `Basic realm="est"` (RFC 7617 realm — curl / wget / browser credential stores key off this to disambiguate between per-service credentials); `build_response_with_www_auth_preserves_status_content_type_and_body` exercises the helper on a 403 (not just the 401 happy path) to pin that the `WWW-Authenticate` header is added unconditionally — a future refactor that narrowed the helper to only fire on 401 would silently drop the realm from any other status code that reuses it. Helpers: a `test_signer()` fixture uses `TestCa::new("Test CA")` + `CsrSigner::from_pem(cert, key, "TestOrg")` mirroring the existing `signing::tests` pattern, and a `collect_body()` helper wraps `http_body_util::BodyExt::collect(...).await.to_bytes()` so body assertions stay readable. Suite grows from 6 to 20 tests; zero changes to any production API — every test drives the module's existing pub/crate-private surface through its existing constructors. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-core::constants` value pins and cross-constant ordering invariants** — `crates/rustgate-core/src/constants.rs` is the advertised single source of truth in `CLAUDE.md` (the "Shared Constants" table under `.claude/rules/shared-infrastructure.md`) for every timeout, buffer cap, and synthetic-identity sentinel across all 16 workspace crates. Before this PR the inline suite had five tests — all five focused on the `is_reserved_group_id` helper — which left every timeout, frame-size cap, federation timing, and identity constant completely unpinned: a silent edit that flipped `READ_TIMEOUT` from 5 min to 5 s, or `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` from 16 MiB to 16 KiB, or `FEDERATION_HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT` below `FEDERATION_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL` (breaking the dead-peer detector), would propagate to every call site across TLS, plaintext, WebSocket, MQTT, enrollment, federation, and client crates without any test failure in the touched crate. Twenty-four new inline tests close those gaps in three tiers. **Constant-value pins (12 tests):** `read_timeout_pins_to_five_minutes` pins `READ_TIMEOUT == Duration::from_secs(5 * 60)` AND `as_secs() == 300` so an accidental unit-flip (minutes→seconds, or seconds→millis) fails both equalities; `write_timeout_pins_to_thirty_seconds`, `handshake_timeout_pins_to_ten_seconds`, `http_connection_timeout_pins_to_fifteen_seconds`, `federation_heartbeat_interval_pins_to_thirty_seconds`, `federation_heartbeat_timeout_pins_to_ninety_seconds`, `federation_health_check_interval_pins_to_five_seconds`, `federation_max_reconnect_delay_pins_to_five_minutes`, and `client_max_reconnect_delay_pins_to_thirty_seconds` pin every documented `Duration` constant exactly (both `from_secs(...)` and numeric `.as_secs()`); `max_frame_size_pins_to_sixteen_mib` pins `16 * 1024 * 1024 == 16_777_216` so both the human-readable form and the integer form have to agree; `plaintext_group_sentinel_pins_to_double_underscore_literal` and `anonymous_identity_pins_to_lowercase_literal` pin the two synthetic-identity strings (`"__plaintext__"` and `"anonymous"`) whose exact byte shape is load-bearing — the former is matched by the `is_reserved_group_id` ACL filter, the latter is assumed to be lowercase by the CN-normalization path. **Cross-constant ordering invariants (8 tests):** `read_timeout_exceeds_write_timeout` pins the "reads long-lived, writes bounded" asymmetry whose inversion would cause premature idle disconnects; `handshake_timeout_bounded_below_read_timeout` pins that a stalled TLS negotiation cannot consume a read slot for 5 minutes; `handshake_timeout_bounded_below_http_connection_timeout` pins that the TLS handshake is strictly shorter than the HTTP request/response envelope that contains it; `federation_heartbeat_timeout_tolerates_three_intervals` is the critical packet-loss-resilience invariant — `TIMEOUT >= INTERVAL * 3` means two consecutive heartbeat drops are tolerated before a peer is declared dead, and dropping the ratio below 3× would cause federation flap on ordinary packet loss; `federation_health_check_fires_multiple_times_per_heartbeat` and `federation_health_check_bounded_below_heartbeat_timeout` pin that the supervisor samples state faster than both the heartbeat cadence and the dead-peer detection window; `federation_reconnect_delay_exceeds_client_reconnect_delay` pins the documented "federation ceiling > client ceiling" ordering from the `CLIENT_MAX_RECONNECT_DELAY` module docstring (server-to-server links tolerate longer backoff than interactive clients); `every_duration_constant_is_non_zero` iterates all nine `Duration` constants in a labelled table and asserts none was accidentally set to `Duration::ZERO` or `from_secs(0)` — the failure message names the offending constant so the regression is one `cargo nextest` run away from root-caused. **Boundary and cross-sentinel tests (3 tests) + const-block floor assertion:** `max_frame_size_exceeds_one_mib_floor` uses `const { assert!(MAX_FRAME_SIZE >= 1024 * 1024) }` so the floor is enforced at compile time rather than at test execution — a regression that drops `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` below 1 MiB fails the build itself; `plaintext_group_constant_matches_reserved_shape` is a belt-and-braces pairing check that asserts `is_reserved_group_id(PLAINTEXT_GROUP) == true`, which fails loudly if either constant is renamed in isolation; `anonymous_identity_is_not_reserved_group_shape` pins the dual invariant — `ANONYMOUS_IDENTITY` is a CN (subject identifier) and must NOT collide with the reserved group namespace, otherwise an operator who typed `anonymous` as a group name would be rejected unexpectedly; `plaintext_and_anonymous_sentinels_are_disjoint` asserts the two synthetic values live in different namespaces (group vs CN) — a merge would make the group-vs-identity distinction ambiguous in log lines and audit records. Total suite grows from 5 to 29 tests; zero changes to any production API. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-enrollment::cms_bag` EST wire-format invariants (OID pins, degenerate SignedData structure, certificate count preservation, base64 alphabet, determinism, error shape)** — `crates/rustgate-enrollment/src/cms_bag.rs` builds the PKCS#7 certs-only bag that every EST `/cacerts` and `/simpleenroll` response depends on (RFC 5652 degenerate `SignedData` with zero signers, zero digest algorithms, `encapContentInfo` advertising `id-data` with no content, and the certificate SET carrying the payload). Before this PR the inline suite (5 tests) covered the happy-path `ContentInfo` round-trip and two error branches (empty input, invalid DER) but left every wire-format invariant load-bearing for client interop unpinned: a silent regression in the outer `content_type` OID (dispatching to `id-envelopedData` instead of `id-signedData` would produce a bag that decodes via `ContentInfo::from_der` but fails dispatch at every known EST client), the inner `EncapsulatedContentInfo` shape (if `eContentType` drifted off `id-data` or `eContent` became `Some(_)`, strict verifiers reject the bag), the `CMSVersion::V1` pin (the RFC-mandated version for a zero-signer bag; a drift to V3 breaks EST clients that validate version as an allow list), the empty `digestAlgorithms` / `signerInfos` sets (the defining property of a "degenerate" SignedData — a leaked signer would trip any verifier trying to check it), the `certificates: Some(_)` field being present (a `None` would succeed encoding but silently deliver zero certs — worse than an error because it looks valid on the wire), certificate count preservation for varying `N` and the DER-canonical membership of each input cert in the output set (a DER SET sorts its members, so order may shift, but every input must survive — losing a cert silently breaks EST chain completion), the base64 output using the RFC 4648 Section 4 standard alphabet with no URL-safe characters and no whitespace/newlines (RFC 7030 §3.2.4 forbids wrapping, and some EST clients parse line-by-line), the byte-identical determinism of repeated encodes (DER is canonical, so any drift points at an encoder bug), the agreement between `build_certs_only_base64` and `b64::encode(build_certs_only_bag(...))` so the two helpers cannot diverge on a padding / alphabet quirk, and the error-message content for the empty-certs and parse-failure branches (operators grep these strings in runbooks, so they have to stay stable). Seventeen new inline tests close the gap: `outer_content_type_is_id_signed_data` pins the outer OID to `1.2.840.113549.1.7.2`; `encap_content_is_id_data_with_absent_econtent` decodes the inner `SignedData` and asserts `eContentType == 1.2.840.113549.1.7.1` and `eContent.is_none()`; `signed_data_version_is_v1_for_degenerate_bag` pins `CmsVersion::V1`; `digest_algorithms_set_is_empty` and `signer_infos_set_is_empty` pin the defining zero-signer invariants; `certificates_field_is_present_not_absent` guards against a silent `None`; `certificate_count_is_preserved_for_various_sizes` drives `N ∈ {1, 2, 3, 5, 10}` through the bag and asserts the output set size matches; `every_input_certificate_appears_in_output` uses three CN-distinct certs (`host-a.example`, `host-b.example`, `host-c.example`) and pattern-matches each input DER against the decoded `CertificateChoices::Certificate(_)` set members to pin membership without depending on DER-SET ordering; `base64_output_uses_standard_alphabet_only` walks every character of the base64 output and asserts membership in `A-Z a-z 0-9 + / =`; `base64_output_has_no_whitespace_or_newlines` pins the "no wrapping" invariant; `bag_bytes_are_deterministic_for_same_input` encodes the same input twice and asserts byte-identical output; `base64_helper_matches_standard_encoding_of_der_helper` pins that the two helpers cannot drift; `empty_certs_returns_signing_error_with_named_message` destructures the `EnrollmentError::Signing(msg)` variant and asserts `msg.contains("zero certificates")`; `invalid_der_returns_signing_error_with_parse_context` and `truncated_cert_der_fails_at_parse_step` both pin that the error message names the certificate-parse step (so on-call knows where to look); `mixed_valid_and_invalid_input_fails_whole_call` pins the all-or-nothing contract (a partial-success mode would emit a bag missing the bad cert — the CA operator never asked for that); `single_byte_garbage_fails` guards against a parser that short-circuits on the SEQUENCE header without validating the tail. A local `decode_signed_data` helper projects the outer `ContentInfo` through `SignedData::from_der(ci.content.to_der()?)` so every structural assertion reuses the same decode path rather than open-coding the projection at each call site. Total suite grows from 5 to 22 tests; zero changes to any production API. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-server::otel` OpenTelemetry layer construction, shutdown idempotence, error surface, and `Send + Sync + 'static` bound** — `crates/rustgate-server/src/otel.rs` is the module-level `OnceLock<SdkTracerProvider>` that bridges every `tracing::info!` / `debug!` / `#[instrument]` span in the workspace into the OTLP gRPC exporter (tonic) for distributed-trace export. Before this PR the inline suite pinned only two behaviours — `init_layer` succeeding against `http://localhost:4317` and `shutdown` being panic-free before init — which left seven contract branches untouched that a refactor could silently break without tripping an integration-level OTel check. Seven new inline tests close those gaps: `init_layer_tolerates_empty_endpoint_without_panic` pins the observed behaviour that tonic's `with_endpoint("")` falls through to the crate default URI rather than erroring, so a deployment that ships an unset `RUSTGATE_OTEL_ENDPOINT` starts cleanly and the connect failure surfaces later as a runtime export error rather than a startup panic (a regression that added `assert!(!endpoint.is_empty())` upstream would break this contract); `init_layer_rejects_malformed_endpoint_without_panic` drives `"not a valid uri at all"` through the builder and asserts `.is_err()` to pin the `map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!("OTLP span exporter: {e}"))` branch so a bad config file surfaces as a readable error in the startup log rather than a stack trace; `init_layer_error_message_names_otlp_span_exporter` pins the `"OTLP span exporter"` prefix on the mapped error via the same malformed URI so operators can grep the startup log to distinguish this failure mode from other `tracing_subscriber` layer errors further down the init sequence — a silent drift of the prefix would break runbook regex patterns (the test uses a manual `match` rather than `.expect_err` because `Box<dyn Layer<Registry> + Send + Sync>` has no `Debug` impl for the `Ok` arm to format); `shutdown_is_idempotent_across_repeated_calls` loops `shutdown()` five times to pin that both the populated-`PROVIDER` and empty-`PROVIDER` branches stay no-op rather than unwrap-on-nothing — a regression that changed `if let Some(provider)` to `.unwrap()` would fail here under any test that doesn't populate `PROVIDER` via the successful init path; `init_layer_returns_send_sync_static_layer` uses `fn assert_send_sync_static<T: Send + Sync + 'static + ?Sized>(_: &T)` to compile-time-assert the returned `Box<dyn Layer<Registry>>` is safe to move onto a background task — a refactor that introduced a non-`Send` temporary inside the layer chain would fail at test-compile time rather than at the call site in `rustgate_server::setup`; `init_layer_accepts_empty_service_name_without_panic` pins that the OTel SDK treats `service.name` as an opaque string and does not reject `""` — deployments that key service.name off an env var that silently evaluated to empty must still start cleanly, so a regression that added `assert!(!service_name.is_empty())` would fail here; `init_layer_accepts_long_service_name_without_truncation_error` pins the 512-char boundary — some deployments synthesize `service.name` from `cluster.namespace.pod` triplets that exceed 128 chars, so the contract is "any string that fits in a `String` flows through" and a future narrowing to a fixed cap would trip the test. Suite grows from 2 to 9 tests; zero changes to any production API. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-enrollment::keypair::ZeroizingKeyPair` — trait bounds, cryptographic contract on delegated `sign`/`der_bytes`, Debug non-leakage, and Drop safety** — `crates/rustgate-enrollment/src/keypair.rs` hosts the `ZeroizingKeyPair` newtype that wraps `rcgen::KeyPair` so the CA signing key's serialized-DER copy is wiped on drop (issue #766 / IMPROVEMENT-ANALYSIS F-39 residue-closure). The type is the `Issuer<'static, ZeroizingKeyPair>` plugged into `CsrSigner` and `RevocationStore` in `signing.rs` — the CA-key lifecycle path every enrollment request traverses. Before this PR the inline suite had four tests (`signs_via_delegation`, `public_key_delegation_matches_inner`, `debug_redacts_inner`, `drop_wipes_serialized_der`) that established the happy-path wipe-on-drop contract but left five contract-shaped invariants unpinned that a refactor could silently break without the downstream `Arc<CsrSigner>` call sites tripping: (a) the `Send + Sync` bound the tokio multi-threaded runtime requires (a regression that added a `Cell` / `Rc` / non-`Send` field would fail at the `Arc<CsrSigner>` call site one trait-bound layer removed, not at a test site adjacent to the change); (b) the `'static` bound `Issuer<'static, _>` specifically demands (a regression that added a `&'a KeyPair` field would silently break every caller storing the signer in an `Arc` past any stack frame); (c) the stricter "Debug must never leak any fragment of DER bytes even incidentally" contract — the existing `debug_redacts_inner` test only checks for the presence of the `[REDACTED]` marker, so a regression that added `.field("serialized_der", &...)` alongside the marker would leak the very bytes the wrapper exists to protect; (d) the cryptographic contract on the delegated `sign` method (distinct inputs produce distinct outputs; empty-message signing is total over byte slices rather than panicking; bounded ECDSA P-256 DER signature length 70–72 bytes across repeated calls of the same input so a counter-mode-like state leak would trip); and (e) the shape contract on `der_bytes()` — rcgen's default ECDSA P-256 algorithm returns the raw SEC1 uncompressed public-key point (0x04 tag + 32-byte X + 32-byte Y = exactly 65 bytes), and a regression that accidentally PEM-encoded or hex-encoded the return value would break every downstream caller that concatenates the bytes into an X.509 SPKI structure. Nine new inline tests close the gap: `wrapper_is_send_and_sync` compile-time-asserts the `Send + Sync` bound via `fn assert_send_sync<T: Send + Sync>(_: &T) {}`; `wrapper_satisfies_static_signing_key_trait_object` asserts the `'static` bound with a separate `fn assert_static<T: 'static>() {}` and then moves the wrapper into a `Box<dyn SigningKey + Send + Sync + 'static>` that exercises the trait-object shape the downstream `Issuer` expects (a regression that narrowed the bound would fail at this call rather than at the `Box::new`); `debug_does_not_leak_der_bytes` samples a 12-byte window from the middle of the DER serialisation (the start is ASN.1 overhead `30 82 ...` that also appears in other structures' headers, the end may contain small integers that coincidentally show up in the debug text), wraps the key, and does a windowed byte-slice search of the rendered Debug string for the probe — a binary substring match catches any regression that accidentally printed the DER even through a non-UTF-8 path; `sign_produces_distinct_outputs_for_distinct_inputs` asymmetrically compares signature byte slices across two distinct inputs (pinning the collision-resistance claim of ECDSA without tying the test to rcgen's default algorithm — a future switch away from ECDSA P-256 still holds); `sign_empty_message_does_not_panic_and_returns_signature` drives `sign(&[])` and pins the "sign is total over byte slices" contract so a future caller who passes an empty buffer from a failed length check does not panic the whole tokio task; `sign_same_message_twice_returns_bounded_length_signatures` pins both the exact P-256 DER length range (70..=72 for `SEQUENCE { r, s }` where r and s are 256-bit integers whose byte length may vary by one byte on high-bit flips) and the `abs_diff(sig1.len(), sig2.len()) <= 2` stability assertion — a regression that returned progressively-longer signatures would indicate a counter-mode-like state leak and would fail here; `der_bytes_are_raw_sec1_uncompressed_point` pins both `der[0] == 0x04` (the SEC1 uncompressed-point tag; a PEM-encoded return would start with `0x2d` from `-----BEGIN` and a hex-encoded return would start with an ASCII digit) and `der.len() == 65` (the exact uncompressed-point length for P-256: 1 tag + 32 X + 32 Y); `drop_does_not_panic` exercises the Drop path in a scoped block and relies on the test harness's panic reporting to catch any regression that introduced a `.unwrap()` inside Drop (a panic-in-drop is a double-panic that aborts the process, so this pin is load-bearing for process resilience); `multiple_wrappers_drop_independently` loops four constructions-and-drops to pin the independence contract so a regression that moved the wipe into a shared `static` buffer would trip on the second drop. Total suite grows from 4 to 13 tests; zero changes to any production API. `cargo nextest run -p rustgate-enrollment keypair::` reports 13/13 passing. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-websocket::rate_limit` `WsRateLimiter` / `RateLimitExceeded` sliding-window contract, flag shape, overflow guard, and disconnect threshold** — `crates/rustgate-websocket/src/rate_limit.rs` is the per-connection sliding-window limiter every WebSocket session runs each inbound frame through (`check(size)` → `Result<(), RateLimitExceeded>`, with `should_disconnect()` as the sustained-abuse tripwire that the session task calls after each reject). The existing six inline tests covered happy-path admission and per-dimension rejection (frames-only, bytes-only), sustained disconnect, violation counter reset, and the "0 means unlimited" sentinel on both axes, but left eight contract-shaped invariants unpinned: the `Debug` derive that every `tracing::debug!(?limiter, …)` call site and the `missing_debug_implementations = "warn"` workspace lint rely on; the `RateLimitExceeded` two-bool flag shape (`frame_exceeded` vs `byte_exceeded`) that SIEM ingest and operator triage key off of (a silent swap would produce nonsense alerts); the single-oversized-first-frame path where `size > max_bytes_per_sec` must reject without needing accumulated history and must not credit the rejected frame to the counters; the getter contract (`frame_count` / `byte_count` / `max_frames_per_sec` / `max_bytes_per_sec`) where a refactor that swapped two getters would compile but produce nonsense Grafana gauges; the window-rollover invariant that `maybe_reset_window` must zero **both** counters (the existing `violation_counter_resets_on_success` test only pins violations==0 and would pass even if `byte_count` were left stale); the `max_violations = 0` disables-disconnect sentinel; the exact-threshold `violations >= max_violations` boundary (not `>`); the `saturating_add` overflow guard for pathological `size: usize` values where a naive `byte_count + size` would wrap; the zero-sized-frame admission contract (keepalive probes must count against the frame cap but not the byte cap, otherwise a chatty client bypasses the frame cap forever); and the `Send + Sync` auto-trait bounds that every tokio-multi-thread session spawn relies on. Thirteen new inline tests close those gaps. `ws_rate_limiter_and_error_type_are_debug` compile-time-asserts the `Debug` bound on both types via `fn assert_debug<T: Debug>()`; `rejects_with_only_frame_exceeded_flag_when_byte_limit_disabled` / `rejects_with_only_byte_exceeded_flag_when_frame_limit_disabled` / `rejects_with_both_flags_when_both_limits_breach_on_same_frame` pin the full three-way truth table of the flag-shape contract (a silent swap of the two `bool` fields would fail every one of them with a concrete assertion); `single_oversized_frame_is_rejected_without_accumulated_history` builds a 100-byte-per-second limiter, sends one 101-byte frame as the first call, and asserts both `byte_exceeded` is set AND that `frame_count() == 0` / `byte_count() == 0` after the reject — so a regression that credited rejected frames to the counters (and thereby tightened future budgets) fails here; `getters_return_their_dedicated_fields` pins four distinct values (7, 4096, 0, 0) through the four getters so any two-way swap trips a concrete equality assertion; `frame_and_byte_count_advance_independently_per_successful_check` uses three frames of distinct sizes (10, 25, 7) so `frame_count == 3` and `byte_count == 42` distinguish cleanly from any cross-wiring; `window_rollover_resets_both_frame_count_and_byte_count` is the strongest of the eight — it burns 80 bytes in one frame, rewinds `window_start` past the 1-second threshold, then sends a 90-byte frame that would sum to 170>100 if `byte_count` had not been cleared, and asserts both counters reflect only the post-rollover state (pins that `maybe_reset_window` zeros **both** counters, not just `frame_count`); `zero_max_violations_never_triggers_disconnect_regardless_of_breaches` subjects the limiter to 1,000 consecutive violations and asserts `should_disconnect()` stays `false`, pinning the `max_violations > 0` sentinel in the predicate; `should_disconnect_fires_at_exact_threshold_not_one_later` pins the `>=` boundary by driving exactly `max_violations=2` violations and asserting disconnect fires on the second, not the third — a regression to `>` (which would require one extra violation before evicting) fails here; `saturating_add_guards_against_size_arithmetic_overflow` calls `check(usize::MAX)` with a non-zero accumulated byte_count and asserts both the reject-not-panic contract and that the limiter state stays intact for the next legitimate check (an attacker-crafted WebSocket frame header claiming `usize::MAX` size must never crash the session task); `zero_size_frames_count_against_frame_cap_but_not_byte_cap` sends four zero-sized frames through a 3-frame/1000-byte limiter and asserts the fourth breaches the frame cap with `frame_exceeded=true` / `byte_exceeded=false` — pinning that the frame counter increments on zero-byte frames (otherwise chatty clients bypass every frame cap) while the byte counter stays at zero; `ws_rate_limiter_is_send_and_sync` compile-asserts `Send + Sync` on both types via generic no-op functions so a refactor that added a `Rc<...>` or `Cell<...>` field (both `!Send`) would fail here rather than at the distant session-spawn call site. Test suite grows from 6 to 19 tests; zero changes to any production code path. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-federation::v1::client` contract-shape surface — `OnMessage`/`ReplayDrain` `Send+Sync+'static` bounds, `V1ClientConfig` Debug `finish_non_exhaustive()` redaction, `V1Client` `Debug`+`Send`+`Sync` bounds, idempotent `shutdown()`, `peer()` accessor reference-identity, and send-after-shutdown error-message contract** — `crates/rustgate-federation/src/v1/client.rs` hosts the outbound v1 TAK federation client, the piece `FederationManager` spawns per peer to maintain a long-lived connection, replay stored messages on reconnect, and surface send failures via an error channel to the orchestrator. Before this PR the module had five tests, all integration-style (construct a real `V1Client` against an unreachable address, drive it through the start/shutdown happy path, assert peer state reflects the error). Those are valuable but miss a significant contract-shape seam: a refactor that silently dropped `Send` from `OnMessage` (breaking `tokio::spawn(client.run_session(...))` at every call site), narrowed `V1ClientConfig` to `#[derive(Debug)]` via a future newtype wrapper that impl'd `Debug` for `Arc<dyn Fn>` by printing its vtable pointer (re-opening a callback-pointer leak path in operator logs), removed the `Option::take` idempotence guard from `V1Client::shutdown` so a second call panicked during graceful drain (binding a SIGTERM handler plus an atexit hook plus an admin endpoint means real production triggers multi-shutdown paths), or rebroke the documented `"V1 client send channel closed"` error-message contract (SIEM rules grep for this exact string on federation send failures) — would pass every existing integration-style test in the file and break downstream call sites in ways that would only surface in production. Eleven new inline tests close that seam: `on_message_is_send_sync_static` and `replay_drain_is_send_sync_static` compile-time-assert the type-alias bounds via `fn assert_bounds<T: Send + Sync + 'static>()` so a regression at the alias trips at test-compile time rather than at the first spawn site; `v1_client_config_is_send` mirrors the same for `V1ClientConfig` (which is moved across an `await` into `tokio::spawn` in `V1Client::start` — if any field ever became `!Send` via a future `Rc` slipping in, the spawn would fail to compile at every call site); `v1_client_is_send_and_sync` pins the owning-task / polling-task split in `FederationManager`; `config_debug_surfaces_operator_fields_and_redacts_closures` drives a real config built via `TestCa::issue_client_cert` through `format!("{cfg:?}")` and asserts the operator-facing fields (`addr = 192.0.2.42:19990`, `server_name`, `server_uid`, `max_reconnects`) are present, the `finish_non_exhaustive()` sentinel `..` is present (so a future refactor that swaps to `finish()` — which hard-errors if a new non-Debug field is added — fails here rather than silently losing the "fields exist but are not shown" signal), and the callback field names `on_message`, `replay_drain`, `connector` do NOT appear in the rendering (belt-and-suspenders against a future `Arc<dyn Fn>` newtype that impl'd `Debug` by printing the vtable pointer); `config_debug_renders_durations_in_debug_format` pins the `42s` / `96s` / `7s` Duration Debug tokens so a refactor that stringified them via `Display` (producing ambiguous "42" vs "42s") fails here — operators triage heartbeat misconfigurations from the Debug line and "42" without a unit is actively misleading; `v1_client_type_implements_debug` compile-asserts `V1Client: Debug` via the helper and additionally constructs a live `V1Client` with `max_reconnects=0` + unreachable 127.0.0.1:19995 address, formats it with `{client:?}` (exercising the `Debug` derive on a real `Some(JoinHandle)` + `Some(watch::Sender)` shape), and shuts down before the outer timeout; `shutdown_is_idempotent_across_repeated_calls` calls `client.shutdown().await` three times back-to-back — the first drains the real task handle, the second and third hit the `Option::take` → `None` branches and must silently no-op rather than panic (a regression would crash the server binary during the very sequence that's supposed to drain cleanly); `peer_accessor_returns_same_reference_across_calls` uses `std::ptr::eq` on two consecutive `client.peer()` return values and asserts they point at the same underlying `PeerHandle` — a regression that accidentally wrapped each call in `Arc::new` would break every cache / metric / circuit-breaker keyed on handle identity; `send_error_is_returned_with_documented_message` is the strongest of the eleven — shuts down the client, sleeps 100ms to let the receiver fully drop, then `tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(2), client.send(...))` calls send and asserts (a) the future returns promptly (not hangs — a regression that held the sender side open would surface here rather than in CI's 10-minute timeout) AND (b) the error message contains the exact literal `"V1 client send channel closed"` (pins the contract downstream SIEM rules grep for on federation send failures). Tests are all gated under the existing `#[cfg(test)] #[allow(clippy::panic, clippy::unwrap_used)]` header; total suite grows from 5 to 15 tests (one existing test is `client_send_fails_after_shutdown` which asserted `is_err()` without inspecting the error content — the new test explicitly complements rather than duplicates it by pinning the message string). (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-server::acl_reloader` fail-closed contract on stored-hash advancement, empty-policy `None` install, case-insensitive CN normalisation, full deny-list plumbing, error-message file-path pinning, background-watcher smoke, and `set_hash` metrics-hook round-trip** — `crates/rustgate-server/src/acl_reloader.rs` owns the SIGHUP-free hot-reload path for the group ACL policy — a security-relevant control that `Router::swap_acl` consumes directly, so an operator mistake here either (a) silently flips a deny into an allow, or (b) silently wedges the reloader into "already loaded, nothing to do" forever after a single bad edit. Before this PR the inline suite (7 tests: happy-path change detection, ACL-swap round-trip, invalid-JSON rejection, missing-file rejection, hash determinism, hash-input-sensitivity, and the #749 reserved-sentinel rejection) covered the sunny path plus two of the four failure modes, but left several contract-shaped invariants unpinned: `Arc<AclReloader>` `Send + Sync` bounds (the `tokio::spawn` in `spawn_watcher` moves the Arc across tasks — losing either bound would fail to compile at the spawn site, not here); the empty-input boundary of `compute_hash` (a refactor that swapped SHA-256 for a different digest algorithm would slip past every determinism test but fail here against the canonical all-zero-length hash `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855`); the 32-byte output length as a compile-time shape pin (a regression widening to `Vec<u8>` would propagate silently through `set_hash` / `new` / the stored Mutex cell); the *fail-closed* invariant that a parse failure MUST NOT advance the stored hash (a reordering bug that moved `*guard = new_hash` above the parse/validate step would make a single bad edit permanently silence the reloader — one warn line then amnesia); the same fail-closed invariant on the file-read branch (a transient mount-point glitch must not poison recovery); the empty-policy optimisation where `groups: {}` installs as `None` in the router's `ArcSwap` rather than `Some(empty policy)` (the two values have opposite semantics in the default-policy path — `None` means "no policy, allow everything" and `Some(empty)` means "policy exists but no allow-list entries, deny everything"); the case-insensitive CN normalisation at install time across ALL FOUR lists (`send`, `receive`, `deny_send`, `deny_receive` — missing `.to_ascii_lowercase()` on any one silently breaks operators who write mixed-case JSON); the full-plumbing branch of `GroupAcl::with_deny(send, receive, deny_send, deny_receive)` (a regression that passed the same list twice would still compile, would still swap a policy, and would still pass every sunny-path test — but would break the documented deny-first precedence the router's `is_send_authorized` / `is_receive_authorized` depend on); the operator-triage file-path interpolation in the two `anyhow::anyhow!` wraps (dropping `{}` from either the read-failure or parse-failure branch would make multi-ACL deployments un-debuggable from logs alone); the `spawn_watcher` fire-and-forget signature taking `self: &Arc<Self>` so external callers can keep the Arc for later `set_hash` mutation; and the `#[cfg(feature = "metrics")]` `set_hash` REST-API hook that advances the stored hash out-of-band (the default-features build does not exercise it at all). Twelve new inline tests close those gaps: `arc_acl_reloader_is_send_and_sync_for_background_task` compile-asserts `Arc<AclReloader>: Send + Sync` via `fn assert_send_sync<T: Send + Sync>()`; `compute_hash_empty_input_matches_known_sha256_boundary` pins the hex literal `e3b0c442…7852b855` via a local `hex_literal` helper (no dev-dep on the `hex` crate); `compute_hash_output_length_is_thirty_two_bytes` shape-pins the `[u8; 32]` return via an unannotated `let _compile_pin: [u8; 32] = h`; `reload_does_not_advance_stored_hash_on_parse_failure` writes invalid JSON, asserts the FIRST reload errors, then calls reload a second time against the same bad file and asserts it ALSO errors (proves the stored hash did not advance past the bad-file hash and silence the reloader); `reload_does_not_advance_stored_hash_on_missing_file` mirrors the contract on the read-failure branch, removes the file, asserts two consecutive `Err` returns, then writes a *different* valid file (`UPDATED_ACL`, not `INITIAL_ACL`) and asserts the next reload swaps — proving the stored hash is still on the initial value rather than the empty-file hash from the missing state; `reload_swaps_to_none_when_policy_becomes_empty` overwrites `INITIAL_ACL` with `{"groups": {}}` and asserts `router.acl_snapshot().as_ref().is_none()` rather than `Some(empty)` — the two values have opposite routing semantics; `reload_normalises_acl_cns_to_ascii_lowercase` writes a file with uppercase CNs `ALICE`/`BOB`/`CHARLIE`/`DAVE` across all four lists, reloads, and asserts both the presence of the lowercase form AND the absence of the uppercase form in every list (negative assertion prevents a regression that only lowercased *some* of the four lists from slipping past by duplicating data); `reload_installs_multiple_groups_independently` parses a two-group file (`alpha`, `beta`) and asserts both are present in `policy.groups()` and `policy.len() == 2` (pins the `group_count` the audit log line emits); `reload_carries_deny_send_and_deny_receive_through_to_router` writes a file where `mallory` is on both `send` and `deny_send` and `eve` is on both `receive` and `deny_receive`, reloads, and asserts the deny-first precedence holds (`is_send_authorized("alpha", "mallory") == false`, `is_receive_authorized("alpha", "eve") == false`) — a regression that passed the `send` list twice in the `GroupAcl::with_deny(send, receive, deny_send, deny_receive)` call would let mallory send; `reload_error_on_parse_failure_names_file_path_for_operator_triage` and `reload_error_on_missing_file_names_file_path_for_operator_triage` assert the error `Display` contains both `path.display().to_string()` and a failure-mode token (`parse` / `read`) so an operator grepping audit lines can distinguish "I forgot to create the file" from "JSON syntax error"; `spawn_watcher_returns_immediately_without_panicking` drives `spawn_watcher` with a 10ms interval on an `Arc<AclReloader>` reference and sleeps 50ms to let the task tick at least once, proving the `drop(tokio::spawn(…))` fire-and-forget path does not panic synchronously; `set_hash_updates_stored_hash_observable_via_subsequent_reload` (gated on `#[cfg(feature = "metrics")]`) constructs a reloader with a deliberately-wrong initial hash, calls `set_hash` to the on-disk file's hash out-of-band, and asserts the next `reload()` returns `Ok(false)` — pins the REST-API mutation-path contract. Suite grows from 7 to 19 tests (20 with `--features metrics`); zero production code changes. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport::throttle` `ClientThrottle`/`ThrottleConfig` derive bounds, `rate()` accessor round-trip, zero-burst/zero-rate clamping, bucket-starts-full invariant, refill cap, pack/unpack boundary correctness, `config.build()` producing independent throttles, and CAS correctness under concurrent contention** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/throttle.rs` is the per-session token-bucket admission gate that every accepted TLS session wraps around inbound traffic; its packed `AtomicU64` state (`tokens_x100 << 32 | ts_offset_ms`) is load-bearing for the concurrent `allow()` fast-path, so any regression to the pack/unpack algebra or the refill-clamp invariant silently corrupts per-client rate limiting. The original suite covered happy-path refill and basic allow/deny, but left several defensive invariants unpinned: eleven new inline tests close those gaps. `client_throttle_is_debug_send_sync` and `throttle_config_is_debug_clone_copy_default_send_sync` pin the trait-bound surface (`Debug + Send + Sync` on `ClientThrottle`, plus `Copy + Clone + Default` on `ThrottleConfig`) so that future maintainers cannot accidentally remove a derive that downstream call sites depend on when sharing a throttle across the read loop, the drop path, and the audit sink via `Arc`, or when passing a config through the builder chain by value; both are compile-time checks via generic `fn assert<T: …>()` helpers. `rate_accessor_returns_configured_value` asserts the `rate()` accessor is a lossless u32 round-trip across `[0, 1, 100, u32::MAX]` — this pins the wire contract for rate=0 as a live sentinel (meaning "refills disabled", distinct from "unlimited") and prevents any future `clamp`/`saturating_*` from quietly rewriting the stored value. `new_clamps_zero_burst_to_one_so_throttle_always_allows_at_least_one_message` verifies that a misconfigured `burst=0` gets clamped to capacity=1 at construction time, preserving the invariant that a configured-but-misconfigured throttle still admits at least one message (rather than hard-denying all traffic to the client). `new_with_zero_rate_exhausts_then_never_refills` pins the rate=0-as-disable semantics: the bucket starts full, drains normally under `allow()`, and once empty stays empty regardless of elapsed wall time — this is the contract that lets operators set rate=0 to freeze a client at its current burst without the token accountant reviving it on the next tick. `initial_available_equals_capacity_so_bucket_starts_full` iterates over `[1, 5, 10, 200, 65536]` and asserts that a freshly constructed `ClientThrottle` reports `available() == burst`, pinning the bucket-starts-full invariant that prevents new sessions from being admission-denied before their first inbound frame. `available_never_exceeds_capacity_regardless_of_idle_duration` sleeps 50 ms on a `rate=1000, burst=5` throttle and asserts `available() <= 5`, catching any regression to the `.min(max_x100)` refill clamp that would otherwise let the bucket overflow above its advertised burst ceiling. `pack_unpack_round_trip_at_u32_max_boundary` and `pack_unpack_is_injective_for_distinct_token_counts_at_same_timestamp` pin the packed-state encoding: the first verifies that `u32::MAX` tokens and `u32::MAX` timestamp offsets survive a pack→unpack round trip without truncation, and the second verifies that two distinct `tokens_x100` values at the same timestamp produce distinct `u64` states (which is the injectivity property the CAS loop relies on to detect stale reads). `config_build_produces_independent_throttles_per_call` asserts that two throttles built from the same `ThrottleConfig` draw from independent token pools — this pins the build-is-a-factory contract so that per-session isolation does not regress into accidental shared state if the config internals ever gain an `Arc`. `concurrent_allow_calls_never_exceed_burst_without_refill` is the strongest defensive test: it spawns 16 `std::thread::scope` threads each attempting 100 `allow()` calls against a `rate=0, burst=50` throttle, counts total successes via `AtomicUsize`, and asserts `total == 50` exactly — this proves the CAS loop in `allow()` is linearizable under contention (no lost updates, no double-counts), which is the core safety property of the entire module. All eleven tests pass deterministically (`20/20` via nextest, with the existing `9` plus the `11` new) and the full-workspace clippy run with the canonical feature set stays clean. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-http::dlq` `DeadLetterQueue` `Send + Sync + 'static` bounds, `DEFAULT_DLQ_MAX_BYTES` constant pin, per-target rotation scope, `DlqEntry` full-field JSON shape, payload-base64 round-trip, `format_timestamp_now` ISO-8601 contract, broader path-traversal defence surface, and `finish()` idempotence / empty-queue safety** — `crates/rustgate-http/src/dlq.rs` is the encrypted dead-letter queue that circuit-breaker-shed HTTP messages land in for forensic recovery via `rustgate_core::encrypted::decrypt_file`; it is shared across every per-target sender task as an `Arc<DeadLetterQueue>` and sealed during graceful shutdown. The previous seven inline tests covered the happy-path (lazy writer creation, writer reuse, per-target file separation, seal-on-finish, basic `Debug` redaction, JSON smoke, and a single `../etc/passwd` path-traversal check) but left several load-bearing invariants unpinned: twelve new tests close those gaps. `dead_letter_queue_is_send_sync_and_static` compile-time-asserts the three trait bounds via generic `fn assert<T: Send>()`/`<T: Sync>()`/`<T: 'static>()` helpers — the DLQ lives inside the HTTP publisher's `Arc` and any regression to these bounds would break the publisher build, so pinning them at the DLQ layer gives a crisp failure message at the source of the regression instead of a noisy one at the consumer. `default_dlq_max_bytes_is_exactly_256_mib` pins the documented default cap (`268_435_456` bytes) so a future refactor that bumps the constant without revisiting `docs/http.md` trips here first. `rotation_creates_new_file_once_bytes_written_meets_max_bytes` configures `max_bytes=50`, writes two entries with a 5 ms sleep between them (to guarantee distinct millisecond timestamps in the rotated filename), and asserts at least two `.dlq.enc` files exist on disk — proving the rotation path actually seals the old file and creates a new one rather than silently truncating in place. `rotation_is_scoped_per_target_and_does_not_affect_other_targets` runs two targets ("quiet" and "chatty") against the same small `max_bytes=50` cap: the chatty target rotates, the quiet target writes once and must stay on a single file — this pins the "one-file-per-target-per-rotation-generation" invariant documented in the module header and guards against a regression that collapsed the per-target writer map into a single process-global writer. `dlq_entry_serializes_empty_source_groups_as_empty_json_array` asserts an `Identity` with no groups renders `"source_groups":[]` (not `null`, not omitted) so the offline recovery tool and any SIEM ingest rule keyed off this shape stays stable. `dlq_entry_payload_b64_round_trips_to_original_bytes` verifies `b64::encode` → `b64::decode` is lossless for a non-UTF-8 byte string (`\xde\xad\xbe\xef\x00\x01\x02\xff` and friends) — forensic replay depends on this being a true round-trip, not a truncation or lossy conversion. `dlq_entry_json_contains_all_documented_fields` sweeps over all ten documented field names (`timestamp`, `target`, `reason`, `source_cn`, `source_groups`, `destination`, `message_type`, `source_protocol`, `payload_b64`, `payload_size`) and asserts each appears in the serialized output — any silent field drop or rename trips here. `format_timestamp_now_returns_iso_8601_parseable_output` parses the helper's output through `time::OffsetDateTime::parse` with the `Iso8601::DEFAULT` description and accepts only `"unknown"` as the non-ISO-8601 fallback — this pins the wire format the audit chain and replay tooling correlate against. `path_traversal_defence_covers_broad_attack_surface` pushes fifteen hostile target-name variants (backslash, single dot, double dot alone, space, non-ASCII letter `café`, embedded null, tab, newline, colon, percent, tilde, at-sign, trailing dot, leading dot, middle dot) through `dead_letter` and asserts zero files are created — strengthening the existing `../etc/passwd` test by covering Windows path separators, shell metacharacters, control characters, and Unicode-equivalence vectors. `finish_is_idempotent_across_repeated_calls` calls `finish()` three times in a row and asserts no panic plus a non-empty sealed file, pinning the shutdown-path contract where operator-initiated SIGTERM may re-enter the seal logic. `finish_on_empty_queue_is_a_noop_and_does_not_create_files` exercises the long-running-publisher-that-never-tripped-a-breaker path and asserts the empty-queue
