shutdown creates zero files. `debug_format_uses_finish_non_exhaustive_and_hides_private_fields` strengthens the existing `debug_impl_redacts_internals` test by asserting the `..` sentinel from `finish_non_exhaustive()` appears AND that none of the private field names (`key_ring`, `max_bytes`, `writers`) render in the `Debug` output — a regression that swapped `finish_non_exhaustive()` for `finish()` would silently dump all fields and trip here. Suite grew from 7 → 19 tests; all pass and the full-workspace clippy run with the canonical feature set stays clean. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-federation::ha::registry` `Direction` trait-bound surface and unknown-variant rejection, `ConnectionInfo` forward-compat/fencing-token boundaries, `PodInfo` JSON wire shape, `ConnectionRegistry: Send + Sync`, NATS-KV constant pins, and ≥3× refresh-margin safety invariant** — `crates/rustgate-federation/src/ha/registry.rs` is the cross-pod NATS-KV connection registry that every HA deployment's split-brain detector, claim-fencing logic, and operator REST dashboard depend on. The previous eight inline tests covered `Direction` Display + serde round-trip, `ConnectionInfo` forward/backward compat of the optional `fencing_token`, a manual `PodInfo` construction, and pinned the `CONN_BUCKET` string + `CONN_TTL_SECS > CONN_REFRESH_SECS` ratio. That left several defensive invariants unpinned: twelve new tests close the gap. `direction_implements_copy_clone_partial_eq_debug_send_sync_static` pins the full trait-bound surface on `Direction` (`Copy + Clone + PartialEq + Eq + Debug + Send + Sync + 'static`) via a compile-time generic assert helper — `Copy` is load-bearing because every hot-path comparison in `list_pods` (`if conn.direction == Direction::Outbound`) uses by-value copying instead of references, and `Send + Sync + 'static` are required because `ConnectionInfo` (which owns a `Direction`) is shipped across the `refresh_registrations` background task. `direction_serde_rejects_capital_case_and_unknown_variants` pushes ten hostile wire values (`"Outbound"`, `"OUTBOUND"`, `"Inbound"`, `"INBOUND"`, `"outbound "` with trailing space, `" outbound"` with leading space, `"wizard"`, empty string, `"both"`, `"none"`) through `serde_json::from_str` and asserts each fails — a rolling upgrade where an older peer emitted `"Outbound"` must produce a crisp parse error, never a silent coerce to a default variant. `connection_info_implements_clone_debug_send_sync_static` compile-time-pins the `Clone + Debug + Send + Sync + 'static` bound surface on `ConnectionInfo` — the refresh task's `Arc<Mutex<HashMap<String, ConnectionInfo>>>` share requires every bound, and a regression that removed any of them would break `tokio::spawn` at the consumer call site rather than here. `connection_info_deserialize_tolerates_unknown_fields_from_newer_pods` pins forward-compatibility: a JSON blob from a hypothetical v3 pod with `future_field_added_in_v3` and `another_unknown` must deserialize successfully and preserve the known fields — any regression that added `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` would silently break every cross-version rolling-upgrade deployment. `connection_info_fencing_token_zero_is_serialized_not_omitted` pins that `skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none"` triggers ONLY on `None`, not on `Some(0)` — NATS KV revisions are zero-indexed and a regression that swapped the predicate to `is_none_or(|x| *x == 0)` would drop the first revision of every fencing claim, breaking the split-brain detector on its most critical code path. `connection_info_fencing_token_roundtrips_at_u64_max_boundary` round-trips `fencing_token: Some(u64::MAX)` and `connected_at: u64::MAX` through `serde_json` — NATS KV revisions grow monotonically over the bucket's lifetime, and pinning the boundary guards against any future narrowing refactor (e.g. swapping `u64` for `u32` to save 4 bytes) that would silently truncate large revisions. `pod_info_json_shape_contains_all_documented_fields` asserts all four documented field tokens (`id`, `claimed_peers`, `connections`, `last_heartbeat`) appear in the serialized output — an operator REST dashboard keyed off these exact field names would break silently if a refactor renamed any of them. `pod_info_implements_clone_debug_send_sync_static` compile-pins the `Clone + Debug + Send + Sync + 'static` bound set on `PodInfo` (the pod registry's `list_pods()` returns `Vec<PodInfo>` across the tokio runtime boundary and the REST API layer clones pod snapshots for Prometheus export). `connection_registry_type_implements_send_and_sync` pins `Send + Sync` on `ConnectionRegistry` itself — the registry is held as `Arc<ConnectionRegistry>` inside the federation manager and shared across the gRPC server's async runtime, the inbound-connection-register task, and the refresh loop; all three require both bounds, and removing an internal field that's `Send + Sync` (e.g., swapping `tokio::sync::Mutex` for a non-`Send` primitive) would break the `Arc<Self>` share at compile time elsewhere rather than here. `conn_constants_have_documented_literal_values` pins all three NATS-KV literals (`CONN_BUCKET == "federation-connections"`, `CONN_TTL_SECS == 30`, `CONN_REFRESH_SECS == 10`) so any silent cadence change trips here — especially `CONN_BUCKET` must stay stable because it is the shared distributed-registry key across a heterogeneous pod fleet and a rename would silently split a rolling-upgrade fleet into two isolated registries. `conn_refresh_fires_at_least_three_times_within_ttl_window` strengthens the existing `conn_ttl_exceeds_refresh` test: the refresh cadence must fire at least THREE times within a single TTL window (`30 >= 3 * 10 = 30`) so two consecutive refresh failures (e.g. a transient NATS disconnect) still leave a successful refresh cycle before the TTL drops the entry — any regression that tightened TTL below the 3× safety margin would make a single missed refresh cause entries to go missing across the pod fleet. `conn_refresh_secs_is_strictly_positive` pins the zero-duration floor — `tokio::time::interval(Duration::from_secs(0))` panics (the tokio contract rejects zero-duration intervals), and a regression that set it to zero would crash the background refresh task at `start()`. Suite grew from 8 → 20 tests; all pass under `cargo nextest run -p rustgate-federation --features ha` and the full-workspace clippy run with the canonical feature set stays clean. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport::repeater` `EmergencyRepeater` — Debug+Send+Sync bounds, documented interval/stale-delay constants, `new`/`default` symmetry, `repeat_interval()` boundary round-trip, `extract_uid` strict-double-quote contract, `is_cancel_event` non-UTF-8 safety + strict-`"true"` match, `process()` same-UID replace, missing-UID rejection, and unknown-UID cancel no-op** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/repeater.rs` hosts the emergency-event repeater that re-broadcasts active 911 alerts (CoT `b-a-o-tbl` / `b-a-g` events) to reconnecting clients so situational-awareness continuity survives transient disconnects. Before this PR the inline suite (9 tests: `cache_emergency_event`, `cancel_removes_emergency`, `stale_events_excluded`, `sweep_removes_stale`, `ignores_non_alert_messages`, `extract_uid_works`, `extract_uid_missing`, `is_cancel_detects_cancel`, `is_cancel_normal_event`) covered the sunny-path lifecycle but left eleven contract-shaped invariants unpinned that a refactor could silently break. Eleven new inline tests close those gaps. `repeater_implements_debug_send_sync_static` compile-time-asserts `Debug + Send + Sync + 'static` on `EmergencyRepeater` via `fn assert<T: std::fmt::Debug + Send + Sync + 'static>(_: &T)` — the bounds are load-bearing because `start_repeat_task` wraps the repeater in `Arc<EmergencyRepeater>` and moves clones into a `tokio::spawn` body, so a future refactor that accidentally dropped `Sync` (e.g. by adding an `Rc` field) would break every call site in the server bin but only at link time. `default_repeat_interval_and_stale_delay_have_documented_literal_values` pins both `DEFAULT_REPEAT_INTERVAL == Duration::from_secs(3)` and `DEFAULT_STALE_DELAY == Duration::from_secs(300)` — the doc comment advertises "every 3 seconds" and "5 minutes" as stable behaviour, and operators tune their emergency-event tooling against these literals, so a silent bump to 10s / 600s would shift the latency budget every alerting rule depends on. `new_and_default_produce_observably_identical_repeaters` drives both constructors and asserts `repeat_interval()` matches on both results, pinning the `impl Default` → `Self::new()` delegation so a future divergence (e.g. `Default::default()` returning `Duration::ZERO`) fails here rather than at the first deployed server that never repeats. `repeat_interval_accessor_round_trips_across_boundary_values` drives `EmergencyRepeater::new(interval)` at `Duration::from_millis(1)` (a pathologically aggressive re-broadcast cadence an operator might set during stress tests) and `Duration::from_secs(u64::MAX)` (the arithmetic-boundary a config-parser bug could feed in), asserting the getter round-trips unchanged — the accessor is a `pub fn` that `start_repeat_task` reads on every tick, so a silent narrowing to `u32` inside the struct would fail here before deploy. `extract_uid_rejects_single_quoted_attribute_value` feeds `<event uid='abc'>` into the regex-free byte scanner and asserts `None` — the CoT wire contract mandates double-quoted attribute values, and every real iTAK/ATAK/WinTAK client emits double quotes, so accepting single quotes would open a small attack surface where a malformed or hand-crafted event could parse under rustgate while being rejected by every downstream consumer (splitting the situational-awareness picture). `extract_uid_returns_none_for_event_tag_without_attributes` feeds `<event>` (with no attributes at all) and asserts `None` — pins that the scanner does not silently fall through to a default UID when the attribute is absent, preventing a silent cache pollution where every no-UID event would share a single bucket in the `HashMap<String, Emergency>`. `is_cancel_returns_false_gracefully_for_non_utf8_payload` feeds `[0xff, 0xfe, 0xfd, 0x00, 0x80]` (a deliberately non-UTF-8 byte sequence that `std::str::from_utf8` rejects) and asserts `false` — the payload parser must stay panic-free on hostile wire input, and a regression that unwrapped the `Result<&str, Utf8Error>` would crash every router task on the first malformed payload, taking down emergency-event handling for every connected client. `is_cancel_requires_exact_true_literal_value` drives three near-miss variants (`cancel="false"`, `cancel="TRUE"`, `cancel="1"`) through `is_cancel_event` and asserts all three return `false` — pins the strict-literal match contract that prevents silent emergency-cancel on any casing or truthy-numeric variant a future XML-serializer change could introduce; the CoT spec is explicit that only `cancel="true"` signals cancellation, and operators rely on this to avoid accidental emergency dismissals. `process_replaces_existing_cached_emergency_for_same_uid` is the strongest test: constructs two emergency events with the same UID (`EM-123`) but different payloads (second carries `lat="42"`), processes both in sequence, then asserts the cached `active` entry for `EM-123` contains the second payload's `lat="42"` bytes — pins `HashMap::insert`'s replace-semantics contract so a future refactor that accidentally used `entry().or_insert()` (first-wins) would cause every re-broadcast to carry the stale initial payload, masking the victim's updated coordinates during an active emergency. `process_returns_false_for_alert_with_missing_uid_attribute` feeds an emergency-typed event with no `uid` attribute and asserts `process()` returns `false` — pins the `Option<String>::is_none()` early-return so malformed events are rejected at admission rather than silently polluting the cache with a synthesised or empty UID. `cancel_of_unknown_uid_is_a_silent_no_op` processes a cancel event for a UID that was never stored and asserts the `active` cache remains empty and `process()` returns `false` — pins the `HashMap::remove` safely-returns-None-on-missing contract so a cancel-without-preceding-alert path (which happens in practice when a client reconnects mid-emergency and its tooling emits a defensive cancel on startup) does not panic or log spurious warnings. Suite grows from 9 to 20 tests; zero changes to any production API. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-http::resolver` SSRF-safe `Resolve` impl — `Arc<dyn Resolve>` shareability, `Resolving` future `Send` bound, concurrent-resolve safety, deterministic rejection-message format, escape-hatch error-kind classification, and log-forging defence** — `crates/rustgate-http/src/resolver.rs` is the DNS-rebinding defence boundary between config-time URL validation (which cannot know what a hostname will resolve to at request time) and outbound HTTP traffic: every per-target `reqwest::Client` in the `rustgate-http` egress bridge is built with `ClientBuilder::dns_resolver(Arc::new(SsrfSafeResolver::new(allow_private)))`, so this resolver is the single choke point that catches an attacker-controlled hostname that initially resolves to a public IP (passing config validation) but later returns `127.0.0.1` / `169.254.169.254` / a private-RFC-1918 address on the actual HTTP call. Before this PR the inline suite (7 tests) covered the sunny-path rejection, the escape-hatch happy path, the `PermissionDenied` ErrorKind mapping, and the DNS-failure propagation contract, but left six contract-shaped invariants unpinned that a refactor could silently break. Six new inline tests close those gaps. `resolver_is_shareable_as_arc_dyn_resolve` compile-time-asserts that `Arc<dyn Resolve>` constructs around `SsrfSafeResolver` via `fn assert_resolve_trait_object<T: Resolve + ?Sized>(_: &Arc<T>)` — the `Arc<dyn Resolve>` shape specifically matches how reqwest's `ClientBuilder::dns_resolver` consumes the type, and a future refactor that accidentally added a non-`Send`/`Sync` field (an `Rc`, a `RefCell`, a `*const T`) would fail here rather than at the transitive reqwest call site deep inside the egress hot path. `resolving_future_is_send_across_await_points` pins the `Resolving` return type (reqwest's `Pin<Box<dyn Future + Send>>` alias) — the future is driven from a multi-threaded executor and a regression that held a non-Send local across the `.await` inside the `Box::pin(async move { … })` body would silently work on a current-thread test runtime while breaking integration tests that happened to run on a `multi_thread` tokio runtime; the test additionally drives the future through `tokio::spawn` to pair the compile-time bound with a runtime observation. `concurrent_resolves_on_shared_arc_do_not_interfere` spawns two tokio tasks resolving `localhost` through the same shared `Arc<SsrfSafeResolver>` and asserts both complete without panic and yield a loopback address — the struct currently holds only a `bool` so there is no lock to contend on, but a future refactor that added a mutable cache or an atomic counter field could silently regress this, and reqwest hits the resolver concurrently for every in-flight target under load. `resolve_rejection_error_message_is_deterministic_across_calls` pins the byte-identical rejection-message contract operators' log-grep and SIEM ingest rules key off — a regression that introduced non-deterministic field ordering (HashMap iteration leaking into the error string) or an incident-unique identifier (a UUID or a timestamp) would silently break those rules without tripping any existing test. `resolve_escape_hatch_does_not_emit_permission_denied_on_dns_failure` pins the classifier boundary between SSRF rejection (`PermissionDenied`) and DNS failure (any other ErrorKind) from the escape-hatch side — the sibling `resolve_escape_hatch_does_not_filter_public_hostnames` test covers the message content, this one pins the ErrorKind boundary, and together they cover both sides of the retry-and-circuit-breaker classification contract. `resolve_rejection_error_message_contains_no_control_characters` is the log-forging defence: the rejection message is grep'd into operator dashboards and passes through structured logging subscribers, embedded CR/LF would split log lines and open a log-forging surface, so the test drives the rejection path and asserts no character in the `Display` output satisfies `char::is_control()`. Suite grows from 7 to 13 tests; zero changes to any production API. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport::filter` `MessageFilter`/`GeoBounds` trait bounds, `new`/`default` symmetry, metadata fallback path, catch-all rejection of unmapped `MessageType` variants, geo-bounds corner inclusivity + NaN safety, non-UTF-8 payload defence, and `extract_point` / `extract_attr` parsing-failure contracts** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/filter.rs` is the message-admission layer the router applies before delivery: every group-send, direct-send, and broadcast-send passes through `MessageFilter::matches` which combines a type-prefix allow-list (against the CoT `type=` attribute or a `MessageType` metadata fallback) with an optional geographic bounding box (against the CoT `<point lat= lon= />` attributes). Before this PR the inline suite (12 tests) covered the happy-path prefix matching, geo-containment sunny path, and the public XML-extraction helpers' success shapes, but left eleven contract-shaped invariants unpinned, each of which could silently regress without tripping a live-router integration test. Eleven new inline tests close the gaps: `message_filter_and_geo_bounds_implement_debug_clone_send_sync_static` uses a compile-time helper `fn bounds<T: Debug + Clone + Send + Sync + 'static>()` parameterised on both public types so a refactor that added a non-`Send` field (`Rc`, `RefCell`, raw pointer) to either struct trips at test-compile time rather than at the first transitive send boundary inside the router; `new_and_default_produce_observably_identical_filters` drives both constructors through `matches` against the same message, asserts output agreement, and additionally asserts `format!("{a:?}") == format!("{b:?}")` — pins the two-way equivalence the module doc advertises so a silent drift where `new()` started with a non-empty `type_prefixes` allow-list would fail here rather than at every `new()`-built router in production; `matches_type_fallback_routes_through_message_type_for_non_xml_payload` exercises the currently-untested fallback branch that fires when `extract_cot_type` returns `None` (non-XML payload) but the metadata carries a mapped `MessageType` — builds a `MessageType::Position` message with a raw binary payload and a type-prefix filter for `a-` and pins that the filter admits the message via the metadata fallback path rather than silently dropping every protobuf/binary position at the filter stage; `matches_message_type_rejects_file_and_other_variants_via_catchall` covers the `_ => false` arm of the `matches_message_type` dispatch table by driving both `MessageType::File` and `MessageType::Other("mystery")` through a filter with a broad `a-` prefix and asserting both are rejected — a regression that flipped the catch-all to `_ => true` would silently admit every file-transfer and unknown-kind message past every configured type filter; `geo_bounds_contains_is_inclusive_at_all_four_corners` pins the `>=` / `<=` semantics at every corner (SW, SE, NW, NE) plus the centre so a future refactor that flipped any edge to strict `>` / `<` would silently narrow every operator-configured bounding box by the floating-point ULP at its boundary, dropping traffic exactly at the configured latitude/longitude limits; `geo_bounds_contains_rejects_nan_coordinates` feeds NaN lat, NaN lon, and NaN/NaN — pins the IEEE 754 rejection path at the filter surface so a future migration to `total_cmp`-based ordering (which *does* place NaN in the sort order) does not silently admit NaN points; `extract_cot_type_returns_none_for_non_utf8_payload` feeds a hostile byte sequence `[0xff, 0xfe, 0xfd, 0x00, 0x80]` through `std::str::from_utf8(payload).ok()?` and asserts `None` — a regression that unwrapped the `Result<&str, _>` would panic every tokio worker handling a malformed wire payload, taking down the transport for every connected client; `extract_point_returns_none_when_lat_is_non_numeric` pins the `parse::<f64>().ok()?` contract inside `extract_attr` (feeds `lat="not-a-number"` and asserts the whole extraction returns `None`) so a regression that silently coerced to `0.0` would bypass every configured bounding box filter on malformed payloads; `extract_point_returns_none_when_lon_is_missing` pins the partial-point rejection (`lat` present, `lon` missing) so a regression that defaulted the missing coordinate to `0.0` would let partial points through the geo filter on `(lat, 0.0)`; `extract_attr_does_not_confuse_prefix_attributes` pins the `="` suffix disambiguation inside the attribute scanner — feeds `<point latitude="50" />` and asserts `extract_attr(..., "lat")` returns `None` (does not match the `lat` prefix of `latitude`) and separately asserts `<point lat="35.5" />` still returns `Some(35.5)`, so a regression that dropped the suffix check would silently extract `50` for `lat` from `latitude="50"` every time a CoT payload used the unofficial `latitude`/`longitude` spelling; `empty_type_prefixes_vec_is_equivalent_to_no_filter` pins the permissive-sentinel contract — `MessageFilter::new().with_type_prefixes(vec![])` must admit every message (the empty-list case falls through the `if self.type_prefixes.is_empty() { return true; }` guard) so a regression that silently flipped "empty list means reject all" would break every operator who configured an empty prefix list as a sentinel for "type filtering disabled". Suite grows from 12 to 23 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives the module's public/crate-private surface through its existing constructors and helpers. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-mqtt::listener` rate-limit constants, IPv6 bind path, Debug redaction, `Send`/`Sync` bounds, `MqttPreSession` field visibility, and the `accept → Ok(None)` quiet-failure contract** — `crates/rustgate-mqtt/src/listener.rs` is the TLS accept loop every MQTT client flows through; it owns the per-IP slow-handshake-DoS mitigation (`CONN_RATE_LIMIT`, `CONN_RATE_WINDOW`) and the TLS handshake timeout branch that must return `Ok(None)` rather than a hard error when a client misbehaves, because the server-side accept loop calls `listener.accept().await?` inside a `loop` and a swap from `Ok(None)` to `Err(_)` would propagate up and take the entire MQTT listener down on the first rude client. Before this PR the inline suite (10 tests) covered the happy-path bind, `local_addr`, SO_REUSEADDR release-on-drop, EADDRINUSE propagation, and the operator-facing Debug field shape, but left a material set of security-critical and refactor-sensitive invariants unpinned. Nine new inline tests close the gap: `conn_rate_limit_constant_matches_documented_value` and `conn_rate_window_constant_matches_documented_value` pin the private `const CONN_RATE_LIMIT: usize = 10` and `const CONN_RATE_WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(10)` values the threat-model D8 guidance and ops runbooks quote — a silent bump of either (a reviewer typing `CONN_RATE_LIMIT = 100` thinking they were loosening a dev tunable) is a deliberate policy change that must land on review, not in a passing green build; `bind_supports_ipv6_loopback_address_family` drives the `socket2::Domain::for_address(addr)` v6 branch by binding `[::1]:0` and asserts `local_addr().is_ipv6() && ip().is_loopback()` — rustgate-mqtt is deployed on dual-stack hosts and behind v6-aware load balancers, so the v6 socket-domain path must exercise cleanly (bind alone does not drive TLS so the v4-SAN test CA is sufficient); `debug_impl_does_not_leak_tls_material` pins a defensive absence assertion against `"TlsAcceptor"`, `"ServerConfig"`, and `"-----BEGIN"` in the rendered Debug output — the current `impl Debug` only prints `local_addr` and `conn_rate`, but a well-meaning `#[derive(Debug)]` on a future field or a third-party `Debug` on `TlsAcceptor` could regress that silently and leak server-config internals to log sinks that aggregate to a less-privileged collector; `listener_type_is_send_and_sync` and `pre_session_type_is_send` compile-time-assert the `Send + Sync` bound on `MqttListener` (it is moved into `tokio::spawn` futures and shared across the accept/drain boundary) and the `Send` bound on `MqttPreSession` (it crosses the accept/drain boundary into a per-session task), via zero-runtime-cost `fn assert_send_sync<T: Send + Sync>() {}` and `fn assert_send<T: Send>() {}` helpers that fail at test-compile time if any field regresses onto a non-`Send` type (`Rc`, `RefCell`, raw pointers); `pre_session_fields_are_destructurable_from_within_crate` pins that `MqttPreSession { identity, stream, peer_addr }` destructure syntax compiles — the session module takes ownership of each field by destructure, so regressing any field to private or renaming one would break the consumer without a clear error surface here (the closure is coerced to `fn(MqttPreSession) -> (Arc<Identity>, TlsStream, SocketAddr)` so the compiler type-checks it without needing a real TLS stream); `pre_session_identity_is_arc_wrapped_for_shared_ownership` pins the identity field to `Arc<Identity>` specifically (not `Box<Identity>` or raw `Identity`) — the session task and the router both hold references to the identity, so anything less than `Arc` would force a deep clone on the hot path and silently regress connect latency; `accept_returns_ok_none_after_client_drops_before_tls_handshake` is the tokio-test that drives the quiet-failure contract end-to-end by spawning the accept future, then completing a real kernel TCP handshake from the test thread and immediately dropping the TCP stream before writing any ClientHello bytes — the server-side `acceptor.accept(...)` observes EOF and returns an `io::Error`, which the accept loop at the `Ok(Err(e))` arm (line 130) converts to `Ok(None)`; the match arms explicitly panic on both `Ok(Some(_))` and `Err(_)` with distinct messages so a future refactor that accidentally upgraded the TLS-error branch to a hard error (taking down the listener on the first rude client) or silently returned a half-initialized `MqttPreSession` would fail with a specific diagnostic rather than an opaque assertion. Suite grows from 10 to 19 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors, methods, and private constants through their documented contracts. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-federation::replay_persist` — `DEFAULT_REPLAY_MAX_BYTES == 64 MiB` and `REDB_PER_RECORD_OVERHEAD == 32` literal-byte pins, `REPLAY_TABLE` `"federation_replay_v2"` schema-version name (via the `redb::TableHandle::name()` accessor, so a silent bump back to `v1` or a typo in the new-version string fails at this test rather than at a field-operator restart), `encode_key`/`decode_key` wire-format round-trips (typical peer+seq, empty peer, peer with an internal `\x00` byte — which must survive because the decoder splits by position `raw.len() - 9`, not by first-NUL-scan — and `u64::MAX` sequence), `decode_key` rejection paths (input shorter than nine bytes, wrong separator byte at the split position, non-UTF-8 in the peer segment), `encode_value`/`decode_value` wire-format round-trips (typical payload, empty-groups-and-payload, `ttl_ms: Some(0)` round-trips as `None` per the documented `0 = no TTL` sentinel convention, many-groups), `decode_value` rejection paths (header shorter than twenty bytes, group length declaring more bytes than remain, missing group-length `u32` field, non-UTF-8 in a group name), `accounted_size` per-record byte-accounting contract (`key_len + val_len + REDB_PER_RECORD_OVERHEAD` with saturating arithmetic on `u64::MAX` inputs rather than silent wrap), `ReplayStore::open` directory auto-create (`fs::create_dir_all` semantics — calling `open` against a path whose parent does not yet exist must succeed, which is the operational contract for the `--federation-replay-dir` flag against a first-boot data volume), `ReplayStore::put`/`delete`/`clear_peer` mutation invariants (`len` and `total_bytes` stay consistent across mutations, delete of a non-existent `(peer, seq)` is a no-op not an error, `clear_peer` on a peer with no entries is a no-op, deleting every entry returns the store to empty state), `ReplayStore::clone` shared-inner-state contract (cloning the handle shares the `Arc<Mutex<Inner>>` so a `put` through the clone is visible to `len()` on the original), `load_all` ordering invariant (entries returned sorted first by `peer_id` then by `seq` within each peer — the consumer relies on this to feed peer-local ring buffers in their original sequence), empty-payload round-trip across a full shutdown/reopen cycle, `byte_cap == 0` pathological-case behaviour (every `put` is immediately evicted; the store never grows), `ReplayStoreError`/`RestoredEntry`/`ReplayStore` `Debug`+`Clone`+`Error`-trait surface compile-time pins, and rehydrate-on-open tolerance for entries written directly through the raw `redb` API bypassing the codec (malformed key bytes and malformed value bytes are silently skipped rather than propagated as an open-time error — the operational contract is "a corrupted single entry must not prevent the server from starting", which matters because the replay database sits next to the process during a crash and can in principle be torn at any byte boundary)** — `crates/rustgate-federation/src/replay_persist.rs` is the on-disk persistence layer for the per-peer federation replay buffer: the in-memory `ReplayBuffer` is bounded and lost on restart, yet operationally a server restart very often coincides with a peer reconnect, which is exactly the moment the buffered messages become necessary, so every enqueue is mirrored to a `redb` table keyed on `(peer_id, seq)` with a value blob carrying `enqueued_at_unix_ms`, `ttl_ms`, `groups`, and the raw payload. The key encoder appends a single `\x00` separator after the peer string and an 8-byte big-endian `seq`, and the decoder splits on position (last 9 bytes) rather than on the first NUL, so peer ids containing embedded NULs round-trip correctly — that split-by-position invariant is what the new `encode_decode_key_preserves_internal_nul_in_peer_id` test guards. The value encoder writes `u64 enqueued_at_unix_ms | u64 ttl_ms | u32 groups_len | [u32 group_len, group_bytes]* | payload`, and `ttl_ms == 0` is the documented sentinel for "no TTL" (the Rust API takes `Option<u64>`; the `Some(0)` → `None` round-trip is explicitly guaranteed). The `REPLAY_TABLE` constant names the table `"federation_replay_v2"` — the `v2` suffix is a schema-version boundary chosen when the value blob gained its `enqueued_at_unix_ms`/`ttl_ms` prefix (the #557 follow-up); a legacy `v1` database opens cleanly with an empty `v2` table, meaning older entries are effectively dropped on first boot of a new binary. Before this PR the inline suite (seven tests: `round_trip_persist_and_load`, `clear_peer_removes_only_that_peer`, `delete_single_entry`, `byte_cap_evicts_oldest_by_enqueue_timestamp`, `byte_cap_evicts_globally_oldest_across_peers`, `replay_buffer_bytes_gauge_tracks_mutations`, `rehydrated_store_preserves_global_oldest_ordering`) covered happy-path persist+reload and eviction ordering but left every codec-level wire-format invariant, every constant pin, every `decode_*` rejection path, the entire `accounted_size` saturation contract, `Clone` sharing semantics, the `byte_cap == 0` pathological case, every trait-surface bound on the public types, and — most important — the silent-skip-on-corruption rehydrate path all unpinned. Thirty-five new inline tests close those gaps. Constant pins (three): `default_replay_max_bytes_is_sixty_four_mib` asserts the `DEFAULT_REPLAY_MAX_BYTES` literal equals `64 * 1024 * 1024` so a well-intentioned "bump default to 128 MiB for gRPC-heavy deployments" edit has to update both the constant and a visible test, `redb_per_record_overhead_constant_is_pinned_to_32` asserts `REDB_PER_RECORD_OVERHEAD == 32` and documents inline that a regression that dropped the constant would produce on-disk footprints exceeding the operator's `--federation-replay-max-bytes` budget (the whole point of adding the overhead estimate instead of paying for `Database::stats()` on every put), and `replay_table_name_is_v2` pulls the `TableHandle` trait into scope and asserts `REPLAY_TABLE.name() == "federation_replay_v2"` — a schema-version regression to `v1` or a typo in a future `v3` name would fail here rather than silently create a second empty table and make every pre-upgrade entry unreadable. Key codec (seven): `encode_decode_key_round_trip_typical` drives `decode_key(encode_key("peer-a", 42))` and asserts the exact `(peer, seq)` tuple comes back; `encode_decode_key_round_trip_empty_peer` exercises the `peer_id.len() == 0` edge case (which reduces the encoded output to exactly nine bytes: one NUL + eight big-endian sequence bytes); `encode_decode_key_preserves_internal_nul_in_peer_id` encodes a peer id containing an embedded `\x00` byte and asserts decode returns the original peer string byte-for-byte — a regression that switched the decoder from position-based splitting to first-NUL scanning would split the peer segment at the internal NUL and return garbage; `encode_decode_key_round_trip_u64_max_seq` pins `seq == u64::MAX` so the big-endian encoder's top byte stays `0xff`; and three `decode_key_rejects_*` tests walk the three rejection arms (input shorter than nine bytes → `None`, wrong separator byte at `raw[len - 9]` → `None`, invalid UTF-8 in the peer segment → `None`) so a regression that collapsed any of those to a panic or to a best-effort lossy decode fails at a named test rather than at a field operator whose replay database has been hand-edited. Value codec (eight): `encode_decode_value_round_trip_typical` drives a full-shape payload (non-zero `enqueued_at_unix_ms`, `ttl_ms: Some(30_000)`, two groups, non-empty payload bytes) through the codec; `encode_decode_value_round_trip_empty_groups_and_payload` pins the zero-length arms of the encoder; `ttl_some_zero_round_trips_as_none_by_sentinel_convention` is the load-bearing contract test — the module's docstring explicitly states `ttl_ms == 0` on disk decodes as `None` because the in-memory API is `Option<u64>`, so a regression that encoded `Some(0)` as literal zero and then decoded it back to `Some(0)` (rather than `None`) would silently flip the TTL-on-rehydrate semantics from "no expiry" to "already expired"; `encode_decode_value_many_groups_round_trip` exercises the `groups_len` outer loop against a non-trivial groups vector; and four `decode_value_rejects_*` tests walk the rejection arms (header shorter than twenty bytes = `u64 + u64 + u32`, declared group-length exceeding the remaining buffer, missing group-length `u32` field, non-UTF-8 bytes in a declared group name) — each pins a corruption class that a bit-flip in the on-disk database could in principle produce, and each must return `None` rather than panic. Accounted size (two): `accounted_size_sums_lengths_with_overhead` pins the arithmetic contract (`u64::from(key.len()) + u64::from(val.len()) + REDB_PER_RECORD_OVERHEAD`) at a typical input, and `accounted_size_saturates_instead_of_wrapping` drives `usize::MAX` for both lengths and asserts the result is `u64::MAX` — a regression that switched from `saturating_add` to unchecked `+` would silently wrap on the first payload that saturated and would report a much smaller "accounted" size than the real on-disk footprint, defeating the whole point of the byte-cap eviction loop. Store lifecycle (seven): `open_creates_directory_if_missing` asserts the `fs::create_dir_all` contract against a nested path whose parent does not yet exist; `fresh_store_reports_empty_state` pins `len() == 0` and `total_bytes() == 0` on a newly-opened store; `put_increments_len_and_total_bytes_consistently` asserts the two accessors stay in lockstep across a `put`; `delete_nonexistent_key_is_a_noop_not_an_error` pins the "idempotent delete" contract that the peer-draining path relies on; `clear_peer_with_no_entries_is_a_noop` mirrors the same contract for `clear_peer`; `delete_every_entry_returns_store_to_empty` walks the full mutation cycle and asserts both accessors return zero afterwards; and `clone_shares_inner_state_with_original` binds the `Arc<Mutex<Inner>>` sharing contract (the handle is cheaply cloned into every federation peer task, so a `put` through one clone must be visible to `len()` on another — a regression that `Clone`d the inner by value would silently produce two diverging stores that report independent counts). Full-cycle (three): `load_all_returns_entries_sorted_by_peer_then_seq` fills the store with interleaved `(peer, seq)` inserts and asserts `load_all` returns them in the documented lexicographic-by-peer, numeric-by-seq order the consumer feeds into per-peer ring buffers; `put_preserves_empty_payload_across_reload` writes a zero-length `Bytes` payload, drops the store, re-opens it, and asserts the rehydrated entry carries the same empty payload (a regression that treated empty-payload as "skip the `put`" would silently lose every control-plane message whose payload happened to be empty); `byte_cap_zero_drops_every_write` opens a store with `max_bytes == 0`, issues a `put`, and asserts the store stays empty (`len() == 0`), pinning the edge case where eviction must kick in on the very first insert. Surface (three): `replay_store_error_is_debug_and_display_and_error` uses a compile-time bound helper to assert `ReplayStoreError: Debug + Display + std::error::Error` so `?`-propagation into `anyhow::Result` at call sites compiles; `restored_entry_is_clone_and_debug` pins the two derives; and `replay_store_is_debug` asserts `ReplayStore: Debug` so the standard `#[derive(Debug)]` on every enclosing `FederationManager` / `V2Client` struct compiles forever. Rehydrate tolerance (two): `rehydrate_skips_malformed_keys_written_directly_to_redb` opens a raw `redb::Database`, writes a junk key that will fail `decode_key` (too short), closes, re-opens through `ReplayStore::open`, and asserts the open succeeds and `len()` reports zero entries — pinning the "one corrupted row must not prevent startup" operational contract; `rehydrate_skips_malformed_values_written_directly_to_redb` does the same for a well-formed key paired with a truncated value blob. Suite grows from 7 to 42 tests under `--features api` (41 without, since the pre-existing `replay_buffer_bytes_gauge_tracks_mutations` test is api-feature-gated); zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors, methods, free functions, and trait bounds through their documented contracts. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-authz::error` — `AuthzError: std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static` trait-bound pins, `From<serde_json::Error>` conversion-trait existence, `Error::source()` asymmetry (`Json` chains to the wrapped serde error, `Validation` returns `None`), `Json` variant Display-delegates-to-wrapped-serde template contract (`{0}` positional forwarder not `{0:?}`), `Validation`/`Json` variant `Debug` render includes variant tag + field values, `Validation` Display empty-message tolerance, and `validation(stage, message)` constructor's `impl Into<String>` accepting both `&str` literals and owned `String` inputs** — `crates/rustgate-authz/src/error.rs` is the small (two-variant) error enum every authz-crate call site propagates via `?` into `anyhow::Result` at the router + reloader + startup boundaries. Before this PR the inline suite (8 tests) covered the `validation` constructor's field-setting contract, `impl Into<String>` against an owned `String`, the `Json`/`Validation` Display prefixes, `Send + Sync` via a compile-time bound helper, the `#[from] serde_json::Error` conversion path via `.into()`, and the `stage: &'static str` field-type pin — but it left four load-bearing contract surfaces unpinned that a refactor could silently break. The one that matters most is the `std::error::Error::source()` asymmetry: the `Json` variant is reached via `#[from] serde_json::Error` which the `#[derive(thiserror::Error)]` macro translates into a `source()` impl that returns `Some(&serde_json::Error)`; the `Validation` variant has no source because its `message` is self-contained; a refactor that either added a `#[source]` attribute to the `Validation.message` field (silently inventing a cause chain where there is none) or removed `#[from]` from `Json` in favour of a manual `From` impl that forgot to wire the source (silently breaking the downstream `anyhow`/`eyre` cause-chain unwind) would change observable error-reporting behaviour without tripping any existing test. Ten new inline tests close those gaps. `authz_error_implements_std_error_trait` uses a `fn assert_error_trait<T: std::error::Error>()` compile-time helper to pin the trait-bound that the `?`-propagation chain into `anyhow::Error::from` relies on — a regression that switched `#[derive(thiserror::Error)]` for a hand-rolled Display-only impl would compile in-crate but fail here and at the first `anyhow::Error::from(authz_err)` call site; `authz_error_is_static_lifetime_bound` uses `fn assert_static<T: 'static>()` to pin that both variants are `'static` (trivially so today — `&'static str` + `String` on `Validation`, owned `serde_json::Error` on `Json` — but a future `&'a str` message field would silently narrow the bound and fail here rather than at the first reloader-side `Arc::new(err)` spawn); `authz_error_from_impl_is_present_for_serde_json_error` uses `fn assert_from<T, U: From<T>>()` to pin the conversion trait a refactor that dropped `#[from]` would remove; `json_variant_source_chains_to_underlying_serde_error` triggers a known-shape parse failure, wraps it via `.into()`, and asserts `err.source().is_some()` — the `use std::error::Error;` scoping makes the intent unambiguous and the assertion fails with a named message if a future refactor broke the source link; `validation_variant_has_no_source` is the sibling asymmetry test (`source().is_none()` on the `Validation` variant), pinning the documented "no cause chain" contract against a silent `#[source]` addition to one of the fields; `validation_debug_exposes_both_field_values` formats the variant via `{:?}` and asserts the rendered output contains the variant tag (`Validation`), the stage string (`rules`), and the message text (`dup`) — a manual `Debug` impl that dropped one field (a misguided "trim noise" refactor) would hide half the information from incident logs, and the test catches the drop; `json_variant_debug_names_variant` is the symmetric test for the other variant — asserts `Debug` output names `Json` so a manual impl that collapsed both variants into a single render would silently make incident logs ambiguous between "bad JSON" and "bad rule"; `json_variant_display_delegates_to_wrapped_serde_error` is the template-shape test — the `#[error("invalid policy JSON: {0}")]` attribute uses `{0}` (positional, which delegates to the wrapped type's `Display` impl) rather than `{0:?}` (which would delegate to `Debug` and emit quoted/escaped text that breaks operator grep patterns keyed on specific serde messages); the test triggers a serde error, captures its own Display output, wraps into `AuthzError`, and asserts the outer Display contains the inner Display verbatim — a refactor to `{0:?}` would fail here rather than at a deployed operator whose alerts stop firing; `validation_display_tolerates_empty_message` pins the edge case where `parser::validate` builds the message via `format!` with an empty interior component (e.g. empty rule-id list) — the Display template must still render the canonical prefix and the stage name without panicking or dropping segments; and `validation_constructor_accepts_str_slice_input` is the sibling of the existing owned-String test, pinning that the `impl Into<String>` bound accepts bare `&str` literals (which several parser.rs call sites depend on) so a regression that narrowed the signature to `String`-only would force clone-site changes in the parser and be caught here. Suite grows from 8 to 18 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors, the `Error`/`From`/`Debug`/`Display` trait bounds, and the public enum surface through their documented contracts. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-http::payload` — exact-string content-type pins (`application/json`, `application/xml`), `HttpWireMessage` snake_case field-name wire contract (all nine keys: `source_cn`, `source_groups`, `destination`, `message_type`, `source_protocol`, `received_at`, `payload`, `payload_size`, `instance_id`), `payload_size` is raw-byte-length not base64-expanded length, empty-payload round-trips as `payload_size: 0` + `payload: ""`, `instance_id` passthrough preserves hyphens + alphanumerics, `format_destination` multicast uses bare `,` separator with no whitespace, multicast empty-group-vector yields bare `"multicast:"` prefix, `Group("")` yields bare `"group:"` prefix (no default synthesis), RawXml passthrough preserves non-UTF-8 binary bytes byte-for-byte, and `source_protocol == "federation"` survives verbatim into the JSON envelope** — `crates/rustgate-http/src/payload.rs` is the HTTP-forward serializer the webhook egress bridge and the generic `http://` target type rely on to shape every outbound request: it wraps every `RoutableMessage` into either a JSON envelope (via the internal `HttpWireMessage` struct), a raw XML passthrough, or a protobuf-encoded CoT event, and returns the `(body, content_type)` tuple the HTTP publisher hands straight to `hyper::Request::builder`. Before this PR the inline suite (10 tests) covered the JSON serialisation round-trip with field-value assertions, groups-array shape, ISO-8601 timestamp fragment, raw XML passthrough, all four `format_destination` Display shapes (group, direct, broadcast, multicast), a protobuf-without-feature error path, and a base64 round-trip on binary payloads — but it left a material set of on-the-wire defensive invariants unpinned that a silent refactor could break without any downstream integration test noticing. The one that matters most is the `HttpWireMessage` snake_case field-name contract: the struct uses `#[derive(Serialize)]` with no `#[serde(rename_all = …)]` attribute, so the JSON keys are the Rust field names verbatim, and every deployed downstream consumer (Grafana, SIEM ingest, third-party webhook receivers) decodes against those exact keys. A refactor that added `rename_all = "camelCase"` to the struct would silently produce `sourceCn`, `messageType`, `sourceProtocol`, etc., and break every consumer without any compile error or test failure. Ten new inline tests close the gaps. Content-type pins (two): `json_content_type_is_application_json_exact_string` and `raw_xml_content_type_is_application_xml_exact_string` pin the exact literal content-type strings the `serialize` dispatcher returns — a "normalise" refactor that swapped to `application/json; charset=utf-8` (technically valid under RFC 8259) or `text/xml` (the legacy form) would silently change every deployed consumer's content-negotiation outcome and would fail at these named tests. Field-name wire contract (one): `json_wire_message_field_names_are_snake_case_contract` iterates over all nine field names and asserts each key is present in the serialised JSON object — a regression that added `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]` would fail here for every one of the nine keys rather than at the first deployed consumer's JSON decoder, and the failure message names the offending field list explicitly. Payload-size contract (one): `payload_size_is_raw_byte_length_not_base64_expanded_length` drives a 10-byte payload and asserts `payload_size == 10` while also asserting the base64-encoded `payload` string is longer than 10 chars (the base64 expansion ratio guarantees ~33% growth so 10 bytes → ~16 chars), so the "raw" vs "expanded" distinction is unambiguous — a refactor that used `payload_b64.len()` (which looks defensible because the field sits next to `payload: String`) would inflate the billing / quota / protocol-frame-size field by ~33% and silently break downstream rate-limiting decisions. Empty-payload contract (one): `empty_payload_roundtrips_with_zero_size_and_empty_base64_field` pins that a zero-length payload serialises as `payload_size: 0` + `payload: ""` (empty string, not `null`, not a missing field) — a regression that short-circuited empty payloads to `null` would silently break every downstream consumer whose JSON schema declared `payload: string`; a regression that added `#[serde(skip_serializing_if = "String::is_empty")]` to the `payload` field would silently omit the key entirely and produce the same breakage. Instance-id contract (one): `instance_id_passthrough_preserves_hyphen_and_alphanumeric_characters` drives an id with the representative shape `pod-07-a1b2c3` (k8s pod-name style) and asserts the field renders byte-for-byte into the JSON envelope — a sanitiser / url-encoder added to the pipeline "for safety" would silently break operator dashboards keyed on exact pod identifiers. Destination-format contracts (three): `format_destination_multicast_three_groups_uses_comma_separator` pins the literal `,` separator (a regression to `";"`, `" "`, or URL-encoded `"%2C"` would silently break every downstream parser splitting on `","` to recover the group list); `format_destination_multicast_empty_group_vector_yields_bare_prefix` pins `"multicast:"` render against an empty `vec![]` input (a naive `groups[0]`-style implementation would panic on empty; the current iter-based implementation does not, and this test pins the contract); `format_destination_group_with_empty_name_yields_bare_prefix` pins `"group:"` on an empty `Group(String::new())` input so a regression that synthesised a default group name on empty input (which would silently route orphaned messages into a real group) fails at a named test. Binary-passthrough contract (one): `raw_xml_passthrough_preserves_binary_bytes_byte_for_byte` drives a deliberately-non-UTF-8 payload (`vec![0xFF, 0xFE, 0x00, 0x80, 0x81, 0xC0]`) and asserts the RawXml branch returns the bytes verbatim — the module docstring explicitly notes that CoT XML may contain binary content or non-UTF-8 sequences in some edge cases (embedded attachment bytes, raw protobuf interop blobs), and a regression to a UTF-8-validating pass-through (e.g., via `str::from_utf8(...)?`) would silently fail on those payloads. Source-protocol-grep-token contract (one): `json_preserves_source_protocol_verbatim_for_federation_hop` overrides `metadata.source_protocol` to the literal string `"federation"` and asserts the JSON field emits that exact lowercase string — the router's echo-prevention path grep-compares against this token, so a silent case-normalisation (`"Federation"`, `"FEDERATION"`) refactor would bypass echo prevention and create a routing loop. Suite grows from 10 to 20 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors, free functions, and the `Serialize` derive through their documented contracts. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-http::filter` — `exclude_protocols` case-insensitivity on both the sender and config sides, `groups`-dimension deliberately-case-sensitive asymmetry vs `source_cns`, `MessageType::Display` lowercase-token wire contract across every built-in variant plus the `Other(_)` `"other:"` prefix, `Some(vec![])` fail-closed behaviour on every positive-match dimension, sender-with-no-groups fails the group filter, `exclude_protocols` exact-match-not-substring (`"http2"` and `"https"` are NOT blocked by an `"http"` exclusion), duplicate-config-entry idempotence via HashSet dedup, destination-field-is-not-consulted guard across Broadcast / Group / Multicast, `default_filter()` ≡ `from_config(all-None)` equivalence, and `CompiledFilter: Debug` derive pin** — `crates/rustgate-http/src/filter.rs` is the per-target message filter compiled from `FilterConfig` and evaluated on the HTTP publisher's hot path: every outbound message hits `CompiledFilter::matches` before the publish queue, so every filter-semantic bug directly translates into either a dropped legitimate message or a routing-loop-creating echo. Before this PR the inline suite (12 tests) covered the happy-path cases — single-dimension accept/reject, combined-filter AND logic, `default_filter()` smoke, echo prevention for a literal `"http"`, and case-insensitivity of `source_cns` — but left a material set of defensive invariants unpinned that a silent refactor could break without any downstream integration test catching it. The one that matters most is the **asymmetric case-sensitivity contract between `groups` and `source_cns`**: the compile step lowercases `source_cns` (following the ACL module convention for CNs) but does *not* lowercase group names, because `rustgate_core::identity::GroupId` is a case-preserving identifier and the ACL module treats `"SpecialOps"` and `"specialops"` as distinct groups. A refactor that "normalised" the group dimension to match the CN dimension would silently collapse distinct groups and route messages to the wrong egress target — the new `groups_filter_is_case_sensitive_unlike_source_cns` test pins both halves of the asymmetry in one assertion pair (exact-case matches, lowercase-variant does NOT match a mixed-case sender group). Twelve new inline tests close the gaps. Echo-prevention case-insensitivity (two): `exclude_protocols_case_insensitive_on_sender_side` drives `"HTTP"`, `"Http"`, `"hTTp"`, `"http"` against `default_filter()` and asserts every variant is blocked — the comparator is `eq_ignore_ascii_case` on every hot-path evaluation because the sender-side `source_protocol` string is attacker-influenced (a buggy upstream peer could emit any case), and a regression that dropped the comparator and relied only on pre-lowercased storage would still pass every happy-path lowercase test but silently let `"HTTP"` from a federated peer re-enter the HTTP egress path and create a routing loop; `exclude_protocols_config_side_also_lowercased_on_insert` pins the symmetric invariant — configured entries `"NATS"` and `"Mqtt"` still match lowercased sender protocols, guarding the operational contract "operators can write exclude-protocols in whatever case they like and the filter just works" against a refactor that touched one side without the other. Group-vs-CN asymmetry (one, load-bearing — described above). Message-type wire contract (two): `message_type_filter_matches_display_lowercase_rendering` iterates over all six built-in `MessageType` variants (`Position`, `Chat`, `Alert`, `Status`, `File`, `Mavlink`) and asserts each renders to its lowercase token — a regression that changed the `Display` impl to TitleCase (a plausible "operator-facing improvement" diff) would silently break every deployed message-type filter without a compile error, since the filter compares against `msg.metadata.message_type.to_string()` and the configured values in the HashSet stay verbatim; `message_type_other_variant_renders_other_colon_prefix` pins the `"other:"` prefix contract for `MessageType::Other(_)`, so a regression that dropped the prefix would silently break filters configured with the documented `"other:foo"` tokens. Fail-closed (one): `empty_filter_vector_rejects_everything_in_that_dimension` walks all three positive-match dimensions (`groups`, `message_types`, `source_cns`) with `Some(vec![])` and asserts every case rejects — an empty list means "nothing", not "everything". A regression that conflated `Some(empty)` with `None` (as a "helpful" short-circuit) would silently open the filter wide and route every message to the target, inverting the least-privilege expectation every downstream operator assumes. Ancillary contracts (six): `sender_with_no_groups_fails_group_filter` pins the correct fail-closed behaviour for unauthenticated or misconfigured clients whose mTLS cert has no group SAN; `exclude_protocols_is_exact_match_not_prefix_or_substring` asserts `"http2"` and `"https"` both pass an `"http"` exclusion, so a regression to substring matching "for convenience" would silently block every HTTP/2, HTTPS, or HTTP-adjacent federated protocol; `duplicate_config_entries_do_not_affect_match_outcome` pins HashSet-dedup idempotence on `source_cns` (also exercising the `"alice"` + `"alice"` + `"ALICE"` case-folded-dedup path); `destination_field_is_not_inspected_by_filter` constructs three different `Destination` variants (`Broadcast`, `Group`, `Multicast`) against an otherwise-matching filter and asserts every one passes, pinning that the filter reads only `source.groups`, `source.cn`, `metadata.source_protocol`, and `metadata.message_type` — a regression that started consulting `destination` would silently start dropping unicast / multicast messages whose destination did not match an operator-invisible heuristic; `default_filter_and_empty_exclude_protocols_config_are_equivalent_for_http` pins the two-path `http`-block equivalence between `default_filter()` and `from_config(FilterConfig { all None })` so a regression that made `from_config` fail to unconditionally seed the `"http"` entry on `None` input would fail here; `compiled_filter_implements_debug` pins the `#[derive(Debug)]` so enclosing types' auto-derived Debug chains stay compilable. Suite grows from 12 to 24 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors and the single `matches` entry-point through their documented contracts. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-authz::cache` — `DecisionCache: Send + Sync + 'static` compile-time bound pin, `DecisionKey` + `SubjectHash` Copy+Clone+Eq+Hash+Debug derive pins, `capacity_per_shard == 0` pathological-input degrades-to-FIFO-of-one contract, cross-shard FIFO isolation (eviction in shard A does not touch shard B), all five `Action` variants (`Send`, `Receive`, `Federate`, `Publish`, `Subscribe`) produce distinct cache entries, `Decision::Deny` round-trips through the cache identically to `Allow`, policy-epoch downgrade lookups miss (bidirectional invalidation), `DecisionOutcome::allow()`/`deny()` helper-shape pin (`rule_id` absent, `obligations` empty, verdict correct), and `get`-returns-independent-clones isolation (mutating one retrieval's `obligations` vec does not affect a subsequent retrieval)** — `crates/rustgate-authz/src/cache.rs` is the sharded FIFO-eviction decision cache every authz engine sits behind: every permission check queries this cache before re-compiling a rule match, so a cache-layer correctness bug is directly an authorisation bug. Before this PR the inline suite (13 tests) covered the happy-path hit/miss contract, epoch invalidation (forward direction), FIFO eviction within a single shard, shard-count rounding, default sizing, Debug output shape, Clone-drops-entries semantics, insert-dedup no-overwrite + no-FIFO-bump, the full `DecisionOutcome` field round-trip, `SubjectHash` secondary-half differentiation, `action` and `resource_hash` differentiation, hit/miss counter independence, and the shard-formula-consumes-secondary adversarial-resistance invariant — but it left a material set of defensive invariants unpinned that a silent refactor could break. The one that matters most is the **`Decision::Deny` round-trip**: the existing coverage pins Allow outcomes in round-trips, but a regression that stripped the `decision` field from `CachedOutcome` and defaulted to `Allow` on retrieval would be a catastrophic authz bypass — every Deny decision would silently become an Allow after the first cache hit, and every existing Allow-only test would still pass. The new `decision_deny_round_trips_through_cache` test pins the Deny path explicitly: `Decision::Deny` + `rule_id` + `obligations` all survive the round-trip verbatim. Ten new inline tests close the gaps. Trait-surface pins (three): `decision_cache_is_send_sync_and_static` uses a local `fn assert_bounds<T: Send + Sync + 'static>()` compile-time helper — the cache is shared across concurrent request evaluators via `Arc<DecisionCache>` inside every engine, so a future field that broke `Send + Sync` (a `Cell`, an `Rc`, a `!Send` `RefCell`) would fail to compile at those sites but with error messages pointing at the enclosing engine's `tokio::spawn` rather than at this module; `decision_key_derives_copy_clone_eq_hash_debug` pins the full derive set (`Copy + Clone + PartialEq + Eq + Hash + Debug`) since the key is passed by value to every `get`/`insert` call, copied into `order: VecDeque<DecisionKey>` on eviction, and rendered in triage diagnostics; `subject_hash_derives_copy_clone_eq_hash_debug` pins the same derive set for `SubjectHash` since it is the load-bearing 128-bit identity token flowing by value through `DecisionKey` on every lookup — a refactor that replaced one 64-bit half with a non-`Copy` type (e.g. a `Zeroizing<[u8; 8]>`) would break every caller with non-local errors. Pathological-input pin (one): `capacity_per_shard_zero_degrades_to_fifo_of_one` drives `DecisionCache::new(1, 0)` — a misconfigured `authz-cache-per-shard = 0` in operator-supplied TOML — and asserts the cache stores exactly one live entry and evicts on every subsequent insert (the eviction-loop body is `if len >= capacity && let Some(oldest) = pop_front()`, so at cap=0 the first insert's `pop_front()` returns `None` and the insert proceeds anyway; every subsequent insert's `pop_front()` returns the one live entry and the new key replaces it). A regression that panicked on cap=0 (via unchecked division, or an off-by-one in capacity math) would break every engine started with that misconfiguration. Sharding-isolation pin (one): `fifo_eviction_is_per_shard_not_global` fills one shard to its `cap + 1` (forcing eviction there) and asserts an entry in a different shard survives untouched — a regression that collapsed the per-shard `order` queues into a single global one (e.g. by moving `order` up to the parent struct) would silently start evicting across shards, degrading hit rate unpredictably and decoupling capacity from shard count. The test includes a belt-and-suspenders probe — an explicit `assert_ne!(shard_idx(&s0_a), shard_idx(&s_other))` — so a future refactor to `shard_idx` that accidentally collided the two probe keys would trip *that* assertion first and direct the maintainer to update the probe, rather than letting the real assertion silently tautologise. Action-differentiation pin (one): `all_action_variants_produce_distinct_cache_entries` inserts each of the five `Action` variants with a distinctive `rule_id` and retrieves them in the same order, asserting every action retrieves its own outcome — a regression that folded two actions into one cached slot would silently grant a `Send` decision the authority of a `Federate`, a `Publish` the authority of a `Subscribe`, and every one of those collapses is a directly exploitable authz bypass. Epoch pins (two): `decision_deny_round_trips_through_cache` pins the Deny path (described above); `policy_epoch_downgrade_lookup_is_a_miss` pins the bidirectional-invalidation contract — the existing `epoch_change_invalidates` tests forward (insert at epoch 1, lookup at epoch 2 misses), this test pins the reverse (insert at epoch 2, lookup at epoch 1 misses), guarding the niche case where a stale request held onto an older epoch value while the compiler already advanced. Helper-shape pin (one): `decision_outcome_helpers_produce_documented_shape` asserts `DecisionOutcome::allow()` and `deny()` each produce the documented shape (correct verdict, `rule_id` absent, `obligations` empty, `is_allow()` agreeing) — a regression that populated a default `rule_id` (e.g. `Some("default")`) would silently attribute every fast-path decision to a phantom rule in audit logs. Retrieval-isolation pin (one): `repeated_gets_on_hit_return_independent_cloned_outcomes` inserts an outcome, retrieves it, mutates the retrieval's `obligations` vec, retrieves again, and asserts the second copy is unmodified — pinning that the cache clones the `CachedOutcome` under the shard lock and drops the guard before converting to `DecisionOutcome`, so two sequential callers never alias the underlying `Vec<Obligation>`. A regression that returned a borrow, or reused a single `Arc`-backed `Vec`, could let one caller's mutation leak into another's retrieval. Suite grows from 13 to 23 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors, methods, and derive-generated traits through their documented contracts. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-mqtt::error` — `std::error::Error::source()` chaining through the `#[from] std::io::Error` attribute on the `Io` variant, `source() == None` sweep across every non-Io variant, `'static` lifetime bound compile-time pin, full-variant `Debug` name-presence sweep across all twelve variants, `UnsupportedProtocol(0)`/`(u8::MAX)` boundary-value Display renders, `KeepAliveTimeout(u16::MAX)` no-truncation render, `PacketTooLarge { size: usize::MAX, max: usize::MAX }` full-digit render, empty-String payload Display tolerance on all four string-carrying variants (`NotAuthorized`, `InvalidTopic`, `Malformed`, `ClientIdInUse`), compile-time exhaustive-match pin (no `#[non_exhaustive]`, so the reason-code mapper compiler-checks every variant when new ones are added), and `ErrorKind` round-trip preservation through the `From<io::Error>` conversion across five representative kinds** — `crates/rustgate-mqtt/src/error.rs` is the MQTT session error enum that maps directly into MQTT 3.1.1 CONNACK return codes + MQTT 5.0 reason codes; every variant's Display is rendered into either a disconnect-reason field on the wire or an operator-facing audit / tracing event, so the exact text shape is an interop contract. Before this PR the inline suite (16 tests) pinned the Display text of every variant, `From<io::Error>` basic mapping, `Error` trait bound, and `Send + Sync` — but it left a material set of defensive invariants unpinned that a silent refactor could break. The one that matters most is the **`Error::source()` chain through `#[from]` on the `Io` variant**: downstream callers funnel MQTT errors into `anyhow::Context::context()` chains, and the operator-facing chain rendering depends on `source()` walking through to the inner `io::Error`. A regression that dropped the implicit `#[source]` behaviour of `#[from]` (or swapped the template to `"{source}"` without the `#[source]` marker) would silently break the chain — the outer `I/O error:` prefix would render but the wrapped io::Error's detail ("Connection reset by peer", "Broken pipe", etc.) would vanish from every `{e:#}` output, and no existing test would notice. Ten new inline tests close the gaps. Source-chain pins (two): `io_variant_source_returns_inner_io_error` asserts `Io` variants chain to their inner io::Error's Display verbatim; `non_io_variants_return_none_from_source` iterates over every non-Io variant (all eleven) and asserts `source() == None` so a regression that added a `#[source]`-annotated field to e.g. `Malformed` would silently extend operator-facing error chains. Trait-bound pin (one): `mqtt_error_is_static_bound` uses a local `fn assert_static<T: 'static>()` — the enum carries only owned data today, and downstream `Box<dyn std::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static>` boxing depends on this bound; a regression that added a borrowed field (e.g. `&str` on `Malformed`) would fail here with a pointed error rather than at the far-away boxing site. Debug-sweep pin (one): `debug_names_every_variant_when_present_in_log_stream` iterates over all twelve variants and asserts each Debug render contains its own variant name — operators grep on variant names to filter log streams, so a `thiserror::Debug`-generation regression that elided a variant would silently break alerting without failing any other test. Boundary-value pins (three): `unsupported_protocol_zero_and_max_render_exactly` pins `UnsupportedProtocol(0).to_string() == "unsupported protocol version: 0"` and `UnsupportedProtocol(u8::MAX).to_string() == "unsupported protocol version: 255"` (a refactor to `{0:02}` zero-pad, or `{0:x}` hex, would change operator-facing text and break SIEM regex rules); `keep_alive_timeout_at_u16_max_renders_exactly` pins `KeepAliveTimeout(u16::MAX).to_string() == "keep-alive timeout (65535s)"` since a hostile or buggy client can advertise the maximum value and the render must survive without a truncated string; `packet_too_large_at_usize_max_renders_exactly` pins full-digit rendering of `usize::MAX` in both `size` and `max` fields (on a 64-bit host that is a 20-digit literal), guarding against a refactor to `fmt::LowerHex` or a "GiB" human-readable renderer that would change the log shape. Empty-payload defence (one): `string_variants_tolerate_empty_payload_without_panicking` drives `String::new()` through all four String-carrying variants and asserts every Display template preserves its static prefix verbatim, including the trailing separator — an adversary-supplied CONNECT with a zero-length Malformed reason, or a topic filter that normalises to empty, must not produce a garbage render. Exhaustive-match pin (one): `error_enum_exhaustive_match_compiles_with_no_wildcard` defines a local `classify` fn that exhaustively matches on every variant with no wildcard arm; the match compiles iff the enum does not carry `#[non_exhaustive]`, guarding against a future annotation that would let the reason-code mapper in `crate::session` silently add a `_ => PROTOCOL_ERROR` fallback and start mapping new variants to the wrong reason code. ErrorKind-preservation pin (one): `io_variant_preserves_error_kind_through_from_conversion` drives five representative `ErrorKind` values (`TimedOut`, `ConnectionReset`, `BrokenPipe`, `UnexpectedEof`, `WouldBlock`) through the `From<io::Error>` conversion and asserts `kind()` survives unchanged — upstream callers branch on `err.kind()` to route session drops into the appropriate audit event, so a regression that collapsed every kind to `Other` would silently break alerting on the correct disconnect class. Suite grows from 16 to 26 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors and the derive-generated `Display`/`Debug`/`Error`/`From<io::Error>` impls through their documented contracts. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-cot::mask` — `MaskError::kind()` stable-discriminant-string pin across all three variants, `MaskError::kind()` payload-free `&'static str` invariant (three distinct malformed payloads return identical static-pointer tokens), `std::error::Error::source()` chain behaviour across every variant (Xml and Io chain through, AttrBudgetExceeded is terminal), `AttrBudgetExceeded` Display text exact-format pin with embedded limit value, `MaskError: Send + Sync + 'static` compile-time bound, `is_point_tag` suffix / prefix / substring negative-match sweep (`<pointer>`, `<pointful>`, `<mypoint>`, `<xpoint>`, `<points>` must NOT mask), coordinate-attribute byte-exact case-sensitive matching asymmetry with element-name case-insensitive matching pin (`<point LAT="…" LON="…" HAE="…"/>` preserves uppercase attrs), deeply-nested `<point>` inside `<detail><wrap><inner>` subtree masking pin, per-element-not-document-cumulative attribute-budget counter pin (two `<point>` elements each at 50 attrs — total 100, over the 64 cap if summed — both pass), and compile-time `MAX_POINT_ATTRIBUTES == 64` public-constant value pin** — `crates/rustgate-cot/src/mask.rs` implements the data-plane side of the `MaskPoint` authorization obligation: when the policy engine returns an Allow carrying `MaskPoint`, the router calls `strip_coordinates` on a per-recipient basis so analysts who may see the event metadata but not its geography receive a copy with `<point>`'s `lat`, `lon`, and `hae` attributes zeroed to `"0.0"` while all other XML (event metadata, detail children, source group, comments, processing instructions, CDATA) is passed through byte-for-byte. Before this PR the inline suite (17 tests) pinned self-closing and open-form `<point>` element masking, non-coordinate attribute preservation, multi-`<point>` handling, case-variant element name matching (`<Point>`, `<POINT>`, `<cot:point>`, `<cot:Point>`), fast-path pass-through for non-XML payloads and leading-whitespace non-XML, malformed-XML fail-closed behaviour, exact attribute-budget boundary (at-cap ok, over-cap rejects), and CoT round-trip re-parse of the masked output — but it left a material set of defensive invariants unpinned that a silent refactor could break. The one that matters most is **`MaskError::kind()` remaining a payload-free `&'static str` alternative to `Display`**: the module doc explicitly calls out that `quick_xml::Error`'s own `Display` echoes payload-derived tag names via `EndEventMismatch { expected, found }`, and a DEBUG log that prints the full error chain therefore risks leaking fragments of a classified payload that failed to parse. Operators must be able to emit `kind()` into structured-log `reason` fields knowing the token is drawn from a static pool of three strings — a regression that changed `kind()` to format the inner error, or to allocate a `String`, would silently start leaking payload bytes into audit-sink destinations. Ten new inline tests close the gaps. Discriminant-string pins (two): `kind_returns_stable_discriminant_strings_across_variants` pins the exact tokens `"xml_parse_error"`, `"io_error"`, and `"attr_budget_exceeded"` on all three variants — operators grep on these to filter log streams, so a refactor that renamed any of them would silently break SIEM rules; `kind_returns_static_str_independent_of_payload_bytes` feeds three distinct malformed payloads through `strip_coordinates`, binds each returned `kind()` to a `&'static str`, and asserts all three return the same `"xml_parse_error"` with identical pointer addresses — a regression that made `kind()` allocate a per-payload `String` would fail the pointer-equality check. Source-chain pin (one): `source_chain_behaves_as_documented_for_every_variant` iterates over all three variants and asserts `Xml` and `Io` both return `Some` (so downstream `anyhow::Context::context()` chains can still render the parser detail in operator-facing error output) while `AttrBudgetExceeded` returns `None` — a regression that added a `#[source]`-annotated field to the budget variant would silently extend operator-facing error chains, and a regression that dropped the manual `Error::source` forwarding would collapse the chain. Display-text pin (one): `attr_budget_exceeded_display_embeds_limit_value_exactly` asserts the literal render `"<point> attribute count exceeded masking budget of 64"` for `limit: 64` and the equivalent for `limit: 128` — a refactor to `{limit:03}` zero-pad, `{limit:#x}` hex, or "`<point> had too many attributes`" would break SIEM correlation rules that match on the numeric literal to correlate amplification attempts. Trait-bound pin (one): `mask_error_is_send_sync_static` uses a local `fn assert_bounds<T: Send + Sync + 'static>()` — `MaskError` crosses async boundaries in the router, and downstream audit sinks wrap it in `Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync + 'static>` for chained-error reporting; a regression that added a borrowed field (e.g. `&str` name) would fail this bound here with a pointed compile error instead of at the far-away boxing site. Negative-match pin (one): `point_tag_match_rejects_suffix_and_prefix_element_variants` asserts that `<pointer>`, `<pointful>`, `<mypoint>`, `<xpoint>`, and `<points>` are NOT masked — the matcher uses `eq_ignore_ascii_case` on the namespace-stripped local name against the exact literal `"point"`, and a refactor to a `starts_with` / `ends_with` / `contains` substring match would over-mask legitimate elements that happen to share the substring. Asymmetric-match pin (one): `coordinate_attribute_matching_is_byte_exact_and_case_sensitive` asserts the documented contract that element-name matching is case-insensitive but coordinate-attribute matching is byte-exact — `<point LAT="38.8977" LON="-77.0365" HAE="125.4"/>` preserves the uppercase attribute values verbatim; a refactor to case-insensitive attribute matching would over-mask legitimate events re-using `LAT`/`LON`/`HAE` as custom attributes, and pinning the asymmetry explicitly keeps that choice deliberate. Depth-tolerance pin (one): `deeply_nested_point_inside_detail_subtree_is_masked` drives a `<point>` element five levels deep under `<event><detail><wrap><inner>…` — `strip_coordinates` walks a flat event stream so depth is irrelevant, but a refactor to stateful tracking (e.g. "only mask top-level `<point>`") could create a leak by wrapping coords in deeper elements; pinning the depth-independence invariant guards against that. Budget-scope pin (one): `attribute_budget_is_per_element_not_document_cumulative` drives two `<point>` elements each carrying 50 attributes (total 100 — well over the 64-cap if summed) and asserts the payload succeeds — the counter resets between elements; a regression that hoisted the counter to document scope would reject legitimate multi-point CoT payloads without failing any other test. Constant-value pin (one): `max_point_attributes_public_constant_value_is_pinned_at_64` uses a `const _: () = assert!(MAX_POINT_ATTRIBUTES == 64)` compile-time probe and a runtime `assert_eq!` — `MAX_POINT_ATTRIBUTES` is a public constant governing the per-recipient masking cost ceiling, and a regression that loosened it (e.g. to 1024) would let a hostile sender amplify CPU cost on a broadcast without failing any other test. Suite grows from 17 to 27 tests; zero changes to any production API — every new test drives only existing constructors, `strip_coordinates`, the public `MAX_POINT_ATTRIBUTES` constant, and the manual `Display` / `Error` / `From` impls through their documented contracts. (Refs #909)
