Five concrete edits land in the coverage job: (1) the `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` step now installs the `llvm-tools-preview` component alongside the default profile so `llvm-profdata` and `llvm-cov` are available for the source-based coverage instrumentation — this is the same component tarpaulin's `--engine llvm` path required internally, so the coverage model is unchanged; (2) a new `Install cargo-nextest` step guarded with `command -v cargo-nextest` mirrors the guard pattern used in the `test` job so the tool is installed once on GitHub-hosted and skipped on the pre-baked self-hosted image; (3) the `taiki-e/install-action@v2` step swaps `tool: cargo-tarpaulin` for `tool: cargo-llvm-cov` so the installer resolves to the prebuilt `cargo-llvm-cov` binary rather than downloading and linking tarpaulin; (4) the run command changes from `cargo tarpaulin --engine llvm --workspace --features … --out Xml --output-dir coverage --locked` to `mkdir -p coverage && cargo llvm-cov --locked --workspace --features nats,enrollment,est,proto,full-detail,metrics,federation,federation-ha,websocket,mqtt,otel,http --cobertura --output-path coverage/cobertura.xml nextest` — cargo-llvm-cov's flag-ordering contract requires top-level options (`--locked`, `--workspace`, `--features`, `--cobertura`, `--output-path`) to precede the `nextest` subcommand, and unlike tarpaulin's `--output-dir` the `--output-path` argument does not auto-create the parent directory so an explicit `mkdir -p coverage` is needed ahead of the run; (5) the inline comment block documents both the model-equivalence (both tools ultimately emit LLVM source-based coverage) and the flag-ordering gotcha so a future maintainer editing the invocation does not silently break it. The `CONTRIBUTING.md` §CI Pipeline table is updated to name the new tool so operators reading the repo-level docs see `cargo llvm-cov --nextest` rather than `cargo tarpaulin`. Expected wall-clock impact: coverage drops from ~9m16s toward ~5-7 min on the same self-hosted runner, and the speed-up is proportional to the CPU count — `test` already scales with `$(nproc)`, so the coverage job inherits the same linear speed-up once llvm-cov drives nextest. No production code paths are touched, the artifact contract (`coverage/cobertura.xml`) is preserved, and the `RUNNER_POOL` ternary on `runs-on` is untouched so the job still falls back to `ubuntu-latest` when the variable is flipped.
- **Speed up CI on self-hosted runners: wire mold linker, prune redundant installers, split check-features depth by event** — the CI pipeline is now shaped around the `RUNNER_POOL == 'self-hosted'` repo-variable path where the `github-runner:v2` container image bakes in Rust 1.94, sccache, mold, cargo-nextest, protobuf-compiler, cmake, golang, musl-tools, and the aarch64 cross-compiler. Four changes land in `ci.yml` plus the installer guards in `nightly.yml` and `weekly-fuzz.yml`. (1) Top-level `RUSTFLAGS` becomes a conditional expression — `${{ vars.RUNNER_POOL == 'self-hosted' && '-Dwarnings -C link-arg=-fuse-ld=mold' || '-Dwarnings' }}` — so self-hosted jobs link through `mold` (3-10x faster than the GNU linker on Rust workspaces of this size) while the GitHub-hosted fallback keeps the default linker since `ubuntu-latest` does not ship mold. (2) Every `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` step (5 call sites across `lint`, `clippy`, `check-features`, `test`, `coverage`) is guarded with `if: vars.RUNNER_POOL != 'self-hosted'` — on self-hosted the shared sccache volume at `/home/rlchiofalo/ci-shared/sccache` (40 GB, 96%+ hit rate once warm) is the only cache we need, and layering Swatinem on top would waste 30-90 s per job on cache upload/download against a GitHub Actions cache that is itself capped at 5 GB per repo. (3) Redundant installer steps are guarded with `command -v X` checks rather than `RUNNER_POOL` conditionals so they are robust against misconfiguration — if the variable is unset or set to an unexpected value on a self-hosted image where the tool is already present, the check still skips the install; if the container image loses the tool, the install still fires and CI does not flake. Guards cover `protoc` (3 sites), `cargo-nextest` (3 sites), the `fips` multi-tool bundle (`protoc`, `cmake`, `go`, `musl-gcc`), the `aarch64-linux-gnu-gcc` cross-compiler, the `post-quantum` bundle (`protoc`, `cmake`, `go`), and the `cargo-fuzz` nightly binary on the nightly/weekly-fuzz workflows. On self-hosted this collapses ~5-8 minutes of per-job apt-get/curl time into near-zero; on GitHub-hosted the behaviour is unchanged. (4) The `check-features` job's `cargo hack check --feature-powerset` now runs at `--depth 1` on pull-request events and `--depth 2` on push-to-main — most feature regressions are per-feature rather than combinatorial, and depth 2 was burning ~32 wall-clock minutes per PR for rare pair-breakage. Branch protection on `main` ensures depth-2 runs before any release is cut from a protected branch, so the combinatorial surface is still exercised before a release tag; PRs get a ~8-12 minute check-features job instead. Net effect on a cache-warm self-hosted PR run: PR wall-time drops from ~114 minutes (documented baseline) toward ~48 minutes, a ~60 % reduction. The fallback to `ubuntu-latest` via the `RUNNER_POOL` ternary is preserved unchanged, so flipping the repo variable reverts every job to the previous behaviour without a merge. No production code paths are touched.
- **Replace `FramedCodec` disabled-feature `unreachable!()` with fallible `try_from_strategy`** — `rustgate_transport::FramedCodec::from_strategy` is replaced by `try_from_strategy(&FramingStrategy) -> Result<Self, FramedCodecError>`. The two pre-existing `unreachable!()` branches in the `#[cfg(not(feature = "proto"))]` / `#[cfg(not(feature = "mavlink"))]` arms would have aborted the process under the workspace-wide `panic = "abort"` release profile if a SIGHUP-reloaded listener config ever pointed at a codec whose cargo feature was not built in — taking down every in-flight TLS connection for a misconfiguration that should have produced a structured rejection. The new `FramedCodecError::FeatureNotEnabled { feature: &'static str }` variant names the offending cargo feature (`"proto"` or `"mavlink"`) in its `thiserror`-derived `Display`, and a `From<FramedCodecError> for io::Error` impl maps onto `io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput` so existing session pipelines propagate the failure via `?` without changing the error-kind surface. The three server-side call sites in `rustgate_server::session` migrate to `try_from_strategy(...)?`. The `rustgate-client` call site uses `.expect(...)` with a rationale ("Codec enum only exposes always-available strategies") since the public `Codec` enum intentionally exposes only unconditional variants (`LengthPrefixed1/2/4`, `XmlStream`, `LineDelimited`) — the fallible signature documents the contract at the crate boundary while provably never firing from that caller. Aligns with the workspace `clippy::panic = "deny"` rule. Pinned by four new regression tests in `crates/rustgate-transport/src/codec.rs`: always-available variants round-trip, both `#[cfg(not(...))]` branches return `FeatureNotEnabled` with the correct feature name, and the `From<FramedCodecError> for io::Error` conversion yields `InvalidInput` with the feature name in the rendered message. (#750)
- **Consolidate `base64` engine usage into `rustgate_core::b64`** — new `rustgate_core::b64` module provides workspace-wide `encode`, `decode`, `encode_url_safe`, and `decode_url_safe` helpers wrapping the two `base64` engines the workspace actually uses (`STANDARD` per RFC 4648 §4 and `URL_SAFE_NO_PAD` per RFC 4648 §5). Previously 17 files across five crates reached for `base64` directly in three inconsistent styles — `base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(…)`, `base64::Engine::encode(&STANDARD, …)`, and `use base64::Engine as _; STANDARD.encode(…)` — making it easy to paste the wrong call shape or mismatch the engine to the spec (the JWT code in `rustgate-federation` correctly used §5 while every other caller used §4). `rustgate-http`, `rustgate-federation`, `rustgate-transport`, and `rustgate-enrollment` drop their direct `base64` dependency and import via `rustgate_core::b64` instead; the raw engines are re-exported under the same module for the handful of callers that still need the `Engine` trait directly. No behavioural change — the underlying `STANDARD` / `URL_SAFE_NO_PAD` semantics are preserved byte-for-byte. (#830)
- **Expand SA affiliation coverage to full MITRE Appendix A set** — `rustgate_cot::SaAffiliation` now recognizes all 11 affiliation codes defined in MITRE CoT Message Router User's Guide (MP090284) Appendix A / Figure 2-3, aligned with MIL-STD-2525 standard identity: `Pending` (`a-p-*`), `AssumedFriend` (`a-a-*`), `Friend` (`a-f-*`, renamed from `Friendly`), `Neutral` (`a-n-*`), `Suspect` (`a-s-*`), `Hostile` (`a-h-*`), `Joker` (`a-j-*`), `Faker` (`a-k-*`), `Unknown` (`a-u-*`), `NoneSpecified` (`a-o-*`), `Other` (`a-x-*`). Previously only `f/h/n/u` were recognized, so `a-s-…` (suspect), `a-j-…` (joker), and `a-k-…` (faker) events were misclassified as non-atoms by `is_sa_event` / `sa_affiliation`. (#932)

### Security

- **Cap authz policy rule count at 10,000 before compile** — `rustgate_server::authz_reloader` gains a `MAX_POLICY_RULES = 10_000` cap enforced in both `load_once` (startup) and `AuthzReloader::reload` (hot-reload) paths. The existing 4 MiB raw-file cap (`MAX_POLICY_FILE_BYTES`) bounds the pre-parse read, but a 4 MiB JSON document can still compile into tens of millions of minimal rules — enough to starve a pod's RAM during `NativeEngine::new()` or degrade every subsequent decision eval to a linear scan through the compiled bucket table. A new private `check_rule_count` helper rejects oversized policies between parse and compile, emitting `audit::authz_reload_failed` with stage `"validate"` so operators can distinguish a size-rejected policy from a malformed one in the audit trail. Real-world policies live in the low hundreds of rules; 10,000 is ~100x headroom over any plausible hand-authored deployment. Closes the F-37 half of #812. The F-36 half (sharded decision cache against adversarial CN rotation) was already in place — `rustgate_authz::cache::DecisionCache` shards (default 16) are keyed by a mix of both 64-bit halves of `SubjectHash` plus the resource hash so adversarial collisions on any single component cannot funnel traffic into one shard, and `rustgate_authz_cache_hits_total` / `rustgate_authz_cache_misses_total` counters are already emitted every 10 s from `main.rs` for hit-ratio derivation in PromQL. (#812)
- **Apply `step-security/harden-runner` to every PR CI job and deduplicate the pinned `nats-server` release descriptor** — the runner-hardening action previously shielded only the `audit` job, leaving the remaining eleven jobs (commit-lint, playbook-lint, lint, clippy, check-features, test, coverage, fips, post-quantum, helm, docker) running with default egress and no telemetry into outbound connections from third-party actions. Every job now starts with `step-security/harden-runner@v2.18.0` in `egress-policy: audit` mode — matching the workspace-wide pattern already used in `audit` and `release-binary.yml` — so every outbound connection is observable in the step-security dashboard; this is the prerequisite for a later tightening to `egress-policy: block` with a curated allowlist once real traffic data has been collected. Separately, the pinned `nats-server` v2.10.24 tarball SHA-256 was hardcoded in four places (cache key + sha256sum check, repeated across the `test` and `coverage` jobs) — a bump now only requires editing the two new workflow-level env vars `NATS_SERVER_VERSION` and `NATS_SERVER_SHA256`, eliminating the silent-mismatch class of bug where the cache key advertises one hash while the integrity check validates another. (#781)
- **Helm chart RBAC / ServiceAccount posture is now CI-enforced** — the `helm` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` previously ran only `helm lint` and a bare `helm template … > /dev/null`, so a future chart change adding a `Role`, `ClusterRole`, `RoleBinding`, `ClusterRoleBinding`, or `ServiceAccount` with wildcard verbs, over-privileged rules, or a default-SA binding would land on `main` with a green check. The job now renders the production values (`helm template rustgate helm/ -f helm/values-production.yaml`) and scans every document with a purpose-built Python validator that fails the build on: wildcard `verbs` / `resources` / `apiGroups` / `resourceNames` inside any `Role` or `ClusterRole`; `ClusterRole` rules that list `secrets` cluster-wide; rules granting mutating verbs on `nodes`; rules that create/delete/patch/update `customresourcedefinitions`; `RoleBinding` / `ClusterRoleBinding` subjects targeting the `default` ServiceAccount; `ServiceAccount` objects that do not explicitly set `automountServiceAccountToken: false`; and workload `PodSpec`s (Pod, Deployment, StatefulSet, DaemonSet, Job, CronJob, ReplicaSet) that omit `automountServiceAccountToken: false` without naming a non-default ServiceAccount. The scanner is a pure-Python script — deliberately chosen over kubeaudit (planned-for-deprecation upstream since October 2024) and polaris (pulls in broader pod-security hardening that is out of scope for this PR). A prerequisite chart fix (`helm/templates/tests/test-connection.yaml`) adds `automountServiceAccountToken: false` to the Helm test Pod so the existing chart passes the scan on a clean baseline. (#782)
- **Release pipeline: attest private-repo builds and sign SBOMs** — closes the remaining two gaps from the release-pipeline hardening epic (#778). **F-79**: both `attest-build-provenance` invocations (`release.yml` for the container image, `release-binary.yml` for each cross-compiled binary archive) previously ran under `if: github.repository_visibility == 'public'`, which silently skipped SLSA build-provenance attestation on private forks and internal mirrors — a build that passes CI on an internal repo shipped without the in-toto statement that downstream `gh attestation verify` or Sigstore policy controllers expect. The guard is removed; both actions use the Sigstore public good instance for public and private repos alike per upstream docs, and the transparency-log entries contain only subject digests and build metadata (no private source material), so unconditional attestation is both safe and required for SLSA L3 on every build. **F-84**: the SPDX SBOMs produced by the release pipeline were previously uploaded as raw release assets with no cryptographic binding to the build that produced them. The container-image SBOM is now bound to the image digest via `cosign attest --type spdxjson`, verifiable downstream with `cosign verify-attestation --type spdxjson`. The source SBOM is signed as a detached `.sigstore.json` bundle via `cosign sign-blob` (since release-asset files are not registry-addressable, `sign-blob` rather than `attest` is the correct primitive) and additionally receives a build-provenance attestation via `actions/attest-build-provenance`, verifiable downstream with `gh attestation verify`. The container-image attestation is gated behind `continue-on-error: true` on the post-sign best-effort block in `release.yml` so an SBOM generation or attestation hiccup does not un-ship an already-signed image; consumers who require attested SBOMs gate admission on the attestation's presence, not on the release job's exit code. F-78 (Trivy scan gates cosign sign) was already addressed in #777; this PR closes the remaining two sub-items in the same triple. (#778)
- **Reject federation RPC when mTLS client-cert CN is unavailable** — the V2 gRPC `FederationService` previously fell back to the remote IP address when `request.peer_certs()` returned no cert or when `x509_parser` failed to extract a CN, logging only a `warn!` and proceeding. A peer presenting an expired, malformed, or missing cert would then be identified by IP and could silently match an IP-keyed `peer_groups` allow-list entry previously populated by a legitimate peer on the same address — a `send_one_event`, `health_check`, or streaming RPC would be processed under that stolen identity, reaching the authorization and hop-count checks. `peer_id_from_request` now returns `Result<String, Status>`; on any extraction failure it emits the new `audit::federation_peer_rejected(remote_addr, reason)` event and short-circuits every RPC handler (`send_one_event`, `health_check`, `server_event_stream`, `binary_message_stream`, `server_rol_stream`, `client_federate_groups_stream`) with `Status::unauthenticated` before any group or hop-count evaluation runs. The `reason` tag distinguishes `peer_cert_missing` (non-mTLS connection) from `peer_cert_unparseable_or_empty_cn` (cert present but subject CN absent after DER parse and control-char stripping) so operators can triage deployment misconfigurations separately from malformed-cert probes. Non-mTLS testing paths that relied on the previous IP fallback must now supply a real client certificate; the `v2_integration` test harness is migrated to TestCa-backed mTLS to match the production invariant. (#759)
- **Sanitize peer-controlled fields and hash client CNs in structured logs** — the federation gRPC server in `rustgate_federation::v2::server` emits a `warn!` with the offending group name when a peer claims a group it is not authorized for. `group` was logged raw, so a malicious peer could register a group name containing `\r\n[audit] forged=true\r\n` and forge an entire fake audit record in text-mode log output (the JSON layer is not vulnerable, but the default `tracing_subscriber::fmt` text formatter is, and operators often run mixed pipelines). `group` is now routed through `rustgate_core::sanitize::sanitize_for_log` on both call sites — the single-event `validate_event_groups` path and the streaming `send_event_stream` path that logs the full `federate_groups` vector. A regression test attaches a capturing `MakeWriter` to the `rustgate_federation::v2::server` target, pushes an event with an attacker-crafted group name, and asserts no raw `\r\n` survives into the captured bytes and no line in the output begins with the forged marker. Separately, `rustgate_websocket` and `rustgate_mqtt` session handlers replaced 24 raw `cn = %identity.cn` / `cn = %cn` tracing-field emissions (session start/end, CONNECT/DISCONNECT, subscribe/unsubscribe, client-ID collisions, rate-limit drops, replay drops, tenant-quota rejections, keep-alive timeouts) with `cn = %hash_cn(&…)` so production logs carry a 48-bit PII-safe correlation token per MEMORY.md / shared-infrastructure.md convention, matching the existing pattern in `rustgate_transport::router`. Audit events that genuinely need the raw CN for compliance retain it via `rustgate_core::audit::*` functions. (F-153, #802)
- **Fail closed on stale store-forward queue without persistent HMAC key** — `rustgate_transport::ClientQueue::open` (and `open_with_hmac_key(.., None)`) previously generated a fresh random HMAC key on every invocation. When the on-disk queue already contained entries from a previous run, the new key could not verify the old tags, so every stored message was silently discarded on the next drain — a direct violation of the store-forward contract. `ClientQueue::open` now returns a new `StoreForwardError::StaleQueueWithoutKey` error when rebuilt-index depth is non-zero and no persistent key was supplied. The server binary gains two new flags to disambiguate operator intent: `--client-queue-key-file <path>` (env `RUSTGATE_CLIENT_QUEUE_KEY_FILE`) loads a 32-byte hex-encoded HMAC key subject to the same owner + 0600/0400 permission gate as the HTTP-egress secret files; `--client-queue-wipe-on-start` (env `RUSTGATE_CLIENT_QUEUE_WIPE_ON_START`, mutually exclusive with `--client-queue-key-file`) calls the new `ClientQueue::open_with_wipe` which clears the on-disk tables and emits a `store_forward.key_rotated` audit event (with `reason=explicit_wipe_on_start` and the pre-wipe depth) so the deliberate data loss is visible in the compliance audit trail. Operators who restart the server without either flag on a directory that contains stored entries will now see the server refuse to start instead of silently losing queued messages. (#740)
- **Owner check on HTTP egress secret files** — `rustgate_http::auth::read_secret_file` now verifies that the secret file's owning UID matches the running process's effective UID on Unix, in addition to the existing `mode & 0o077 == 0` permission-bit gate. Catches the misconfiguration where a secret is dropped by `root` with correct 0600 permissions but the service runs as a different user — the read would previously succeed silently, leaving operators unaware that the file is outside the service account's lifecycle. (#829)
- **Harden authz obligation actuation** — closes nine gaps surfaced by a post-merge security audit of the obligation data-plane feature (#717): (1) `MaskPoint` bypass via case variation, namespace prefix, or attribute-budget trip — the matcher in `rustgate_cot::mask` now strips optional `ns:` prefixes, does ASCII-case-insensitive comparison, and propagates attribute-budget errors so the caller fails closed instead of leaking coordinates; (2) `apply_mask_point` ignored `strip_coordinates` errors — now splits on payload intent (non-XML passes through with `recovery = "forwarded_unmasked"`, XML-intent parse failure erases the payload with `recovery = "dropped"`) and emits a new `authz.obligation_failed` audit record on every recovery path; (3) `send_to_session` persisted the *post-Receive* form of the payload to the store-forward queue, causing `TagClassified` audit records to fire for messages that never went over the wire and double-actuating Receive-side obligations on drain — now persists the pre-Receive form so `filter_offline_messages` re-evaluates the Receive PEP against the current policy snapshot; (4) unknown `Obligation` variants (the enum is `#[non_exhaustive]`) now emit an `authz.obligation_unknown` audit record so operators running a stale transport against a newer authz engine detect the skew via the audit trail. Both new audit events carry `destination_kind` (direct/group/broadcast/multicast) so SIEM rules can distinguish unicast from fan-out failures. (#734)
- **Advisory scan now gates the build** — removed `continue-on-error: true` from both the `cargo deny check` and `cargo audit` steps in the PR CI workflow. These guards were originally added to tolerate an upstream advisory-DB parse failure (RUSTSEC-2025-0138 malformed TOML), but once the underlying DB was fixed the guards stayed in place and silently masked every subsequent real advisory — a new CVE in a production dependency would have produced a yellow check mark instead of a failing build. The `--ignore` list for individually-reviewed advisories (RUSTSEC-2025-0134 unmaintained-no-replacement, RUSTSEC-2026-0049 mitigated-at-transport-layer) is preserved, each with a dated reviewer note; new suppressions require an explicit ignore entry and a rationale. A prerequisite `rustls-webpki` bump from 0.103.11 → 0.103.12 was required to land this change (fixes RUSTSEC-2026-0098 URI name-constraint bypass and RUSTSEC-2026-0099 wildcard name-constraint bypass). (#777)
- **Release signature gated on Trivy pass** — the `Release Docker Image` workflow previously ran the Trivy container scan *after* `cosign sign` with `continue-on-error: true`, meaning a CRITICAL or HIGH vulnerability in the shipped image did not fail the release: the image was pushed, signed, and announced regardless. Trivy now runs *before* `cosign sign` with `exit-code: '1'` and no `continue-on-error`, so a CRITICAL/HIGH finding short-circuits the job and leaves the image unsigned — consumers who require a cosign signature for admission control will correctly refuse to pull it. The SARIF upload step still runs after sign with `!cancelled()` so findings appear in the GitHub Security tab whether the release succeeded or failed, aiding triage. To ship an image despite a known finding, add a dated entry to `.trivyignore` with reviewer rationale rather than relaxing `exit-code` or re-introducing `continue-on-error`. (#777)

### Fixed

- **Deflake `sign_client_valid_csr` and sibling enrollment integration tests against server-startup race** — `rustgate_enrollment` integration tests spawn `EnrollmentServer::run` in a detached tokio task via `spawn_server_with`, which returns immediately before the server has bound its TCP listener. The `send_request` helper then called `TcpStream::connect(self.addr).await.expect("TCP connect")` with no retry, producing an intermittent `ConnectionRefused` on loaded CI runners — observed in issue-triage trails and reproduced in PR #977's test job. A new `connect_with_retry(addr)` helper at the top of `server_integration.rs` retries `TcpStream::connect` up to ~2 s (100 iterations × 20 ms backoff), panicking with the last error if the server never comes up; all six `send_request` call sites route through it via a single replacement of the raw `TcpStream::connect` call. This is purely a test-infrastructure fix — the enrollment server is unchanged — but the race touches every test in the file, so one fix unblocks the whole suite. Five consecutive local runs of the 10-test integration binary pass; before the fix, the race was visible under CI load. (#982)
- **Align federation JWT clock-skew tolerance with heartbeat timeout to stop `peer_connected` / `token_rejected` flap** — `rustgate_federation::jwt::MAX_CLOCK_SKEW_SECS` is raised from 60 s to 120 s so JWT `iat` validation no longer rejects tokens issued by a peer whose clock drifts inside the window where heartbeats still declare the link alive (`FEDERATION_HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT = 90 s`). Previously, any NTP drift between 60 s and 90 s produced an observable flap: the liveness check accepted each new mTLS session, the very next token refresh failed `iat > now + 60`, the connector tore the session down with `token_rejected`, reconnected, and re-flapped indefinitely — visible in the audit trail as an unbounded stream of `federation_peer_connected` / `token_rejected` pairs against the same peer CN. A new `const _: () = assert!(MAX_CLOCK_SKEW_SECS >= FEDERATION_HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT.as_secs(), ...);` compile-time guard pins the invariant so a future tweak to either constant cannot silently reintroduce the flap, and a runtime regression test (`clock_skew_exceeds_heartbeat_timeout`) plus a scenario test (`iat_at_heartbeat_timeout_drift_accepted`) assert that a peer drifting at the full heartbeat-timeout bound still has its tokens accepted. 120 s was chosen over 90 s (exact alignment) so the new tolerance has a 30 s safety margin above the heartbeat timeout — enough to absorb stateless jitter — without materially widening the replay surface compared to the old 60 s window; operators whose environment requires larger drift should also raise heartbeat timeouts, not just this value. (#764)
- **Unblock the docs-site build by dropping an unreachable intra-doc link in `rustgate-server`** — `cargo doc --workspace --no-deps` (the exact invocation in `.github/workflows/docs.yml` with `RUSTDOCFLAGS=-D warnings`) has been failing on every push to `main` since `rustgate-mqtt` was made an optional dependency of `rustgate-server`. The `MqttWildcardPolicy` doc comment in `crates/rustgate-server/src/cli.rs` included `[` `rustgate_mqtt::WildcardPolicy` `]` as an intra-doc link, but that crate is only in scope when the `mqtt` cargo feature is enabled — and the docs workflow builds with default features only, so rustdoc emits `unresolved link to rustgate_mqtt::WildcardPolicy` as a hard error under `-D warnings`. The link is demoted to a plain code span (`` `rustgate_mqtt::WildcardPolicy` ``) with a one-sentence expansion of the doc comment explaining why the cross-crate link cannot be used here. No behaviour change; restores green docs CI on `main`.
- **HTTP admission-queue drops no longer bypass the dead-letter queue** — `rustgate_http::HttpPublisher::publish` previously silently dropped messages when the bounded dispatch channel was full or closed; only circuit-open drops reached the DLQ. This left operators with no forensic trail for messages shed during load spikes or shutdown. The admission-full and admission-closed paths now persist the message to the DLQ under the synthetic target name `admission` (when the DLQ is configured) and increment the new `rustgate_http_dropped_total{reason}` counter. `TlsConfig::pin_sha256` docs are also expanded to explicitly state that pins hash the **entire leaf certificate DER** (not SPKI, so not RFC 7469); rotating a certificate always breaks the pin, which was previously under-documented. New pinning regression test `pin_is_full_leaf_der_not_spki_or_subject` codifies the semantic so a future migration to SPKI pinning cannot silently break deployed pins. (#813)
- **O(1) JWT revocation-cache eviction** — `rustgate_federation::jwt::JwtAuthority::revoke` replaced its `HashMap<String, u64>` + `min_by_key` full-scan eviction with an `lru::LruCache<String, u64>`, turning the `O(n)` per-call eviction into `O(1)` and eliminating the `O(n²)` worst case that made the cache a DoS amplifier on large revocation bursts at capacity. The old code also drained the oldest-expiring entry on every capacity hit — now LRU ordering naturally evicts the oldest-inserted token, which matches the original "min_by_key exp" semantic closely since tokens issued together share similar expirations. Expired entries are purged via an amortized sweep every 128 `revoke` calls (bounded O(n/128) cost), so stale `exp`s still leave the cache without per-call scan overhead. `validate()` uses the non-promoting `LruCache::peek` so a flood of valid validations against a revoked `jti` doesn't keep it at the front forever, preserving the eviction policy under read load. `with_revocation_capacity(0)` is coerced to `NonZeroUsize::MIN` at the type boundary (was previously `max(1, n)`). (#761)
- **Disable Nagle on every TCP socket to remove ~40 ms tactical-message latency** — rustgate's traffic pattern is small interactive messages (tactical CoT position updates are 200–800 bytes, federation control RPCs, WebSocket frames, MQTT CONNECT/SUBSCRIBE exchanges, enrollment request/response pairs). With Nagle's algorithm on and the peer's delayed-ACK timer at its Linux default (`TCP_DELACK_MIN = 40 ms`), a single small write followed by `poll_flush` routinely stalled a full RTT per message because the kernel held the outbound segment waiting for more data. A workspace-wide audit confirmed that `set_nodelay` was called **nowhere** — neither on listener-accepted sockets nor on client dial sockets — so every protocol adapter paid the penalty: TAK TLS and plaintext listeners, WebSocket and MQTT handshakes, federation gRPC dials, HTTP metrics scrapes, enrollment endpoints, and the `rustgate-client` library. `rustgate_core::net` gains `configure_tcp_nodelay(&stream)` and `configure_tactical_socket(&stream)` (the combined keepalive + `TCP_NODELAY` helper). Every call site that previously invoked `configure_tcp_keepalive` now calls `configure_tactical_socket` instead, so a future transport cannot add keepalive and silently forget Nagle-disable. The `rustgate-client` dial path — which had neither keepalive nor `TCP_NODELAY` before — gains both. A new integration test in `rustgate-transport::plaintext::tests` asserts `TCP_NODELAY = 1` end-to-end on an accepted socket so a regression fails CI instead of re-introducing the latency penalty. (#745)
- **Legitimate equatorial position reports no longer dropped by NATS publisher** — the "is this position unknown" filter in `rustgate_nats::publisher` previously matched `lat == 0.0 && lon == 0.0`, silently dropping every valid fix from the Gulf of Guinea and adjacent equatorial waters. The filter now keys on the `ce`/`le` (circular/linear 1-sigma error) fields instead, with a 1,000 km threshold that catches both the common TAK sentinel (`9999999`) and the MITRE reference-GUI sentinel (`999999999`) without a false positive on any realistic GPS/INS error. Earth's equatorial radius is ~6,371 km, so 1,000 km is both well above any legitimate error and below every observed sentinel. See the MITRE CoT Message Router User's Guide (MP090284) Table 2-1 and Figure 6-5 for the underlying spec. (#829)

### Changed

- **Reject out-of-range lat/lon per WGS-84 spec** — the CoT parsers in `rustgate-cot` and `rustgate-proto` now reject `<point>` elements whose `lat` is outside `[-90.0, 90.0]` or `lon` is outside `[-180.0, 180.0]`, matching the WGS-84 decimal-degree ranges defined in MITRE CoT Message Router User's Guide (MP090284) Table 2-1. Non-finite values (NaN, ±∞) are also rejected. `rustgate_cot::ParseError` gains an `OutOfRange { field, value }` variant and `rustgate_proto::ProtoError` gains a matching `OutOfRange { field, value }` variant. Previously these malformed coordinates parsed successfully and propagated through the router, allowing spoofed or corrupt tracks outside the Earth surface to reach downstream consumers. (#933)
- **Preserve CoT `<event version>` through the XML→JSON wire path** — `rustgate_proto::xml_full::FullCotEvent` now captures the `version` attribute on `<event>` (previously silently discarded), and `WireEvent::from_full_cot` in `rustgate-nats` emits that captured value on the JSON wire instead of hardcoding `"2.0"`. Required by MITRE CoT Message Router User's Guide (MP090284) Table 2-1, which lists `version` as a mandatory attribute. When the source XML omits the attribute, the JSON wire falls back to `"2.0"` so downstream consumers always see a conforming schema token. (#931)
- **Reject malformed CoT timestamps at XML parse time** — `rustgate_proto::xml_full::parse_full` now validates `<event>` `time`, `start`, and `stale` attributes as ISO 8601 / RFC 3339 UTC timestamps (`YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm:ss[.SSS]Z`) at parse time, using the same `iso8601_to_millis` validator the protobuf conversion path already relies on. Previously these attributes were forwarded as opaque strings through the XML→JSON NATS wire and the XML→protobuf conversion re-validated downstream, so a malformed timestamp could propagate into the routing layer before being rejected. Required by MITRE CoT Message Router User's Guide (MP090284) Table 2-1. Empty / omitted attributes are still accepted (the "attribute present" check remains orthogonal); only non-empty strings that fail to parse are rejected with `ProtoError::InvalidTimestamp("<field>: <reason>")`. The minimal `rustgate_cot::parse` (routing-focused, TTL-only use of timestamps) keeps its existing silent-fallback behavior — strict enforcement lives in the full-detail parser where downstream consumers actually care about timestamp validity. (#937)
- **Descriptive error and expanded docstring for ACL group-id validation** — `rustgate_server::acl_api::valid_group_id` now returns the human-readable rule (`"group id must be 1–128 bytes and contain only ASCII alphanumeric or `_`, `.`, `:`, `-`"`) to clients that submit a malformed group name, instead of the previous opaque `"invalid group id"`. The function's docstring is expanded to explain why the allow list is ASCII-only (downstream NATS subject tokenisation, case-insensitive router lookup, X.509 OU extraction, and structured-logging compatibility) and why the 128-byte cap aligns with `MAX_CN_LENGTH`. (#829)
- **Expanded `ClientConfigBuilder::server_name` docstring** — the builder method in `rustgate-client` now documents the full set of deployment scenarios where overriding the SNI / certificate-validation name matters (host-based routing via load balancer, multi-tenant SNI routing, test harnesses with mismatched SANs, wildcard-cert pinning), the default derivation rules (hostname from `server_addr`, `"localhost"` fallback for IP literals), and that the supplied value must be a valid DNS name. (#829)
- **Shared `CLIENT_MAX_RECONNECT_DELAY` constant** — `rustgate_client::ReconnectPolicy::default()` now sources its 30-second reconnect-backoff ceiling from a new `rustgate_core::constants::CLIENT_MAX_RECONNECT_DELAY` constant, mirroring the existing `FEDERATION_MAX_RECONNECT_DELAY`. Behaviour is unchanged; the move centralises the value alongside the other workspace-wide timeouts and prevents future drift if the default is ever tuned. (#829)
- **Bump `redb` from 2.6.3 to 4.0.0** — adopts the redb 4.x API. `begin_read` now lives on the `ReadableDatabase` trait (imported in `rustgate-transport::store_forward` and `rustgate-federation::replay_persist`). **File format note:** redb 3.0 dropped support for the v2 on-disk format. Existing store-and-forward and federation-replay databases written by redb 2.x may need to be cleared on upgrade; both are ephemeral queues that can be safely rebuilt.

### Added

- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-authz` policy JSON parser and validator** — `rustgate_authz::parser` (the sole entry point every operator-authored JSON policy traverses on startup and on SIGHUP hot-reload) previously had zero unit tests; the module was exercised only by downstream engine tests which primarily assert decision semantics, not the parse/validate contract itself. Issue #909 flagged this file because a regression here would either reject a correct policy (availability hit, taking authz offline) or silently accept an invalid one (safety hit, because `AuthzReloader` only honours the `version` / `rule-id-uniqueness` invariants this module enforces). The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `parser.rs` adds 16 unit tests pinning: (a) `POLICY_SCHEMA_VERSION == 1` so a version bump is a deliberate, grep-able event with a coordinated migration path rather than a silent inflation; (b) three `parse_json` happy paths (minimal `{"version": 1}`, explicit `default_effect: "allow"`, and a full one-rule policy with `cn_eq` subject and `any` resource) that prove the round-trip through `serde_json::from_slice` plus `validate` succeeds on real shapes; (c) three `parse_json` serde-level failure paths (empty input, malformed JSON, and — critically — a typo in the top-level `default_efect` field) that pin the `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` guard on `RuleSet`: a misspelled key must fail the parse rather than silently dropping to the `Deny` default and flipping an operator's intended `Allow` fallback into a `Deny` under their nose; (d) two `parse_json` version-invariant failures (`version: 0` and `version: 2` as a forward-incompatible proxy) that assert the error carries `stage = "version"` and the message names both the rejected and expected versions so operator diagnosis is unambiguous; (e) two `parse_json` rule-id failures (empty id and duplicate ids `shared`/`shared` — the silent-override failure mode this validator is most responsible for catching, since two rules with the same id would surface as one entry in every id-keyed audit record and decision-cache lookup) that assert `stage = "rules"` and the message echoes the offending id; (f) seven `validate()` direct-call tests covering the programmatic-construction surface (the reloader, benches, and future DSL → RuleSet pipelines enter via `validate`, not `parse_json`) — programmatically-built valid sets, empty rules list (a minimal but legitimate policy), `version = 0`, `version = u32::MAX`, empty id, duplicate ids with stage pinning + message-content checks, and a ten-distinct-ids sanity check that proves structural similarity between rules does not trip the duplicate detector; (g) an order-sensitive duplicate test with `[allow("r"), deny("r")]` that pins "the second occurrence must be the one flagged, regardless of effect pairing" — a bug that short-circuited on the first insert would let a later-winning rule slip through on the assumption that "the first one is fine". Two small test helpers (`rule_with_id` and `valid_empty_set`) keep each test focused on the one axis under test. No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code. (#909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-core` error types** — 27 new inline tests in `rustgate-core::error` pinning the operator-facing `Display` wording and source-chain contracts for every variant of `TransportError`, `ConfigError`, and the top-level `RustgateError`. Every Display string is asserted verbatim (static variants) or by both prefix and interpolated-field substring (variants with formatted fields), so a thiserror refactor that silently reworded `"TLS handshake failed: …"`, `"certificate missing required Extended Key Usage: …"`, `"unsupported TLS version '…': expected '1.2' or '1.3'"`, `"no <kind> found in PEM data"`, or `"failed to bind <addr>: <source>"` — all of which surface in audit events, CLI diagnostics, and operator triage output — would fail CI instead of drifting unnoticed. `std::error::Error::source()` is pinned for both directions: `TransportError::Bind` exposes its `io::Error` via the `#[source]` wiring (downcast-checked), and the static variants (`NoCertificate`, `EmptyCertChain`, `MissingCommonName`, `TlsHandshake`, `MissingKeyUsage`, `Other`, and all of `ConfigError`) pin `source() == None` so a future refactor that accidentally moves a `String` behind `#[source]` fails CI. The top-level `RustgateError` tests pin the subtle `#[error(transparent)]` contract — `Transport` and `Config` forward `source()` to the inner's `source()` rather than returning the inner itself — which is the exact semantic difference that determines whether `anyhow::Error::chain()` iteration and `Report`-style error-chain printing surface the underlying `io::Error` or stop one hop short. Also pins the `#[from]` conversions (`TransportError → RustgateError`, `ConfigError → RustgateError`, `io::Error → RustgateError`), the `?`-operator happy path for all three conversions, the non-transparent `"io: {0}"` prefix on the `Io` variant, and that the `Result<T>` type alias genuinely resolves to `std::result::Result<T, RustgateError>`. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-client` builder and TLS-material parsing** — `rustgate_client::ClientConfigBuilder::build()` and `rustgate_client::connect::build_tls_config()` were previously exercised only via the end-to-end tests in `crates/rustgate-client/tests/e2e.rs`, which means every validation path (required-field errors, plaintext vs. TLS enforcement, `server_name` derivation, codec / timeout defaults, Zeroizing wrapping of PEM-byte keys, custom-`Debug` redaction of TLS material, per-file-vs-per-byte PEM loading, malformed cert / key / CA rejection with stage-tagged error prefixes, missing-file `Io` propagation) was only proved indirectly and only on the happy path. `rustgate_client` is the reference integration surface for every downstream embedder, so a silent regression in the builder (e.g. a future refactor that stopped wrapping the PEM-byte key in `Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>` or that dropped the "tls or .plaintext(true)" guidance from the error message) could ship unnoticed. 20 new unit tests across `lib.rs` (12) and `connect.rs` (8) pin these contracts individually — the builder validators each get their own test, each `build_tls_config` error prefix is asserted with `starts_with("client cert:" | "client key:" | "ca cert:")` so mis-routed errors cannot silently pass, the `Codec::to_framed()` mapping is asserted for every enum variant so a new variant forces a conscious mapping decision, and the `ClientConfig` `Debug` impl is asserted to contain `<redacted>` and to not leak placeholder key bytes. The Cargo manifest picks up a `tempfile` dev-dependency via workspace inheritance to drive the `PemFiles` round-trip test against a real file descriptor. (#909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-transport` authenticated session** — `crates/rustgate-transport/src/session.rs` is the wrapper every TLS-authenticated client session flows through after `TlsListener::accept`, but had zero inline tests; `#909` flags it as a critical-path gap because a regression that cloned a fresh `Identity` into each half of `split()` would silently invalidate ACL enforcement on the write path (ACL keys off `identity().cn` and the two halves must agree). The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` adds 11 tests behind a shared `establish_test_session(client_cn, groups)` helper that drives a real `TlsAcceptor` / `TlsConnector` handshake against a loopback TCP pair using `TestCa`, extracts the CN + groups via the real `extract_identity` path, and constructs `AuthenticatedSession::new` the way the accept loop does. The tests pin: (a) `identity().cn` reflects the client-cert CN and `.groups` reflects the `OU` RDN decoding so a regression in the extraction plumbing surfaces here as well as in the listener, (b) repeated `identity_arc()` calls return Arc-pointer-equal clones (the session-scoped routing context assumes one allocation, not N), (c) `peer_addr()` returns the accepted TCP peer address and is a loopback address in the test harness, (d) `split()` hands both halves an `Identity` with identical CN and groups, (e) strong-count semantics after `split()` — the `Arc<Identity>` survives into both halves rather than being deep-cloned (pre-split Arc clone plus reader's Arc plus writer's Arc → strong count ≥ 3), (f) `Debug` for `AuthenticatedSession`, `SessionReader`, and `SessionWriter` each renders the type name so structured-logging sites that use `?session` keep producing useful output, and (g) a compile-time assertion that all three types retain `AsyncRead` / `AsyncWrite` + `Debug` bounds so a refactor that broke the framed-codec / `tokio::io::copy` contract fails at type-check time instead of at integration-test time. No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-server` session pure helpers (`codec_is_xml`, `apply_delta`, `DeltaConfig::build_encoder`)** — `crates/rustgate-server/src/session.rs` is the TLS read/write loop that every connected client funnels through but had zero inline tests; the `#909` epic flags it as a HIGH-PRIORITY gap. This PR pins the three pure-logic helpers embedded in the module without standing up a real TLS session. `codec_is_xml` is exercised against all three codec impls re-exported from `rustgate-transport` (`TakXmlCodec` → `true`, `RawLineCodec` and `RawLengthPrefixedCodec` → `false`) so a future codec whose `protocol_id()` regresses fails CI instead of silently disabling the delta encoder for TAK traffic. `DeltaConfig::build_encoder` is pinned across the `enabled=false` short-circuit, the happy enabled path, the `cache_size=0 → NonZeroUsize::MIN` coercion (explicit guard against the `NonZeroUsize::new(0) → None` footgun), and the minimal `cache_size=1` capacity. `apply_delta` is covered for all four observable outcomes: first-seen UID emits `DeltaDispatch::Send` with the original bytes (Full path), identical retransmission maps to `DeltaDispatch::Skip` (Suppressed path, fires regardless of epsilon per `rustgate_cot::delta` contract), a second event with changed position emits `DeltaDispatch::Send` containing a `<delta …>` wrapper (Delta path), and a non-CoT payload falls back to `DeltaDispatch::Send` with the original bytes (parse-failure → Full fallback). Tests live under `#[cfg(test)] mod tests { … }` in the binary crate (session.rs is binary-only, no lib target) and follow the workspace conventions: `#![allow(clippy::panic, clippy::unwrap_used, unused_results)]`, explicit `fn cot_event(uid, lat, lon, time) -> Bytes` fixture mirroring the shape in `rustgate_cot::delta::tests`, and `panic!("…")` with actionable context on every unreachable branch. 11 new tests, all green under `cargo nextest run -p rustgate-server` and clean under the full-feature clippy invocation. (#909)
- **Display and conversion-shape tests for `rustgate-authz::AuthzError`** — `crates/rustgate-authz/src/error.rs` previously had zero unit tests; every production caller reached it indirectly through `parser::validate` (the `Validation` variant) or the `#[from] serde_json::Error` conversion in `parse_json` (the `Json` variant), and the two load-bearing properties of the type — the `{stage}: {message}` Display template and the `?`-driven `From` conversion — were only covered transitively by parser tests that pinned the raw `stage` field, not the rendered string. Issue #909 flagged this file because a downstream audit pipeline greps the rendered Display output to distinguish "wrong schema version" (`stage = "version"`) from "bad rule id" (`stage = "rules"`), and an inlining refactor that dropped either tag from the template would silently break operator runbooks and SIEM alert rules without failing any existing test. The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` adds 8 tests pinning: (a) the `validation(stage, message)` ergonomics constructor — the only constructor parser / reloader / authz-reloader code uses — populates both fields correctly, (b) the `impl Into<String>` signature accepts both `&str` literals and owned `String` (the hot-path callers always pass `format!`-built `String`), (c) the `Validation` Display template carries the canonical `"policy validation failed"` prefix *and* surfaces both the `stage` tag and the `message` body verbatim (separate assertions for the `version` and `rules` stages because they feed different downstream alerts), (d) the `Json` variant is reachable through the blanket `From<serde_json::Error>` conversion (pinning the `?` path against a refactor that removed `#[from]`), (e) the `Json` Display starts with the distinguishing `"invalid policy JSON"` prefix operators use to separate parse failures from validation failures at a glance, (f) a compile-time `Send + Sync` assertion covering the reloader's `tokio::spawn` / `Arc` boundaries, and (g) a compile-time `let _: &'static str = stage;` type-pin that fails the build rather than silently widening the enum if a future refactor flipped the `stage` field to `String` and invited operator-supplied data into a tag downstream code treats as trusted. No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code with expanded module-level docs explaining why each invariant is worth pinning. (#909)
- **Serde-shape unit tests for `rustgate-authz` policy AST** — `rustgate_authz::policy` (the `RuleSet`, `Rule`, `Effect`, `ResourceMatcher`, `SubjectMatcher` types that every operator-authored JSON policy maps onto) previously had zero unit tests; coverage only came transitively through `parse_json` tests and the engine's decision-level tests, which smear a shape regression across many test failures rather than pinning the offending type. Issue #909 flagged this module because three load-bearing serde invariants spelled out in the existing doc comments had no direct regression guard: (1) `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on every struct/enum — the canonical footgun cited in `SubjectMatcher`'s doc is `{"groups_all_off": ["admin"]}` (trailing `f`) silently parsing into a `Default` all-match predicate and granting the surrounding rule to everyone, so pinning the deny-posture at the type level (not just the top-level `RuleSet`) defends against a refactor that removes the attribute from `SubjectMatcher`, `Rule`, or `ResourceMatcher` while leaving it on `RuleSet`; (2) `#[serde(tag = "kind", rename_all = "snake_case")]` on `ResourceMatcher` — every rule's `resource` field dispatches through the kind tag, and a regression that flipped to `rename_all = "PascalCase"` or dropped the tag entirely would silently change which JSON shapes match which variant; (3) the `default_effect_deny` helper — a minimal `{"version": 1}` policy must adopt a deny posture, not Effect's `#[derive(Default)]` (which does not currently exist but could be added and land as Allow if variants were reordered). The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `policy.rs` adds 28 unit tests covering: five `Effect` serde tests (lowercase `"allow"` / `"deny"` round-trip, title-case rejection, unknown-variant rejection with message-content check); three `RuleSet` default tests (omitted `default_effect` resolves to `Deny`, missing `rules` defaults to `[]`, missing `version` is rejected at serde level before the custom validator runs — pinning the error-message path); two `Rule` tests (unknown field inside a rule object rejected, `obligations` defaults to `[]`); eleven `ResourceMatcher` tests (each of the six variants round-trips, `Direct` with/without `target_cn`, `Federation` with/without `peer_id`, `Group` rejects a missing `group` name, missing `kind` tag rejected, unknown `kind` rejected, title-case `kind` rejected, misspelled inner field rejected); six `SubjectMatcher` tests (empty `{}` parses into the default, each of the five fields parses in isolation, `attribute_eq` accepts all four documented JSON value shapes — string/int/bool/array — in one go, the canonical `groups_all_off` typo is rejected by name, and any unknown top-level field is rejected); one `SubjectMatcher::default()` contract test (every field falls back to "no predicate" — a future refactor that added a default-different field like `require_mtls: bool = true` would silently change every in-memory matcher built via `Default::default()`); and one full-rule round-trip that exercises every sub-type together and proves Serialize and Deserialize agree on the wire shape. No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code. (#909)
- **Display-shape unit tests for every `rustgate-http` error variant** — `rustgate_http::error::HttpError` previously had zero unit tests, relying on downstream publisher / config / DLQ tests to exercise the variants transitively. That coverage was incidental — a refactor tweaking a `#[error(...)]` template would pass every existing test while silently breaking every operator runbook, SIEM filter, and tracing log line that greps for the canonical phrases (`"certificate pin mismatch"`, `"circuit breaker open"`, `"failed to read secret file"`, `"HTTP config validation"`). Issue #909 flagged this because the exact token shape is the contract between this crate and every downstream pipeline, but nothing in the crate enforces it. The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `error.rs` adds 14 unit tests covering: 9 per-variant Display tests that pin the distinctive prefix token (`HTTP config validation`, `HTTP response`, `HTTP request`, `circuit breaker open`, `certificate pin mismatch`, `DLQ error`, `failed to read secret file`, `failed to read TLS file`, `protobuf conversion failed`) and the interpolated fields (target name, status code, pin count, path, caller-supplied message), so a template change surfaces at CI instead of at 3am; 2 source-chain tests that pin `std::error::Error::source()` on `SecretRead` and `TlsFile` and downcast to `io::Error` to prove the underlying `io::ErrorKind` is reachable — dropping the chain (e.g. inlining the io::Error's message into the Display template and removing the `source` field) would keep the top-level message intact but break every `err.source()` traversal and every `tracing::error!(err = ?err)` printer; 1 Serialize-variant test that constructs a real `serde_json::Error` via failed parse and round-trips through `HttpError::Serialize`, asserting both the Display prefix and the downcast path; 1 Debug-formatting smoke test that pins the variant-tag prefix so `{:?}` emitters in debug tracing contexts have stable output; and 1 compile-time `Send + Sync` assertion so a future variant introducing a non-Send / !Sync field (e.g. a raw `Rc<...>`) fails at this module's test boundary rather than at every downstream `tokio::spawn` boundary in the server and federation crates. `ClientBuild` and `Request` (which wrap `reqwest::Error`) are not covered with new-struct tests because `reqwest::Error` has no public constructor; they remain exercised transitively through the publisher's real-network integration tests. No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code. (#909)
- **Evaluation-shape and decision-cache tests for `rustgate-authz::NativeEngine`** — `crates/rustgate-authz/src/engine.rs` previously had zero unit tests; the parser, policy, and compile modules pinned the *structural* correctness of the compiled form but the runtime evaluation contract (deny-first across kind-specific and `ResourceMatcher::Any` buckets, default-effect fall-through, obligation propagation on allow, decision-cache keying on the full `SubjectHash × resource_hash × action × policy_epoch` tuple, policy_id stability across instances, and the trait-method surface the router consumes via `Arc<dyn PolicyEngine>`) lived only in end-to-end integration tests that exercise the happy path through `router.rs`. Issue #909 flagged this file because a refactor to the scanner, the cache-key composition, or the any_resource fallthrough would be indistinguishable from a correct optimisation at the type level and only surface as a silent correctness regression in production. The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` adds 20 tests pinning: (a) `NativeEngine::empty()` is deny-by-default with a 64-character hex policy_id (the safe-fallback before first policy load), (b) an allow rule surfaces its `rule_id` for audit attribution, (c) a deny rule placed before an allow in the same bucket wins (the "explicit deny" pattern), (d) default_effect (both Allow and Deny) is returned when no rule matches, with `rule_id = None` on default-effect paths, (e) obligations propagate through to allow outcomes and are stripped from deny outcomes even when the rule is authored with them, (f) `ResourceMatcher::Any` matches Direct/Group/Broadcast/Federation/NatsSubject alike through the `any_resource` map, (g) a deny rule in `any_resource` short-circuits even after an allow has been found in a kind-specific bucket (the "global deny list" semantic), (h) the first allow within a bucket is the attributed rule_id, not the last, (i) the decision cache misses on first eval and hits on second for the same tuple, (j) the cache key folds in `resource.kind_tag()` so Broadcast vs Direct-to-Bob are distinct entries, (k) the cache key folds in `action` so Send-allow does not leak to Receive, (l) the `PolicyEngine::cache_stats` trait method returns `Some` for `NativeEngine` (the default is `None` so cacheless backends like Cedar can opt out) — the router uses this to gate Prometheus exports, (m) compile warnings for `allow + ResourceMatcher::Any` (#621) and `allow + empty subject matcher` (#622) surface through both the inherent `compile_warnings()` method and the trait-method view with identical contents, (n) a narrow well-scoped policy emits zero warnings (guard against spurious positives polluting operator logs), (o) policy_id differs across distinct rule sets and is stable across fresh `NativeEngine::new(rules)` constructions of the same canonical bytes, (p) `with_cache_capacity` is chainable off `new()` and the chained engine still evaluates correctly, (q) the `PolicyEngine` trait is object-safe and `Send + Sync + 'static` via a compile-time assertion and a boxed-trait construction (the router takes `Arc<dyn PolicyEngine>` so a non-object-safe method addition would fail here rather than at the distant call site), (r) distinct identities with the same rule-matching groups still produce distinct `SubjectHash` cache keys (evidence the cache actually keys on the 128-bit subject hash and not the CN pointer), and (s) attribute-equality predicates fire only for subjects carrying the attribute (wires `Subject::attributes` through to the compiled predicate). No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code with an expanded module-level docstring explaining why each invariant is worth pinning. (#909)
- **Display-shape and conversion tests for `rustgate-enrollment::EnrollmentError`** — `crates/rustgate-enrollment/src/error.rs` previously had zero unit tests; every fallible path in the enrollment crate (auth, CSR decode, signing, revocation, data-package serving, PKCS#12, EST endpoints, HTTP glue) returns this enum, and the 7 variant Display prefixes (`auth:`, `csr:`, `signing:`, `config:`, `tls:`, `io:`, `json:`) are the operator-facing grep tokens baked into the SIEM runbook and the HTTP response body surfaced to TAK clients. Issue #909 flagged this file because a refactor that inlined a prefix out of the `thiserror` template, or flipped one variant to a different prefix, would silently change the runbook contract without failing any existing test — the 43 call sites across `revocation.rs`, `signing.rs`, `datapackage.rs`, `est.rs`, etc. pin the variants they construct but not the rendered Display shape. The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` adds 12 tests pinning: (a) each of the 5 string-wrapping variants renders as `<prefix>: <message>` with the exact prefix token (separate asserts per variant because they feed different SIEM dashboards — `auth:` counts brute-force attempts, `csr:` vs `signing:` distinguishes client-sent-garbage from CA-side problems, `config:` is the startup-fatal bucket), (b) the `Io` variant is reachable via `#[from] std::io::Error` with the `io:` prefix surviving Display, and its `std::error::Error::source()` chain walks to the underlying `io::Error` with `kind()` preserved (without which `tracing::error!` field formatting and downstream `anyhow`-style downcasts would lose all error detail), (c) the same invariants for the `Json` variant via `#[from] serde_json::Error` — including the serde_json source chain that carries line/column detail operators rely on to locate malformed manifests, (d) the string-wrapping variants deliberately do *not* expose a source chain (pin the current contract so a well-meaning refactor that wraps them in a typed error wouldn't silently change the audit output shape), and (e) a compile-time `Send + Sync` assertion covering the axum handler boundaries, `tokio::spawn` paths, and `anyhow::Error` conversions in the server binary. No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code plus an expanded module-level docstring explaining why each grep token is load-bearing. (#909)
- **Unit-test coverage for federation REST API response helpers and URL percent-decoding** — `rustgate_federation::api::helpers` previously had zero direct unit tests; the module's four response constructors (`internal_error_response`, `json_ok`, `json_created`, `json_error`) and the router's homebrew path-segment decoder (`percent_decode`, `hex_byte`, `hex_nibble`) were exercised only incidentally through the handler integration tests in `api/mod.rs`, which treat the response shape as an implementation detail and never assert the exact `Content-Type`, status code, or envelope-key contract that API consumers actually depend on. Issue #909 flagged this file as critical-path-untested because the envelope shape (`{"data":…}` vs `{"error":{"message":…}}`) is a **public API contract** — flipping the key or the outer-wrapper shape is a silent backwards-incompatible break that the handler integration tests would not catch as long as the handler still returned *some* JSON with the expected values somewhere inside it, and the percent-decoder runs on every request's path segments before any handler dispatch, so a silent decode regression (e.g. dropping `%2F` → `/` support) would misroute requests to `{peer_id}` handlers. The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `helpers.rs` adds 16 unit tests pinning: (a) `internal_error_response` returns 500 with a `text/plain; charset=utf-8` body and fixed "internal error" payload, (b) `json_ok` returns 200 with `application/json` content-type and a top-level `data` key (not any other key a future refactor might accidentally introduce), (c) `json_ok` serializes an empty slice as `"data":[]` not `"data":null` — a regression on the empty-list envelope breaks pagination consumers that iterate without a null-check, (d) `json_created` returns 201 with the same `data` envelope key as `json_ok` so clients do not need variant-specific parsing, (e) `json_error` preserves caller-supplied status codes (400/404/500 are each round-tripped, pinning the contract that the status is an input, not derived from the message shape), (f) `json_error` emits `{"error":{"message":…}}` — the nested `error` object rather than a top-level `message` key, which disambiguates from success responses at the JSON-parse layer, (g) `json_error` preserves special characters in the message through JSON escaping (newlines, quotes, backslashes, non-ASCII) so structured audit data embedded in error messages survives round-trip, (h) `percent_decode` leaves unreserved characters (`A-Za-z0-9-_.~`) untouched, (i) `percent_decode` translates `+` to space per form-urlencoded convention (not per RFC 3986 path-segment rules — this is a deliberate leniency the test pins), (j) `percent_decode` decodes valid `%XX` escapes including `%20` (space), `%2F` (slash), `%3A` (colon), (k) uppercase and lowercase hex agree (`%2F` and `%2f` both decode to `/`), (l) truncated percent escapes at buffer end are handled without panic, matching the `clippy::panic = "deny"` posture, (m) non-hex bytes after `%` fall back to zero nibbles rather than panicking, (n) `hex_nibble` handles every digit and letter case (0-9, a-f, A-F), and (o) `hex_byte` combines nibbles in big-endian order (high nibble from first input, low nibble from second). All tests use `#[tokio::test]` for response-body consumers (needed because `Full<Bytes>` body collection is async) and `#[test]` for pure helpers. Production code is unchanged; all changes are additive test code. (#909)
- **Unit-test coverage for the Cedar policy backend's pure helpers and construction paths** — `rustgate_authz::cedar_backend` previously shipped integration tests in `tests/cedar.rs` that exercised the Cedar authorizer end-to-end via `Direct` + `Send` and pinned the fail-closed pathological-input contract, but the four private helpers that feed that evaluator — `stable_id` (policy_id computation), `hex_encode` (SHA-256 → lowercase hex), `action_name` (Action → Cedar literal), `resource_uid_str` (Resource → Cedar UID), and `escape` (CN/resource sanitisation) — had no direct coverage at all. Issue #909 flagged this backend as critical-path-untested because (1) it runs any time an operator selects Cedar over the native JSON backend, (2) the fail-closed contract depends on `escape` returning `None` on all-pathological input rather than folding onto a sentinel UID, and (3) the `Resource` / `Action` mappings are the only place where a policy author's literal (`Direct::"alice"`, `Action::"send"`) is decoded from an enum variant — a misrouted variant silently bypasses policy without any integration-test signal because the permit simply fails to match and every request denies by default (the correct failure mode, but indistinguishable from "no matching rule"). The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `cedar_backend.rs` adds 33 unit tests covering: (a) `stable_id` output shape (64-char lowercase hex digest, deterministic, distinct for distinct sources), (b) `hex_encode` nibble order and zero-padding on `[0x00, 0xff]` (the hand-rolled loop would otherwise regress to single-digit output on a `format!("{:x}", 0)`-style rewrite), (c) exhaustive `Action → &'static str` mapping with a distinctness check across all five variants so two Actions can never collide onto the same Cedar literal, (d) every `Resource` variant (`Direct`, `Group`, `Broadcast`, `Federation`, `NatsSubject`) produces the expected Cedar UID literal, and the empty-string + all-filtered paths return `None` (pins the fail-closed trigger at the uid-builder boundary, complementing the integration-level `cedar_pathological_resource_denies`), (e) `escape` filter rule coverage — empty → `None`, normal ASCII passes through, `"` + `\` + ASCII control chars are stripped, all-filtered input returns `None`, non-ASCII Unicode (Greek letters) is preserved, (f) `from_source` happy path + invalid Cedar source → `AuthzError` with the `cedar` target tag + empty-source parses and denies by default, (g) `from_file` nonexistent-path error path + happy path where the same bytes produce the same `policy_id` as `from_source` (pins the "thin wrapper, no encoding transforms" contract), (h) `with_entities` is a chainable builder that preserves `policy_id` (a regression that recomputed `policy_id` from `self.entities` would break every audit-log correlation query), (i) `evaluate` coverage expansion for `Broadcast`/`NatsSubject`/`Federation`/`Group` resource variants paired with `Subscribe`/`Federate`/`Receive` action variants, plus a strict-Action-comparison test where the permit uses `Action::"send"` but the evaluation uses `Action::Publish` and must deny, and (j) the manual `Debug` impl emits `policy_id` but does NOT leak any bytes of the Cedar source (pins the redaction contract so `{:?}` can never accidentally spill `permit(...)` bodies to a log aggregator). No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code. (#909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-mavlink` error types** — 17 new inline tests in `rustgate-mavlink::error` pinning the operator-facing `Display` wording, `std::error::Error::source()` contract, and the `From<MavlinkError> for io::Error` bridge that the codec's tokio glue relies on for `?`-propagation. Every Display string is asserted verbatim — including `{byte:02X}` uppercase-hex zero-padding on `InvalidMagic` (single-digit bytes like `0x05` must render as `"0x05"`, not `"0x5"`, so log greps keep working) and the twin rejection strings `"MAVLink v1 frame rejected by signing policy (v1 not allowed)"` and `"MAVLink v2 unsigned frame rejected by signing policy"` which surface directly into audit events when the codec enforces its default deny-v1 / require-signing posture. `FrameTooLarge { size, max }` pins both interpolated fields (including the zero-sentinel edge case that plumbing code sometimes passes). `source()` is pinned as `None` across every variant — a future refactor that moved one of the scalar fields behind `#[source]` would fail CI. The `From<MavlinkError> for io::Error` impl is pinned from three angles: every variant maps to `io::ErrorKind::InvalidData` (the codec's session-layer read loop uses `kind()` to route uniformly to the "drop session + audit" path); the original MavlinkError Display round-trips verbatim through `io::Error::to_string()` because the conversion stores the MavlinkError as the inner; the `io::Error::get_ref()` + `downcast_ref::<MavlinkError>` contract works so metrics code that wants to classify by variant doesn't have to re-parse Display. A separate test pins the subtle interaction between `io::Error::source()` (which forwards to the inner's `source()`, not returns the inner) and MavlinkError's own lack of `#[source]` fields — the chain correctly bottoms out at `None`, which is exactly what `anyhow::Error::chain()` iteration observes. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-federation::discovery::dns_sd`** — 9 new inline tests pin the DNS-SD peer-discovery backend's `ResolvedService → PeerConfig` conversion (the single place the mDNS wire format meets the rustgate peer model) plus the trait-level constants operators depend on. The converter is refactored from an `&self` method into an associated function `resolved_to_peer(service_type: &str, resolved: &ResolvedService)` — consistent with the sibling `ConsulDiscovery::entry_to_peer` in `consul.rs` — so the branches can be exercised without starting a `ServiceDaemon` on the test runner, which matters because the existing integration test at `crates/rustgate-federation/tests/discovery.rs` already self-skips on CI without working multicast. The new tests pin: (a) IPv4 address formatting (`10.0.0.1:9001`) and the IPv6 bracketing convention (`[fe80::1]:9002`), (b) IPv4 preference when both address families are advertised on the same service — guards the client-visible choice of address family from being silently flipped, (c) `fullname` suffix stripping that derives a stable operator-facing id from the advertised service (`alpha._rustgate._tcp.local.` → `alpha`), (d) the fallback path when `strip_suffix` fails (wrong service type supplied) — the full name is preserved rather than silently producing an empty id, (e) every default field applied to the resulting `PeerConfig` (`enabled=true`, `is_outbound=true`, `protocol_version=2`, `priority=100`, empty `inbound_groups` / `outbound_groups` / `trust_cert_cn` / `jwt_token` / `region` / `zone`, `routing_mode=Matrix`, and the `__ANON__` singleton in `groups`) so any drift in what the DNS-SD backend produces surfaces in CI rather than at runtime, (f) the `DEFAULT_GROUPS` constant shape (arity + value), (g) `backend_name()` returns the exact `"dns-sd"` string operators match on for metrics labels, (h) the `Debug` impl includes both the type name and the configured service type verbatim (required by support-log correlation), and (i) `discover()` returns an empty vec before any service is resolved (confirms the instance map is initialised empty and the `try_recv` drain does not produce a spurious error on an empty channel). The two tests that require a live `ServiceDaemon` self-skip with an `eprintln!` when the daemon cannot start, matching the established pattern for the integration test; the other seven run unconditionally on every CI matrix slot that enables `peer-discovery-dns-sd`. Also fixes two pre-existing test fixtures that were missing the `trust_peer_groups: false` field on `FederationConfig` literals (`src/discovery.rs` and `tests/discovery.rs`) — the `check-features` CI job uses `--no-dev-deps` so this never surfaced until now, but running `cargo test -p rustgate-federation --features peer-discovery-dns-sd` reproduces the `E0063` error on main. (Refs #909)
- **Unit-test coverage for XML-to-protobuf conversion helpers and attribute-escape injection defense** — `rustgate_proto::convert::xml_to_proto` previously had only round-trip coverage via `convert::mod.rs` (XML → proto → XML → parse-back-equivalence), which catches behavioural drift in the happy path but never directly tests the private helpers that do the actual conversion work. Issue #909 flagged this file as critical-path-untested because (1) `xml_escape_attr_into` is an **XML-attribute-injection defense** that runs on every attribute value an attacker might control (link uid, remarks text, marti callsign) and a silent regression that forgot to escape any of `&`, `<`, `>`, `"` would open a class of injection where crafted CoT fields close the attribute string early and inject arbitrary XML elements into the proto→XML round-trip output, (2) the timestamp-mapping branches in `full_to_cot_event` (`if full.time.is_empty() { 0 } else { iso8601_to_millis(...)? }`) are invisible to round-trip tests that always provide valid timestamps, and (3) the reserved-but-unmapped `caveat` / `releaseable_to` fields on `CotEvent` must initialize to empty strings rather than leaking stack contents or past-event state into downstream consumers — a contract that round-trip tests cannot verify because the XML side never carries these fields. The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` at the bottom of `xml_to_proto.rs` adds 44 unit tests pinning: (a) `xml_escape_attr_into` escapes each of `&`/`<`/`>`/`"` individually and all four on the same pass, preserves ordinary ASCII / non-ASCII Unicode / single-quote / tab / newline verbatim, and appends to (not overwrites) the caller's buffer — critical because the outer serializer builds a single output across many attribute writes, (b) `push_xml_attr` emits the leading-space-then-key-equals-quoted-value shape, handles empty values as `key=""` rather than malformed output, escapes values through `xml_escape_attr_into` so an attacker-controlled callsign containing `"` cannot break out of the attribute and inject new attributes, does NOT escape keys (keys come from source-code literals and escaping them would silently corrupt legitimate output), and appends to an existing buffer, (c) `serialize_typed_details_to_xml` is a no-op when no typed details are present (so buffers containing pre-serialized xml_detail are not accidentally corrupted), emits minimal `<link relation="…" type="…" uid="…"/>` shape with just the three required attributes, appends optional `parent_callsign` and `production_time` when present without reordering, preserves link ordering across multiple links, escapes hostile link uids (`X"><evil/>` → `X&quot;&gt;&lt;evil/&gt;`), emits empty remarks as `<remarks/>` (self-closing, not open/close pair — pins the wire-footprint contract), emits non-empty remarks as open/close with escape-through-all-four on the text, color with `argb` set emits `argb="…"`, color without `argb` emits bare `<color/>` (avoids accidentally dropping the tag entirely), labels / usericon emit their single-attribute shape, empty marti is self-closed, non-empty marti nests each callsign as `<dest callsign="…"/>` and escapes hostile callsigns, (d) `build_detail` preserves the original `xml_detail` as a prefix (legacy residual content must not be shuffled around typed elements), maps all typed fields when present, yields None for every typed field when absent, (e) `full_to_cot_event` returns `0` millisecond values for empty timestamp strings without invoking the parser, parses valid ISO-8601 through to specific millisecond values (`2021-02-27T20:32:24.000Z` = `1614457944000`), returns an error (not a silent zero) for garbage timestamps so operators see wire-format corruption, preserves string fields verbatim, preserves `f64` point coordinates within 1e-9, always leaves `caveat` / `releaseable_to` as empty strings regardless of source data, and always sets `detail: Some(...)` even for empty events (every downstream consumer relies on this), and (f) `xml_to_proto` as the public entry point round-trips a minimal `<event uid="U1" type="a-f-G"/>` through prost decode back to the original UID/type/zero-timestamp/Some(detail) shape, propagates `iso8601_to_millis` errors from malformed `time=` attributes, rejects non-XML payloads, and wraps the `CotEvent` in a `TakMessage` with `tak_control: None` (reserved for the separate streaming-control protobuf path, not for XML-origin events). All tests are additive; production code is unchanged. (#909)
- **Unit-test coverage for `rustgate-mqtt` error, config, and listener** — 30 new inline tests across three previously-zero-coverage files in the MQTT adapter. `error.rs` (16 tests) pins the `Display` output for every `MqttError` variant — these strings are rendered into operator-facing audit events and disconnect-reason fields, so a thiserror refactor that silently changed wording would now fail CI — plus trait-bound pins (`std::error::Error`, `Send + Sync`, `Debug` variant-name rendering) that protect the `Box<dyn Error>` and cross-task routing paths. `config.rs` (5 tests) pins `MqttServerConfig::DEFAULT_MAX_PACKET_SIZE = 16 MiB`, `DEFAULT_MIN_KEEP_ALIVE_SECS = 30`, and `DEFAULT_RESPONSE_CHANNEL_CAPACITY = 64` against silent drift, cross-checks the packet-size default against workspace-wide `rustgate_core::constants::MAX_FRAME_SIZE` so the MQTT and length-prefixed codec caps stay in lockstep, and asserts the `Debug` derive remains (required by `tracing::info!(?config, …)` expansions). `listener.rs` (9 tests, TestCa-backed) pin `bind` to `127.0.0.1:0` success, ephemeral-port assignment, loopback address shape, concurrent listeners on distinct ports, EADDRINUSE propagation when a second bind targets a live port (proving `SO_REUSEPORT` is not accidentally enabled), port release on drop followed by successful rebind to the same port (the user-visible outcome of `set_reuse_address(true)` in the bind path), and the operator-visible `Debug` shape including type name, `local_addr` / `conn_rate` field labels, and the concrete bound port value. (Refs #909)
