- **Unit-test coverage for MQTT protocol negotiation and packet framing** — `rustgate_mqtt::packet` previously had zero unit tests; the whole file was covered only by the single-process `mqtt_integration.rs` harness which exercises the happy-path CONNECT → SUBSCRIBE → PUBLISH round-trip but never the byte-level pre-handshake parser. Issue #909 flagged this file as critical-path-untested because (1) protocol negotiation runs on the very first bytes of every connection, (2) `try_parse_connect` is the only place where a raw TCP peer can hand us bytes before mTLS identity is even consulted, and (3) the `_ => return Ok(())` arm in `write_v4_to_buf` / `write_v5_to_buf` silently swallows unknown packet types as a deliberate DoS-hardening posture — a contract that is invisible at the type level and invites accidental inversion on a future refactor. The new `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `packet.rs` adds 16 unit tests pinning: (a) the 10-byte incremental-read threshold and the level-offset-beyond-buffer case both return `Ok(None)` without committing to a protocol level, (b) a non-CONNECT first packet (`\x30…` PUBLISH) is rejected with `MqttError::ConnectExpected` and leaves `protocol` unset, (c) legacy protocol level 3 (MQTT 3.1 / MQIsdp) is rejected with `UnsupportedProtocol(3)` so operators can triage via the raw error, (d) valid level-4 and level-5 CONNECTs negotiate `ProtocolLevel::V311` / `ProtocolLevel::V50` and fully consume the buffer, (e) post-handshake `try_parse` with a pinned protocol decodes PUBACKs via the correct version codec without re-entering the negotiator (a regression here would route every non-CONNECT v5 packet through the v4 parser), (f) insufficient bytes return `Ok(None)` and malformed frames return `MqttError::Malformed(_)` so the outer I/O loop has a clear "read more" vs. "abort session" dispatch, (g) `try_parse` with `protocol: None` delegates to the CONNECT negotiator even for non-CONNECT input so the first-packet-must-be-CONNECT invariant can never be bypassed by re-framing, (h) v4 CONNACK and PUBLISH round-trip cleanly through `mqttbytes::v4::read`, (i) the v4 server-only-silent-noop arm produces zero bytes for Connect / Subscribe / PingReq (pinning the contract so flipping to panic-on-unknown would fail CI), (j) v5 CONNACK round-trips with the `ConnAckProperties::new()` workaround for the mqttbytes 0.6 `None`-properties serialization bug, and (k) v5 Disconnect produces non-empty output with the correct fixed-header type (server-initiated disconnects would otherwise be indistinguishable from "client reset TCP" on the wire). The I/O-wrapping types (`PacketCodec::read_packet`, `PacketWriter::write_raw`) still live in integration-test territory since they own a live `TlsStream`; the new tests cover the pure byte-level logic underneath. No behavioural change — all changes are additive test code. (#909)
- **Accounted-bytes gauge for the federation replay buffer** — new `rustgate_federation_replay_buffer_bytes` Prometheus `IntGauge` exported via `rustgate-federation::metrics` (feature-gated on `api`, consistent with the rest of the federation metrics surface). The gauge publishes the redb-backed replay store's accounted on-disk footprint — the same `total_bytes` field that drives global drop-oldest eviction in `ReplayStore` — so operators can alert on buffer occupancy before it saturates the `--federation-replay-max-bytes` cap. Every mutation site in `ReplayStore` (`open`'s rehydrate path, `put`, `delete`, `clear_peer`) now calls a `publish_total_bytes` helper that reads the post-mutation total and updates the gauge; under the default no-`api` build the helper is a compile-time no-op so production builds without the management API pay zero overhead. `u64 → i64` conversion saturates at `i64::MAX` to satisfy the signed `IntGauge` contract without introducing a panic path — at the default 64 MiB cap this clamp is unreachable, and the convention matches the `peer_queue_bytes` gauge landed in #744. A new `replay_buffer_bytes_gauge_tracks_mutations` unit test walks the full lifecycle (empty-open → puts → delete → clear_peer → drop/reopen) asserting the gauge always mirrors `ReplayStore::total_bytes()`, so a regression that adds a new mutation site and forgets to publish fails CI instead of silently drifting. (#762)
- **ES256 JWT signing and multi-key verifier registry for federation** — `rustgate_federation::jwt::JwtAuthority` gains asymmetric signing via ECDSA P-256 SHA-256 (RFC 7518 §3.4 `ES256`) in addition to the existing HS256 HMAC path. New constructor `JwtAuthority::new_es256(pkcs8_der, kid, issuer)` accepts a PKCS#8 v1 DER private key (via `ring::signature::EcdsaKeyPair`) and registers its matching SEC1 uncompressed public key under the supplied `kid`. New builder methods `with_hs256_verifier(kid, secret)` and `with_es256_verifier(kid, sec1_pub)` register *additional* verifier-only keys so a peer can validate tokens signed by other peers without holding their private material — the core #760 failure mode (a compromised peer leaks the federation-wide HMAC secret) no longer applies when peers are issued independent ES256 keypairs and only exchange public halves. The JOSE header now carries `alg` (`HS256` / `ES256`) and, when a `kid` is configured, a `kid` field; a public `Algorithm` enum plus `signing_algorithm()` / `signing_kid()` accessors expose the signing state. `decode` looks up the verifier by `kid`, falls back to a legacy (no-`kid`) HS256 verifier when the header omits `kid`, and **rejects tokens whose header `alg` disagrees with the resolved verifier's algorithm** — a direct defense against the classic JWT alg-confusion attack (header `alg: HS256` with a `kid` pointing at an ECDSA verifier would otherwise interpret the ECDSA public key bytes as an HMAC secret). Wire-format backward compatibility: an HS256 authority with no configured `kid` emits the byte-identical `{"alg":"HS256","typ":"JWT"}` header older releases produced, pinned by a regression test. `kid` values are validated as 1–64 ASCII printable bytes (no control characters, no non-ASCII). Private keys (HS256 secret, ES256 PKCS#8) remain wrapped in `Zeroizing`; the ES256 keypair is boxed inside the `SigningKey` enum to keep the enum compact. 14 new tests cover round-trip, cross-authority verification via registered verifier, header shape, alg-mismatch rejection, malformed PKCS#8 / SEC1 inputs, unknown-`kid` rejection, short secrets, and invalid-`kid` guards. Partial close of #760 — Ed25519 (RFC 8037 `EdDSA`), JWKS URL fetching, and the rotation-window state machine land in a follow-up issue.
- **Per-tenant queued-byte quota primitive** — `rustgate_transport::quota::TenantQuota` gains a `max_queued_bytes: u64` field (0 = unlimited) alongside the existing `max_connections`, and `TenantQuotaTracker` gains `try_reserve_bytes(tenant, bytes) -> Option<TenantBytePermit>`, `release_bytes(tenant, bytes)`, and `queued_bytes(tenant) -> usize` to bound the bytes a single tenant can enqueue in addition to the global `BackpressureGate`. `TenantBytePermit` is an RAII guard that releases the reservation on drop (saturating subtract, never underflows); reservation uses an `AtomicUsize` CAS loop on the same per-tenant `TenantCounters` record that tracks connections, so reservation is TOCTOU-free and lock-free on the hot path. Callers that already hold a global `BackpressurePermit` can now additionally hold a `TenantBytePermit` to prevent one noisy tenant from exhausting the shared byte budget; wiring the permit into the router's enqueue path and the HTTP/NATS/federation publishers lands in a follow-up PR. 18 new unit tests cover admission, rejection, drop-release, saturation, unknown-tenant fall-through, and independence from the connection counter. (#744)
- **Per-peer outbound queue depth gauge for federation** — new `rustgate_federation_peer_queue_bytes` Prometheus `IntGaugeVec` (labelled by peer id) exported via `rustgate-federation::metrics` (feature-gated on `api`, consistent with the rest of the federation metrics). The gauge is refreshed from `PeerHandle::record_enqueue`, `record_dequeue`, and `reset_queue`, so operators can alert on per-peer outbound-queue growth before the v2 client's reconnect buffer starts shedding. `u64 → i64` conversion saturates at `i64::MAX` to stay panic-safe on the theoretical overflow path. (#744)
- **HTTP egress queue depth gauge wired end-to-end** — the pre-existing `rustgate_http_queue_depth` gauge declared in `rustgate-http::metrics` was defined but never updated. `HttpPublisher::publish` now `inc()`s on successful `try_send` into the worker channel, and `dispatch_worker` `dec()`s on every recv (both the steady-state `recv()` branch and the shutdown-drain branch). Matches the accounting model already used by the federation peer-queue gauge, so dashboards can now plot HTTP egress backpressure the same way. (#744)
- **MQTT SUBSCRIBE wildcard filters (`+` and `#`)** — `rustgate_mqtt` now honours MQTT 5.0 §4.7.1 wildcards in SUBSCRIBE topic filters, with per-listener policy gating via the new `--mqtt-wildcard-policy` flag (`RUSTGATE_MQTT_WILDCARD_POLICY`). Three settings: `reject` (pre-wildcard behaviour, no `+` or `#`), `single-level-only` (new default — accepts `+` such as `fleet/+/position` but rejects the `#` firehose), and `full` (accepts both `+` and `#`). The adapter enforces the MQTT level-boundary rules at validation time (`+` must occupy a full level; `#` must be the final level and preceded by `/` or be the entire filter). Wildcard filters are expanded at SUBSCRIBE time against the client's certificate-derived `Identity.groups` set — the router never synthesises new groups from a wildcard, so the certificate remains the sole authorisation boundary. UNSUBSCRIBE symmetrically leaves every matching group. `/` is now an accepted character inside rustgate group names so multi-level MQTT topics (`fleet/a/position`) round-trip cleanly; the PUBLISH path continues to reject `+` and `#` per the MQTT spec. (#755)
- **CoT delta encoder LRU eviction counter** — `rustgate_cot::delta::DeltaStats` gains an `evicted` field, and the feature-gated `rustgate_cot_delta_evicted_total` counter (emitted from `rustgate-server::metrics`) increments on every LRU displacement of a distinct UID (same-key updates do not count). Operators can now alert when the configured `cache_size` is undersized for the current subscriber population; previously the displacement manifested only as missing full-event baselines on the receiving side, with no signal on the emitter. (#830)
- **Oversized CoT payload counter and short-circuit** — `DeltaStats` gains an `oversized` field paired with a new `rustgate_cot_delta_oversized_total` counter, and payloads larger than `MAX_SCAN_BYTES` (64 KiB) are now short-circuited before the parse step so an adversarial client cannot pad `<detail>` with whitespace or dummy elements to defeat delta compression and inflate bandwidth. `MAX_SCAN_BYTES` is promoted to `pub const` so callers can set framing limits or alert thresholds against the same value. Oversized payloads do not mutate the per-UID snapshot cache. (#830)
- **Fuzz coverage for MAVLink framing, CoT masking, and obligation actuation** — adds three new targets to the nightly and weekly-extended fuzz matrices so the three most hostile-input-facing codecs that previously ran without coverage are now driven by libFuzzer on every CI run: `mavlink_v2_frame` drains the byte-level v1/v2 parser in a loop to exercise the resync-past-garbage path, `pending_total_len` carry-over, unknown-INCOMPAT rejection, and the 280-byte cap; `cot_mask_strip_coordinates` feeds arbitrary bytes to `rustgate_cot::mask::strip_coordinates` and asserts idempotence on successful output so a serialisation regression surfaces as an oracle crash; `obligation_apply` drives the full `rustgate_transport::obligations::apply_obligations` dispatch with structured `Arbitrary` input (`RoutableMessage` via the `fuzz` feature + a bytes→variant mapping for `Obligation`) and asserts the "MaskPayload anywhere in the list → payload is empty on exit" invariant so a regression that short-circuited on MaskPoint before MaskPayload ran would leak the body. Hand-crafted seed corpora cover the interesting code paths (magic-byte resync, signed vs. unsigned v2, namespaced `<cot:Point>`, attribute-budget trip, `MaskPayload` sandwiched between other obligations). (#780)
- **Typed `<event how>` taxonomy classifier** — `rustgate_cot` gains a `HowOrigin` enum (`Machine` / `Human` / `Other`) plus `how_origin(&str)` and `how_is_spec_conformant(&str)` helpers for CoT `how` attribute inspection per MITRE MP090284 Appendix C. `how_origin` is a permissive classifier that keys on the `m-*` vs `h-*` first segment for observability (metrics, dashboards, SIEM filters), and `how_is_spec_conformant` is a strict exact-match check against the canonical value list (`m-g`, `m-g-a`, `m-g-n`, `m-f`, `m-n`, `m-r`, `m-i`, `h-e`, `h-g-i-g-o`, `h-t`, `h-s`, `h-p`, `h-c`). Deliberately **not** wired into any PEP, audit, or metric in this release — the TAK ecosystem tolerates non-canonical `how` values and rejecting them at parse time would break interop. Callers that want to emit a structured warning for non-conforming values can now opt in. (#938)
- **JSON-array escape hatch for multi-group client certificates** — `rustgate_transport::extract::extract_groups` now accepts a JSON string array (e.g. `["R,D","ops"]`) in a single `OU` RDN *in addition* to the existing comma-separated syntax, so certificates issued for groups whose names legitimately contain commas (departments like "R,D", fused org units, locales) survive round-tripping through the X.509 OU. The comma-separated path remains the default; the JSON branch triggers only when the OU value starts with `[` and parses as `Vec<String>`. Malformed JSON falls back to comma splitting. (#829)
- **Shared resilience primitives crate** (`rustgate-resilience`) — new workspace member consolidating the circuit breaker, backoff, and rate-limiting building blocks that were previously duplicated (or near-duplicated) across `rustgate-http`, `rustgate-federation`, and `rustgate-core`. Modules: `circuit::CircuitBreaker` (three-state with probe limiting and configurable success threshold), `retry::{backoff_with_jitter, is_retryable_status}` (jittered backoff for concurrent request-level retries), `reconnect::{backoff_delay, should_stop_reconnecting}` (deterministic backoff for long-lived client reconnections), `token_bucket::TokenBucket` (per-target rate limiter with lazy refill), `conn_rate_limiter::ConnRateLimiter` (per-IP sliding-window connection limiter, relocated from `rustgate-core::ratelimit`). Transport-agnostic — no `reqwest`, `tonic`, or `rustls` leaks in. `metrics` feature exposes internal state accessors for future Prometheus gauges. Downstream imports update from `rustgate_core::ratelimit::ConnRateLimiter` → `rustgate_resilience::conn_rate_limiter::ConnRateLimiter`. Closes #897.
- **MAVLink v1/v2 framing crate** (`rustgate-mavlink`) — new workspace member providing the byte-level frame parser and a `rustgate_core::codec::Codec` adapter for MAVLink wire frames. The framer handles both v1 (`0xFE`) and v2 (`0xFD`) frames on the same link, disambiguated by magic byte, with automatic resynchronisation past corrupted bytes, spec-mandated rejection of unknown INCOMPAT flag bits, and a hard cap at the 280-byte signed-v2 maximum. Frames are carried opaquely through the router — CRC verification and v2 signature checking (with a replay-timestamp table) land in a follow-up PR. `rustgate-transport` gains a new `mavlink` feature that wires `MavlinkFrameCodec` into the generic `FramedCodec` dispatcher; new `MessageType::Mavlink` variant, `SOURCE_MAVLINK = "mavlink"` constant, and `FramingStrategy::Mavlink` let audit logs, store-and-forward envelopes, and echo-prevention filters distinguish MAVLink traffic without re-parsing the payload. No session listeners yet — UDP and TCP wiring arrive in the next PR of the epic.
- **Authz obligations actuated in the dataplane** — the router now acts on the `mask_point`, `mask_payload`, and `tag_classified` obligations carried on Allow decisions, alongside the existing `audit_extended`. `mask_point` zeroes CoT `<point>` coordinates via a new `rustgate_cot::mask::strip_coordinates` helper (the obligation is named `MaskPoint` to reflect its scope — only the `<point>` element is touched, not `<detail>` children or envelope metadata); `mask_payload` replaces the payload body with empty bytes and coerces the classified message type to `other:masked` while preserving the envelope; `tag_classified` emits the new `audit.classified_delivery` audit event per *delivered* recipient (not per authorized recipient — the audit is deferred until after a successful channel send so a backpressure drop never produces a phantom delivery record). Obligations are applied at every PEP site — Send pre-fanout, Receive per-recipient, store-forward drain, and SA position replay — so a policy change never leaks raw coordinates via an offline queue or a cached position. `docs/authorization.md` documents the residual metadata leak vectors that masking does NOT scrub (source identity, destination CN, envelope timestamps). (#716)
- **HTTP egress bridge** (`rustgate-http` crate): configurable webhook/REST forwarding to multiple independent HTTP endpoints. Features include:
  - **Authentication**: Bearer token, HTTP Basic, and HMAC-SHA256 request signing with timestamp binding for replay prevention. All secrets loaded from files with `Zeroizing` memory.
  - **Outbound mTLS**: Per-target custom CA, client certificates, and optional SHA-256 certificate pinning (defense against CA compromise/misissuance).
  - **SSRF protection**: Dual-layer defense — private/loopback IP rejection at config validation time and DNS-time resolution checks (prevents DNS rebinding attacks).
  - **Payload formats**: JSON envelope with base64 payload, raw XML passthrough, and protobuf via `rustgate-proto` (feature-gated behind `proto`).
  - **Per-target filtering**: AND-combined filters across groups, message types, and source CNs. HTTP echo prevention is always active.
  - **Resilience**: Exponential backoff with jitter, three-state circuit breaker (closed/open/half-open) with configurable probes and success thresholds, and per-target token-bucket rate limiting.
  - **Dead-letter queue**: Messages shed by open circuit breakers are persisted to ChaCha20-Poly1305 encrypted files for forensic recovery, with per-target file rotation at configurable size limits.
  - **Observability**: 7 Prometheus metrics (feature-gated behind `metrics`), OpenTelemetry trace context propagation (feature-gated behind `otel`), structured audit events, and Content-Digest / delivery-ID request headers.
  - Configure via `--http-config-file` TOML file. See [docs/http-egress.md](docs/http-egress.md).

## [0.8.0] — 2026-04-11

### Added

- **Helm StatefulSet for persistent store-forward** — new `storeForward.enabled` value switches the workload from a Deployment to a StatefulSet with a `volumeClaimTemplate` for the redb-backed client queue. A headless Service provides stable DNS for pod identity. Configurable via `storeForward.storageSize` (default 5Gi), `storeForward.storageClassName`, and `storeForward.queueDir` (default /data/client-queue). (#669)
- **Security-focused Grafana dashboard** — new `rustgate-security.json` dashboard covers authorization decisions, eval latency, cache hit ratio, store-forward queue health, backpressure, deduplication, connection security, and JEmalloc memory stats. Existing overview, transport, and federation dashboards updated with panels for recently added metrics. (#670)
- **Typed CoT detail field extraction** — the full-detail parser now extracts link, remarks, color, labels, usericon, and marti detail elements into typed structs instead of leaving them in raw XML residual. Gated behind the existing `full-detail` feature flag. (#667)
- **Authorization engine fully wired into message routing** — verified authz enforcement across all routing paths: direct send, group send, broadcast, store-forward drain re-evaluation, and transport adapters (WebSocket, MQTT, UDP). Added integration tests confirming policy changes take effect on queued message delivery. (#668)
- **TAK Protocol v2 negotiation** — the protocol handshake now supports version range negotiation. The server advertises support for v1 and v2, and selects the highest mutually supported version with each client. Backward compatible with v1-only clients. (#665)
- **SA multicast group routing** — new `Destination::Multicast` variant enables sending messages to multiple groups simultaneously. SA (Situational Awareness) event type detection helpers (`is_sa_event`, `sa_affiliation`) identify friendly/hostile/neutral/unknown atoms from CoT event types. Configurable via `MulticastConfig` on the Router. (#666)
- **Security integration tests** — end-to-end tests for TLS handshake rejection (expired certs, cross-CA certs), store-forward authorization re-evaluation on policy change, and non-blocking delivery guarantees (slow consumer isolation, circuit breaker disconnect). (#650)
- **Multi-region federation with latency-aware routing** — `PeerConfig` gains `region`, `zone`, and `priority` fields. Per-peer EWMA latency is tracked from heartbeat RTTs. Matrix-mode routing prefers same-region, lowest-latency peers when multiple peers serve the same group. New `--federation-region` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_FEDERATION_REGION`). Broadcast mode is unchanged (all peers). (#672)
- **Per-tenant resource quotas** — Identity now carries a `tenant` field extracted from the certificate Organization (O) subject field. New `TenantQuotaTracker` enforces per-tenant connection limits with live tracking. Quota configuration is loaded from a JSON file via `--tenant-quota-file` (env `RUSTGATE_TENANT_QUOTA_FILE`). Tenants without a specific entry fall back to `default_quota`. A Prometheus counter `rustgate_tenant_quota_rejections_total` tracks enforcement actions. (#673)
- **Jemalloc memory statistics** — when the `jemalloc` feature is enabled, the metrics endpoint now exposes four Prometheus gauges: `rustgate_jemalloc_resident_bytes`, `rustgate_jemalloc_active_bytes`, `rustgate_jemalloc_allocated_bytes`, and `rustgate_jemalloc_mapped_bytes`. Values are refreshed on every `/metrics` scrape via the safe `tikv-jemalloc-ctl` API (epoch advance + stat reads). Operators can use these to monitor allocator-level memory usage, detect fragmentation (allocated vs active), and set alerts on resident growth. Requires `--features jemalloc,metrics`. (#674)

### Security

- **Case-insensitive CN matching in ACL** — `GroupAcl` now normalizes all certificate Common Name strings to ASCII lowercase at construction time and before lookup, matching the case-insensitive semantics required by RFC 5280 §7.1. Previously, an attacker with a differently-cased CN (e.g. `CN=Admin` vs. `admin`) could bypass ACL rules. (#652)
- **Default-deny for unconfigured groups when ACL policy is loaded** — `GroupAclPolicy` now denies send and receive for groups that have no explicit ACL entry when at least one other group is configured. Previously, unconfigured groups defaulted to open, which could silently expose groups omitted from the policy file. When no policy is loaded at all (empty map), the router continues to operate in open mode for backward compatibility. (#653)
- **Federation peer identity from TLS certificate** — federation server now extracts peer identity from the mTLS client certificate CN instead of the remote IP address, preventing impersonation behind NAT/load balancers. Falls back to IP with a warning when certificates are unavailable. (#655)
- **Federation hop count validation** — inbound federated events with a hop count exceeding the configurable maximum (default 8) are dropped with a warning, preventing routing loops between federation peers. (#657)
- **ACL re-check on store-forward delivery** — messages drained from the offline queue are now filtered through the current ACL and authorization policies before delivery. This prevents messages stored under a previous policy from being delivered after the sender's permissions have been revoked. (#651)
- **Per-session non-blocking delivery** — direct and broadcast message delivery now uses non-blocking channel sends (`try_send`), preventing a single slow consumer from starving all other sessions. Messages to full channels are persisted for offline delivery (direct) or dropped (broadcast) instead of blocking the routing task. The global backpressure gate is retained for overall memory protection. (#656)
- **Federation peer group validation** — validate federation peer group membership on inbound messages; peers can only inject events into groups they are explicitly authorized for. When no per-peer group allowlist is configured, all messages pass (backward compat). (#658)
- **Verify client identity on EST re-enrollment** — the `POST /.well-known/est/simplereenroll` endpoint now verifies that the CSR subject CN matches the authenticated username (case-insensitive). Previously, re-enrollment used the same flow as initial enrollment without identity verification, allowing a client to request a certificate with a different CN than their authenticated identity. Mismatched requests are rejected with HTTP 403 Forbidden. (#659)
- **Metrics endpoint authentication** — add optional bearer token authentication to the Prometheus `/metrics` endpoint via the new `--metrics-token-file` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_METRICS_TOKEN_FILE`). When set, requests to `/metrics` must present `Authorization: Bearer <token>` matching the file contents; the token is compared in constant time (`subtle::ConstantTimeEq`) and stored in `Zeroizing` memory. When unset, the endpoint remains unauthenticated for backward compatibility. Health probe endpoints (`/health/*`, `/healthz`, `/readyz`) are never gated by the token. (#664)
- **HMAC-SHA256 integrity verification for store-forward messages** — each message persisted to the on-disk client queue is now tagged with an HMAC-SHA256 computed from a per-process ephemeral key. On drain, the tag is verified before decoding; tampered envelopes are discarded with a warning. Protects against filesystem-level attackers modifying stored messages while the server is running. Callers that need cross-restart verification can supply a stable key via `ClientQueue::open_with_hmac_key`. (#660)
- **Delivery dedup for store-forward queue on restart** — drained messages are now recorded in a `delivered_seqs_v1` redb table before removal from the queue. On restart, `ClientQueue::open` skips and purges entries found in the delivered table, preventing duplicate delivery when the process crashes between drain and caller acknowledgement. (#661)
- **RUSTSEC-2026-0002 — bump `lru` to 0.16.3** — the `lru` crate versions `>=0.9.0, <0.16.3` contained a soundness issue where `IterMut::next`/`next_back` temporarily created an exclusive reference to the key, invalidating the shared pointer held by the internal `HashMap` and violating Stacked Borrows. rustgate transitively pulled in `lru 0.12.5` via `rustgate-transport`. The workspace dependency has been bumped to `0.16.3`, which contains the upstream fix. (#582)
- **CSR signature verification** — documented and tested that CSR self-signatures are verified before certificate issuance, preventing forged-subject attacks. rcgen's `CertificateSigningRequestParams::from_pem()` internally calls `x509_parser` signature verification, rejecting CSRs where the public key does not match the self-signature. (#654)
- **UDP transport hardening** — add per-IP rate limiting, source CIDR allowlist, and identity cap to UDP transport to prevent flooding and spoofing attacks. All restrictions are opt-in for backward compatibility. (#662)
- **SLSA provenance and Sigstore signatures in release assets** — binary release archives are now accompanied by `.sigstore.json` cosign bundles (previously `.bundle`, unrecognized by OpenSSF Scorecard) and SLSA Build Level 3 provenance attestations (`.intoto.jsonl`) generated by the `slsa-framework/slsa-github-generator`. These changes improve the OpenSSF Scorecard "Signed-Releases" check from 0 to 10.

### Fixed

- **Federation TTL re-check at forward boundary** — the federation manager now re-evaluates each message's TTL immediately before queuing it to peer outbound channels via the new `FederationManager::forward_with_metadata` API. Messages whose `received_at + ttl` has elapsed between local routing and federation forwarding are dropped with a warning instead of consuming peer bandwidth. A new `rustgate_federation_forward_ttl_expired_total` Prometheus counter (under the `api` feature) tracks drops, and the `federation` snapshot endpoint exposes the same value as `forward_ttl_expired`. The legacy `forward(payload, groups)` entrypoint is preserved for callers that have no TTL context. (#554)
- **Store-and-forward ordering, async safety, and naming** — several correctness bugs in the per-client store-and-forward queue (#548): (1) the reconnect sequence now drains the offline queue **before** registering the session with the router, so live traffic routed mid-handshake can no longer slip ahead of queued messages (FIFO across the reconnect boundary is now strict across TLS, plaintext TCP, and WebSocket transports); (2) `ClientQueue::enqueue` no longer performs `redb::begin_write` + `commit` on the async router worker — persistence is handed off via a bounded `tokio::sync::mpsc` channel to a dedicated writer task that commits inside `spawn_blocking`, with a new `rustgate_client_queue_writer_drops_total` counter for overflow; (3) `peek_cn` was renamed to `peek_dest_cn` with corrected docs, and on reopen non-direct (broadcast/group) entries are now skipped and counted via `rustgate_client_queue_skipped_unsupported_total` rather than warned as malformed; (4) TTL is now enforced on drain, skipping expired entries and incrementing `rustgate_client_queue_expired_total`; (5) new `rustgate_client_queue_enqueues_total` counter surfaces the offline write rate; (6) the router's `send_to_session` failure log was corrected from "session channel full or disconnected" to "session channel closed" since `tokio::mpsc::Sender::send().await` only errors on receiver close.

### Changed

- **Rust toolchain aligned to 1.94** — updated `rust-toolchain.toml`, CI workflows, and `Cargo.toml` MSRV to consistently target Rust 1.94.
- **`/readyz` response format changed from plain text to JSON** — the `/readyz` (and `/health/ready`) endpoint now returns a structured JSON body `{"status": "ok"|"degraded"|"unhealthy", "checks": {...}}` with per-dependency health status instead of a bare `ok`/`not ready` string. Clients that previously parsed the plain-text body must be updated to consume the new JSON format. (#675)
- **GroupAcl default semantics restored** — an ACL entry with an empty allow list and empty deny list again denies all sends and receives, matching the pre-deny behavior. The deny-rule extension (#523) accidentally flipped the empty allow list to mean "open", which silently relaxed existing policies. Block-list mode (empty allow, non-empty deny) is unchanged. (#538)
- **Audit log append mode** — the `--audit-log` file is now opened with create+append instead of truncated, preserving existing audit events across restarts. (#538)

- **`is_match_all()` future-proofing tests** — exhaustive destructuring tests for `CompiledSubjectPred::is_match_all()` that cause a compile error when a new field is added without updating the method and per-field assertions. (#649)
- **`--ws-max-write-buffer-bytes` CLI flag** — operators can now tune the per-connection WebSocket slow-consumer write buffer limit at runtime instead of recompiling. Accepts values from 64 KiB to 64 MiB; defaults to 4 MiB (matching prior hardcoded behavior). Also configurable via `RUSTGATE_WS_MAX_WRITE_BUFFER_BYTES` env var or TOML config file. (#647)
- **Authz compile warnings on health endpoint** — the metrics HTTP server now exposes `GET /health/authz`, returning a JSON object with `policy_id` and `compile_warnings` from the installed authorization engine. Operators can query this endpoint to inspect overly broad rules (e.g. allow + resource `any`, empty subject matchers) without scraping logs. The `PolicyEngine` trait gains a `compile_warnings()` method with a default empty-slice impl so existing backends are unaffected. (#648)
- **Health probe with dependency checks** — the `/readyz` and `/health/ready` endpoints now return structured JSON with per-dependency health status instead of a bare `ok`/`not ready` string. The response body is `{"status": "ok"|"degraded"|"unhealthy", "checks": {...}}` where each registered dependency reports its own status and optional message. Dependency checks include: TLS certificate loaded and not expired (warns at 7 days before expiry), NATS connection state (feature-gated on `nats`), and federation peer connectivity (feature-gated on `federation`). Returns HTTP 200 for `ok` or `degraded`, 503 for `unhealthy`. The `/healthz` and `/health/live` endpoints remain simple liveness probes (200 if the process is reachable). (#675)
- **Documentation site scaffold (GitHub Pages)** — new `docs-site/` mdBook project and `.github/workflows/docs.yml` GitHub Actions workflow publish a structured public documentation site to GitHub Pages on every push to `main`. The site organizes getting-started guides, architecture, operations runbooks, API reference, security analysis, and a research section (why rustgate, comparison to alternatives, exploit resistance matrix, benchmarks, and downloadable papers). The workflow also builds `cargo doc --workspace --no-deps` and mounts it at `/api/rustdoc/` alongside the book. Preprocessors wired in: `mdbook-mermaid` (diagrams), `mdbook-admonish` (callouts), `mdbook-toc` (per-page TOC), and `mdbook-linkcheck` (CI link validation). MathJax is enabled for research pages. The initial commit seeds the exploit-resistance matrix and the "why rustgate" advocacy page with real content; remaining pages are stub placeholders to be filled in subsequent PRs.
- **MQTT 5.0/3.1.1 transport adapter** — new `rustgate-mqtt` crate adds an MQTT transport with mTLS client authentication. Supports CONNECT/CONNACK handshake with protocol-level negotiation, PUBLISH (QoS 0 and 1, QoS 2 rejected), SUBSCRIBE with dynamic group membership via `Router::join_group`/`leave_group`, UNSUBSCRIBE, PINGREQ/PINGRESP, and clean DISCONNECT. MQTT topic names map bidirectionally to rustgate routing groups, with an optional configurable prefix (`--mqtt-topic-prefix`). Certificate OU fields serve as the authorization boundary — clients can only subscribe to groups listed in their certificate. New CLI flags: `--mqtt-bind` (env `RUSTGATE_MQTT_BIND`), `--mqtt-topic-prefix` (env `RUSTGATE_MQTT_TOPIC_PREFIX`), `--mqtt-max-packet-size` (env `RUSTGATE_MQTT_MAX_PACKET_SIZE`, default 16 MiB). Gated behind the `mqtt` feature flag. (#567)
- **Per-message RBAC/ABAC authorization** via the new `rustgate-authz` crate. Policies are loaded from a JSON file with deny-by-default semantics, hot-reloaded on file changes (last-good preserved on validation failure), audited on every deny and reload outcome, and exposed via Prometheus metrics. A sharded LRU keyed on policy epoch evaluates in <100ns P99 on cache hits, well under the 10µs budget enforced by a per-PR criterion-based perf gate. Optional Cedar policy backend behind the `cedar` feature. New CLI flags: `--authz-policy-file`, `--authz-policy-format`, `--authz-reload-interval`, `--authz-fail-open`. Identity now carries a SPIFFE ID and an attribute bag derived from mTLS cert SANs and subject fields. Closes #563.
- **Per-UID CoT delta encoding for outbound XML sessions** — CoT track sources emit periodic position updates where the bulk of each event body is unchanged between pings. Rustgate can now track the last-sent event per `uid` on a per-writer basis and emit a compact `<delta>` wrapper carrying only the fields that changed, saving bandwidth on dense position workloads. Opt-in via the new `--cot-delta-encoding` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_COT_DELTA_ENCODING`, default off); disabled by default so existing deployments are unaffected. The encoder cache is bounded per writer via `--cot-delta-cache-size` (env `RUSTGATE_COT_DELTA_CACHE_SIZE`, default 512 entries, LRU eviction) and position-only drift below `--cot-delta-epsilon-meters` (env `RUSTGATE_COT_DELTA_EPSILON_METERS`, default 1.0 m ground distance) is suppressed entirely so a jittery GPS cannot flood subscribers. The first event for any UID is always emitted in full so new subscribers reconstruct state from an empty cache automatically; immutable attribute drift (`version`, `type`, `how`) also forces a full re-emit. Only XML-framed sessions (protocol id `tak-xml`) are wrapped — protobuf, length-prefixed, and line-delimited sessions pass through unchanged. Four new Prometheus counters (`rustgate_cot_delta_full_total`, `rustgate_cot_delta_delta_total`, `rustgate_cot_delta_suppressed_total`, `rustgate_cot_delta_bytes_saved_total`) are registered under `--features metrics` so operators can compute the compression ratio and suppression rate. See `docs/cot-delta-encoding.md` for the wire format. (#568)
- **Dynamic federation peer discovery (DNS-SD, Consul, etcd)** — the federation manager can now resolve its peer list from three external service registries instead of a static `--federation-peers-file`. New `--federation-peer-discovery <none|dns-sd|consul|etcd>` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_FEDERATION_PEER_DISCOVERY`) selects a backend, and `--federation-peer-discovery-interval` (env `RUSTGATE_FEDERATION_PEER_DISCOVERY_INTERVAL`, default 30 s) sets how often a background reconciler re-polls the source and feeds the result into the existing `FederationManager::swap_peers` API from #556 — so adds, removes, and replays all go through the same zero-downtime path as the file reloader. The DNS-SD backend (`--federation-peer-discovery-dns-sd-service`, default `_rustgate._tcp.local.`) browses an mDNS service type and materialises each resolved instance as an outbound peer. The Consul backend (`--federation-peer-discovery-consul-addr`, `--federation-peer-discovery-consul-service`) queries `/v1/catalog/service/<name>` on any Consul HTTP endpoint. The etcd backend (`--federation-peer-discovery-etcd-endpoints`, `--federation-peer-discovery-etcd-prefix`) performs a prefix scan and accepts either raw `host:port` values or serialized `PeerConfig` JSON per key. A new public `PeerDiscovery` trait makes the design pluggable so operators can bring their own source. The `--federation-peer-discovery` flag is mutually exclusive with `--federation-peers-file`. Each backend ships behind its own sub-feature (`peer-discovery-dns-sd`, `peer-discovery-consul`, `peer-discovery-etcd`) with an umbrella `peer-discovery` feature that enables all three, so minimal images only pay for the registry they actually use. Selecting a backend whose feature was not compiled in fails fast at startup instead of silently ignoring the setting. (#571)
- **OTLP metrics export alongside Prometheus** — new `--otlp-metrics` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_OTLP_METRICS`, disabled by default) enables pushing rustgate's existing Prometheus metrics over OTLP gRPC to the same collector endpoint already configured for traces via `--otel-endpoint` (env `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`). A companion `--otlp-metrics-interval` flag (env `RUSTGATE_OTLP_METRICS_INTERVAL`, default 60 seconds, minimum 1) controls the push cadence. Implemented as a non-invasive bridge: on startup, the OTEL meter walks the Prometheus default registry and attaches one observable instrument per metric family whose callback re-gathers values (including newly-added per-identity label sets) on every collection cycle. Counters map to `f64_observable_counter`, gauges to `f64_observable_gauge`, and histograms/summaries surface as paired `<name>_sum` / `<name>_count` observable counters. The existing `/metrics` scrape endpoint is unchanged — OTLP export is purely additive. Pending metrics are flushed on graceful shutdown so the final batch reaches the collector. Requires `--features otel,metrics`. (#572)
- **SPIFFE / SPIRE workload identity (initial)** — new `spiffe` feature flag (disabled by default) lets rustgate fetch its server TLS certificate, private key, and trust bundle from a SPIRE agent via the SPIFFE Workload API instead of from static files. New CLI flags: `--spiffe` (env `RUSTGATE_SPIFFE`) enables the identity source; `--spiffe-workload-api-socket` (env `RUSTGATE_SPIFFE_WORKLOAD_API_SOCKET`, default `unix:///tmp/spire-agent/public/api.sock`) selects the Workload API endpoint; `--spiffe-trust-domain` (env `RUSTGATE_SPIFFE_TRUST_DOMAIN`) optionally enforces a specific trust domain; `--spiffe-refresh-interval` (env `RUSTGATE_SPIFFE_REFRESH_INTERVAL`, default 300 s) controls the periodic SVID refresh. `--spiffe` is mutually exclusive with `--cert`/`--key`/`--ca`/`--enrollment-bind` at the clap level. The initial X.509-SVID is fetched at startup and seeded into the existing `CertReloader` via a new `CertReloader::from_der` constructor and atomic `CertReloader::update_from_der` path, so subsequent refreshes reuse the same zero-downtime rotation primitive used by file-based hot-reload. A background task re-polls the Workload API on the configured interval and updates the active `CertifiedKey` in place. Two new Prometheus metrics (feature-gated on `metrics` + `spiffe`): `rustgate_spiffe_svid_refresh_total{outcome="success|failure"}` counter and `rustgate_spiffe_svid_expiry_seconds` gauge (unix seconds). Structured `target="audit", event_type="spiffe.svid_refresh"` audit events are emitted on every refresh outcome with `spiffe_id`, `trust_domain`, and `expiry_unix` fields. Depends on the `spiffe` crate (Apache-2.0). Client-side SPIFFE ID extraction (using a connecting peer's URI SAN as its routed identity in place of the X.509 CN) and dynamic trust-bundle rotation are deferred to a follow-up PR; connecting clients continue to authenticate via CN on the existing path. (#570)
- **Grafana dashboards, Prometheus alert rules, and recording rules** — new top-level `ops/` directory ships five importable Grafana dashboards (`rustgate-overview`, `rustgate-transport`, `rustgate-federation`, `rustgate-nats`, `rustgate-enrollment`) using a `${DS_PROMETHEUS}` datasource variable, plus `ops/prometheus/alerts.yml` with alert families covering handshake failure ratio, admission gate saturation, client queue drops, federation peer lag / queue bytes / circuit breaker trips / forward TTL expirations / total peer disconnection, NATS consumer pending / redelivery storms / consumer-info latency, and enrollment rate limiting, and `ops/prometheus/recording-rules.yml` with ten aggregations (message rate, handshake error ratio, federation peer lag, NATS consumer pending, etc.) to speed up dashboard queries. An `ops/README.md` documents the layout, import flow, and rule-file wiring. (#573, #574)
- **Federation frame parser fuzz target** — new `federation_frame_parse` cargo-fuzz target that drives arbitrary bytes through the V1 federation read pipeline (4-byte big-endian length-prefixed codec) and runs `is_heartbeat` on each decoded payload, mirroring the inbound peer read loop. Also exercises the heartbeat fast-path classifier directly on raw input. (#575)
- **EST (RFC 7030) enrollment endpoints** — new `est` feature flag (disabled by default) adds Enrollment over Secure Transport endpoints to the enrollment HTTPS server: `GET /.well-known/est/cacerts` returns the CA certificate chain as a PKCS#7 certs-only bag (RFC 5652), `POST /.well-known/est/simpleenroll` accepts a base64-encoded PKCS#10 CSR and returns the issued certificate plus CA chain as a PKCS#7 certs-only bag, `POST /.well-known/est/simplereenroll` handles re-enrollment (same flow), and `GET /.well-known/est/csrattrs` returns 204 No Content. All responses use base64 per RFC 4648 Section 4 as clarified by RFC 8951. EST endpoints are gated behind the new `--est-enabled` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_EST_ENABLED`) and share the existing HTTP Basic auth, per-IP rate limiting, and `CsrSigner` infrastructure. Labeled EST paths (`/.well-known/est/{label}/cacerts`) are supported for multi-CA deployments. New dependencies: `cms`, `der`, `x509-cert` (all optional, only pulled when `est` feature is enabled). (#569)
- **Client certificate OCSP verification (Tier 1)** — new `--client-ocsp` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_CLIENT_OCSP`) wraps the active client cert verifier with a SKI-keyed OCSP cache. On a cache miss the inner verifier's decision still stands (fail-open) and a background task fetches an OCSP response via the existing `ocsp-stapler` HTTP client so the *next* handshake for the same certificate can be answered from the cache; cached `Revoked` responses cause the handshake to be rejected before the session is established. Provides near-real-time client revocation as a complement to CRLs, which only refresh on a schedule. Two new Prometheus counters track cache effectiveness: `rustgate_client_ocsp_cache_hits_total` and `rustgate_client_ocsp_cache_misses_total` (both registered under `--features metrics`). Requires `--features ocsp`. (#555)
- **Hot-reloadable federation peers** — the federation peer set can now be reconciled at runtime without restarting the server. New `--federation-peers-reload-interval` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_FEDERATION_PEERS_RELOAD_INTERVAL`, default 0 = disabled) enables periodic polling of `--federation-peers-file`, and SIGHUP also triggers a reconcile when the reloader is configured. File changes are detected via SHA-256 hash comparison; only modified files trigger a swap. New peers in the file are connected, peers no longer in the file are gracefully disconnected (their outbound clients shut down, replay buffers cleared, HA claims released when applicable), and peers whose configuration changed are reconnected with the new settings. Peers whose configuration is unchanged are left untouched — existing connections are preserved. New `FederationManager::add_peer`, `FederationManager::remove_peer`, and `FederationManager::swap_peers` APIs back the reloader and return a `PeerSwapDiff` summarising added/removed/unchanged/failed peers. Structured `target="audit", event_type="config.reload", component="federation_peers"` events are emitted on success and failure. Closes the gap left by the SIGHUP-driven ACL reloader, where adding or removing a federation partner still required a full restart. (#556)
- **Encrypted-at-rest CoT archive and audit log** — new `--archive-key-file` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_ARCHIVE_KEY_FILE`) enables streaming ChaCha20-Poly1305 encryption of the CoT event archive (when `--archive-dir` is set) and the JSON audit log file (when `--audit-log` is a path). The key file is a small JSON document `{ "active_key_id": "<id>", "keys": { "<id>": "<64-char hex>" } }` where each key is 32 bytes; key material is held in `zeroize::Zeroizing` buffers so it is wiped on drop. Each output file gains a fixed header (magic `RGENC1\0\0` + version + key id + 8-byte random base nonce) followed by length-prefixed AEAD chunks; the per-chunk nonce is the base nonce concatenated with a 32-bit chunk counter, and any single-byte tampering fails AEAD verification on read. Per-chunk AAD binds the key id and chunk counter to detect chunk reordering, and a sealed terminator chunk is written on close so silent tail-truncation is detectable on read. Key rotation is supported by adding a new key id and switching `active_key_id`: historical files written under prior keys remain readable as long as the old key is still listed. A new `rustgate decrypt-archive --in <path> --out <path> --key-file <path>` subcommand round-trips encrypted files back to plaintext for inspection or export. (#561)
- **Persistent federation replay buffer** — federation per-peer replay buffers can now be mirrored to a redb database on disk so they survive server restarts. New `--federation-replay-dir` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_FEDERATION_REPLAY_DIR`) enables persistence by pointing at a directory; the store file is `<dir>/federation_replay.redb`. Total on-disk usage is bounded by `--federation-replay-max-bytes` (env `RUSTGATE_FEDERATION_REPLAY_MAX_BYTES`, default 64 MiB) with global drop-oldest eviction across peers. On startup, the federation manager rehydrates each peer's in-memory ring buffer from the store before accepting connections, so a peer that reconnects immediately after a restart resumes replay from its last persisted point instead of seeing an empty buffer. Records whose TTL has already expired at rehydration time are skipped (counted by `rustgate_federation_replay_restored_skipped_ttl_total`) so persistence cannot resurrect messages that should have been dropped. Disabled by default; existing in-RAM-only behavior is unchanged when the flag is not set. (#557)
- **Per-peer federation lag metrics** — two new Prometheus gauges track outbound queue lag for each federation peer: `rustgate_federation_peer_queue_bytes{peer="..."}` exposes the bytes currently queued for delivery, and `rustgate_federation_peer_oldest_seconds{peer="..."}` exposes the age (in seconds) of the oldest message still waiting to be sent. Counters are maintained on the `PeerHandle` (atomic byte counter + FIFO of enqueue timestamps) and updated by both the V1 framed and V2 gRPC client send paths. A 1 s background updater task in the metrics layer snapshots the federation manager and republishes the gauges, removing labels for peers that are no longer tracked. Gated on the `metrics` + `federation` features. (#564)
- **Differentiated Kubernetes health probes** — the metrics HTTP server now exposes three independent probe endpoints aligned with the Kubernetes startup/liveness/readiness model: `GET /health/live` returns 200 once the metrics server is reachable (failing probes restart the pod), `GET /health/ready` returns 200 only when the listener is bound and core subsystems have completed initial wiring (flips back to 503 during graceful shutdown so load balancers stop routing new traffic), and `GET /health/startup` returns 200 only after the accept loop has been entered (one-shot, never reset, allowing slow-starting pods extra grace before liveness/readiness begin in earnest). The legacy `/healthz` and `/readyz` paths remain as deprecated aliases for backwards compatibility. The bundled Helm chart is updated to use all three probes with sensible thresholds. (#562)
- **JetStream consumer lag metrics** — the NATS pull-consumer subscriber now polls `consumer.info()` every 5 seconds and forwards `num_pending`, `num_redelivered`, and `ack_floor.stream_seq` to a caller-supplied callback. Under `--features metrics,nats` these are exported as the gauges `rustgate_nats_consumer_pending`, `rustgate_nats_consumer_num_redelivered`, and `rustgate_nats_consumer_ack_floor_stream_seq`, plus the histogram `rustgate_nats_consumer_info_seconds` for per-poll latency, so operators can alert on cross-instance NATS bridge lag and redelivery storms. Transient `consumer.info()` errors are logged and retried on the next tick instead of killing the poller. The `rustgate-nats` crate stays Prometheus-free; the binary registers the metrics and wires the callbacks. (#565)
- **CoT archive retention and rotation** — operators no longer need an external `cron` job to keep `--archive-dir` bounded. Two new CLI flags configure retention: `--archive-max-size <bytes>` (env `RUSTGATE_ARCHIVE_MAX_SIZE`, default `0` = unlimited) caps total bytes across the archive directory and deletes the oldest files (by mtime) until the total drops below the cap; `--archive-max-age <seconds>` (env `RUSTGATE_ARCHIVE_MAX_AGE`, default `0` = unlimited) deletes files older than the configured age. A background task runs the rotation pass on a fixed interval, configurable via `--archive-rotate-interval` (env `RUSTGATE_ARCHIVE_ROTATE_INTERVAL`, default 60s). The currently active in-progress archive segment is excluded from both size accounting and deletion to avoid racing the writer. The new `rustgate_archive_bytes` Prometheus gauge (registered under `--features metrics`) reports the current total size of the archive directory after each pass. Both retention bounds default to disabled to preserve existing behavior. (#560)
- **Graceful connection drain on shutdown** — new `--shutdown-drain-timeout` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_TIMEOUT`, default 10 seconds) bounds how long the server waits for in-flight queued messages to be flushed to clients during graceful shutdown. After listeners stop accepting on SIGTERM, the new `Router::close_all_session_senders` primitive drops every active session's outbound senders so each writer task observes a clean EOF after draining whatever is currently buffered in its priority and normal channels. The TLS, plaintext TCP, and WebSocket write loops were also fixed to keep draining the *other* channel when one of the two priority channels closes — previously a biased `tokio::select!` would `break` on the first `None`, silently dropping any messages still buffered on the other side at drain time. Setting the flag to `0` skips the drain phase and exits immediately. (#553)
- **Configurable enrollment CSR signing rate limit** — the enrollment server's per-IP CSR signing throttle is now exposed via the new `--enrollment-rate-limit` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_ENROLLMENT_RATE_LIMIT`, default 10 requests per source IP per minute). Rejected requests continue to return HTTP 429 with a `rate limit exceeded` body and emit a structured `rate_limit.triggered` audit event. A new `rustgate_enrollment_rate_limited_total` Prometheus counter is registered under `--features metrics` and incremented from a callback wired into `EnrollmentServer::with_rate_limit_callback`. Setting the flag to 0 disables the throttle (not recommended). (#552)
- **Hash-chained, tamper-evident audit log** — when `--audit-log <path>` is set, every audit record is now augmented with `prev_sha256` and `sha256` fields before being written. Each record's `sha256` is computed as `SHA-256(prev_hex_bytes || canonical_json_without_chain_fields)`, with the genesis record using 64 zero hex characters as `prev_sha256`. The chain transparently resumes across process restarts by reading the last line of the existing log. A new `rustgate verify-audit --file <path>` subcommand re-reads the file, recomputes the chain end-to-end, and exits non-zero on the first mismatch with a precise diagnostic (line number, expected vs actual hash). A small `write_anchor` helper writes the current chain tip to `<path>.anchor` so external systems can pin the chain on rotation. Tampering with any record in the middle of the file is now detectable. (#559)
- **Persistent de-identified access log** — new `--access-log` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_ACCESS_LOG`, `"-"` for stdout) opts into a per-message access log written as JSON lines to a dedicated sink. Implemented as a `tracing-subscriber` layer that filters `target="access"` events, mirroring the existing `--audit-log` plumbing. `Router::route` now emits one structured event per routed message with fields `src_cn`, `dst` (`direct:<sha256-cn-hash>` for unicast — the destination CN is hashed and truncated to 12 hex chars to avoid leaking PII — `group:<id>`, or `broadcast`), `msg_uid` (extracted from CoT XML payloads via a bounded scan, empty for non-CoT), `size`, `proto` (source transport), and `outcome` (`delivered`, `dedup`, or `expired`). Emission is gated behind `tracing::enabled!`, so when no access subscriber is attached the cost is a single O(1) filter check. (#558)
- **Harden NATS subject sanitization** — `sanitize_nats_token()` now replaces `.` (NATS subject separator) with `_` to prevent subject injection, strips non-ASCII characters instead of rejecting the entire input, strips whitespace and control characters, and caps token length at 256 bytes. Inputs that sanitize to empty strings return `None`. (#663)
- **Audit log integrity** — operators can now prove that their audit log has not been silently edited or truncated by running `rustgate verify-audit --file <path>` against the configured `--audit-log` file. (#559)

- **Global admission control / backpressure** — new `--max-queued-bytes` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES`, default 512 MiB) caps the total in-flight routed bytes tracked across all sessions. When the counter crosses the high-water mark, the TLS accept loop is **deferred** (not errored) until the counter drops back below the low-water mark, which defaults to 90% of the high-water value and is overridable via `--max-queued-bytes-low-water`. Implemented as a shared `BackpressureGate` (AtomicUsize + `tokio::sync::Notify`) wired into `Router::route` via an RAII permit that releases its byte reservation on drop, making accounting panic-safe. Two new Prometheus metrics track the gate: `rustgate_queued_bytes` (gauge) and `rustgate_admission_deferred_total` (counter). Prevents a flood of slow readers from exhausting server memory on top of the existing per-session circuit breakers. (#551)
- **Per-client store-and-forward with bounded disk queue** — messages destined for a disconnected CN are now persisted to an embedded `redb` (MIT/Apache-2.0) database and drained on reconnect before live traffic resumes. Opt-in via the new `--client-queue-dir` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_CLIENT_QUEUE_DIR`); disabled by default, in which case offline messages continue to be dropped as before. A global byte cap configured via `--client-queue-max-bytes` (default 100 MiB, env `RUSTGATE_CLIENT_QUEUE_MAX_BYTES`) bounds total on-disk usage with drop-oldest FIFO eviction across all CNs. The queue survives server restarts: the in-memory index is rebuilt from the existing database on open. Prometheus metrics `rustgate_client_queue_depth`, `rustgate_client_queue_bytes` (gauges labelled by bucketed `identity_cn`), `rustgate_client_queue_drops_total`, and `rustgate_client_queue_drains_total` (counters) are registered under `--features metrics`, with CN label cardinality bounded by the existing `--metrics-identity-allowlist` mechanism. (#548)
- **Cross-path message deduplication** — new `--dedup-window-secs` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_DEDUP_WINDOW_SECS`, default 0 = disabled) enables a router-level LRU dedup cache keyed by `SHA-256(uid || 0x00 || time_le || 0x00 || payload)`. When the same logical message arrives via multiple ingress paths (e.g. a direct client and a federation peer that forwarded it) within the configured window, the router suppresses duplicates before dispatch so subscribers receive each message exactly once. For CoT XML the `uid` attribute is extracted from the payload; for non-CoT payloads (protobuf, raw binary) `uid` is empty and the key degenerates to a pure content hash, so byte-identical protobuf/binary payloads are deduped as well. The cache size is bounded via `--dedup-cache-size` (env `RUSTGATE_DEDUP_CACHE_SIZE`, default 100000); evictions follow LRU order, and entries older than the window are lazily replaced on lookup. Suppressed duplicates are counted by the new `rustgate_dedup_hits_total` Prometheus counter, and current occupancy is exposed via `rustgate_dedup_cache_size`. Because the full payload is folded into the hash, messages sharing a `uid` but with different bytes are treated as distinct. Disabled by default to preserve existing behavior. (#549)
- **`rustgate-client` reference crate** — new workspace member providing an ergonomic client API on top of the existing `rustgate-transport` codecs. Exposes `Client::connect`, `Client::send`, `Client::subscribe`, and `Client::disconnect`, plus a `ClientConfig` builder with mTLS PEM file/byte sources, codec selection, plaintext fallback for tests, optional connect timeout, and opt-in auto-reconnect with exponential backoff (100 ms → 30 s, configurable max attempts). Reuses `FramedCodec` from the transport crate so client and server cannot drift. Includes an `examples/echo.rs` reference echo client and replaces ad-hoc TLS bring-up in two transport integration tests. (#550)
- **TOML configuration file support** — new `--config <path>` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_CONFIG`) loads a TOML file whose keys mirror the existing CLI flag names (kebab-case). Settings are merged in the order **defaults → file → environment variables → CLI flags**, so command-line arguments and environment variables always override file values. Two new utility subcommands ship alongside the flag: `rustgate print-config` writes the effective merged configuration as TOML to stdout, and `rustgate validate-config` parses the file (rejecting unknown keys and missing required fields) and exits non-zero on error. The config file is loaded via the `figment` crate (Apache-2.0/MIT) with the TOML provider; running the server with no subcommand continues to start the listener as before. (#547)
- **OCSP stapling for server certificates** — new `ocsp` feature flag adds OCSP response stapling to the TLS handshake using the `ocsp-stapler` crate (MPL-2.0). When enabled with `--ocsp-stapling` (env `RUSTGATE_OCSP_STAPLING`), the server's certificate resolver is wrapped with `ocsp_stapler::Stapler`, which fetches OCSP responses for the active certificate in a background tokio task and attaches them to the handshake so clients can verify revocation status without contacting the issuing CA. Coexists with the existing CRL-based client certificate verification (the two operate on opposite sides of the handshake). Most useful with enterprise/private CAs that operate their own OCSP responder; Let's Encrypt dropped OCSP support in 2025. The first handshakes after startup may be served without a stapled response until the background fetcher completes its initial cycle. The stapler is gracefully stopped during shutdown. (#524)
- **Per-identity Prometheus metrics with bounded cardinality** — `rustgate_messages_routed_total` and `rustgate_messages_dropped_total` now carry an `identity_cn` label so operators can attribute routing volume and rate-limit drops to individual clients. Cardinality is bounded by an optional CN allowlist loaded from `--metrics-identity-allowlist` (env `RUSTGATE_METRICS_IDENTITY_ALLOWLIST`, one CN per line, `#` comments allowed); CNs not in the allowlist — and **all** CNs when the allowlist is unset — collapse into the literal `"other"` bucket, capping per-identity cardinality at 1 by default. New `--metrics-per-identity` flag (env `RUSTGATE_METRICS_PER_IDENTITY`, default false) opt-in registers Tier 2 per-identity metrics: `rustgate_per_identity_handshake_failures_total`, `rustgate_per_identity_session_duration_seconds`, `rustgate_per_identity_bytes_sent_total`, and `rustgate_per_identity_bytes_received_total`. Tier 2 metrics are not registered when the flag is disabled, imposing zero overhead. (#526)
- **SIGHUP config reload** — on Unix, sending `SIGHUP` to the running server now reloads the server certificate (via the existing `CertReloader`), the group ACL policy (via the existing `AclReloader`), and re-validates the configured CRL file from disk. Each component is reloaded independently — failures in one do not abort the others — and structured `target="audit", event_type="config.reload"` events are emitted per component with `result=success|failure`. SIGHUP reload is Unix-only; on other platforms the handler is not installed. CRL reload re-parses and freshness-checks the file but does not yet swap CRLs into the live TLS listener; applying CRL changes to in-flight handshakes still requires a server restart. (#528)
- **ACL REST API authentication** — new `--acl-api-token-file` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_ACL_API_TOKEN_FILE`) guards the `/api/acl/` endpoints with a bearer token. When unset, the ACL API is not registered at all and `/api/acl/` requests fall through to 404. When set, every request must present `Authorization: Bearer <token>` and the token is compared in constant time. Mutations (`PUT`/`DELETE`) are now persisted atomically to the configured `--group-acl-file` (write-to-tmp + fsync + rename) and the hot reloader's cached hash is updated so the next poll does not re-detect the change. Group identifiers are validated against a strict `[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{1,128}` allow list. (#538)
- **JSON audit log sink** — new `--audit-log` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_AUDIT_LOG`) writes audit events as JSON lines to a dedicated file, separate from the main log stream. Filters only `target="audit"` events using a per-layer `Targets` filter. Use `"-"` for stdout. Audit events now include `session_id` on connect/disconnect for correlation. (#525)
- **ACL deny rules** — `GroupAcl` now supports optional `deny_send` and `deny_receive` lists alongside the existing allow lists. Deny is evaluated first and overrides allow, enabling fine-grained block-list semantics: a CN appearing in a deny list cannot send/receive even when also in the allow list. With an empty allow list, the deny list acts as a pure block list (the group is open except for denied CNs). Legacy ACL JSON files without `deny_send`/`deny_receive` continue to parse unchanged. (#523)
- **ACL management REST API** — new `/api/acl/` endpoints exposed on the metrics HTTP server (mirrors the `/api/federation/` pattern). Supports `GET /api/acl/groups` to list current rules, `PUT /api/acl/groups/{group}` to upsert an entry, `DELETE /api/acl/groups/{group}` to remove one, and `POST /api/acl/reload` to re-read the on-disk ACL file. Mutations rebuild the full policy and atomically swap it on the router via `ArcSwap`. (#522)
- **Hot-reloadable ACL** — the group access control policy can now be updated at runtime without restarting the server. The router's ACL is stored in an `ArcSwap` for lock-free reads, and a new `--acl-reload-interval` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_ACL_RELOAD_INTERVAL`, default 0 = disabled) enables periodic polling of the `--group-acl-file` for changes. File changes are detected via SHA-256 hash comparison; only modified files trigger a policy swap. Audit events are emitted on reload success and failure. (#521)
- **Message priority channels** — replaced the single per-session mpsc channel with a dual-channel approach: a high-priority channel (capacity 16) for Position and Alert messages, and a normal-priority channel (capacity 64) for all other message types. Write loops use biased `tokio::select!` to always drain priority messages first, ensuring time-sensitive situational awareness data is delivered ahead of bulk traffic. The circuit breaker applies across both channels. (#519)

## [0.7.0] - 2026-04-05

### Added

- **Post-quantum key exchange** — new `post-quantum` feature flag enables hybrid X25519MLKEM768 key exchange via aws-lc-rs. PQ-capable clients negotiate hybrid PQ+classical key exchange; others fall back to X25519 transparently. Negotiated key exchange group is now logged for every TLS connection (transport, federation, WebSocket). New CLI flag `--prefer-post-quantum` (env `RUSTGATE_PREFER_POST_QUANTUM`). Requires Go 1.20+ and cmake at build time (same as `--features fips`). (#496, #498)
- **Configurable key exchange groups** — new `--kx-groups` CLI flag (env `RUSTGATE_KX_GROUPS`) allows operators to set the TLS key exchange group preference order at runtime. Comma-separated group names: `X25519MLKEM768`, `SECP256R1MLKEM768`, `MLKEM768`, `MLKEM1024`, `X25519`, `secp256r1`, `secp384r1`. Requires `--features post-quantum`. (#497)
- **Post-quantum integration tests** — `pq_kex.rs` test suite verifies PQ provider installation, X25519MLKEM768 key exchange negotiation, mTLS handshake, CRL composition, and `parse_kx_groups()` validation. CI job `post-quantum` runs clippy, build, and tests with `--features post-quantum`. (#499)
- **Certificate revocation and CRL generation** — new `POST /api/revoke` endpoint revokes certificates by serial number, persisted to a JSON revocation database (`--revocation-db`, env `RUSTGATE_REVOCATION_DB`). CRLs are automatically generated and signed by the enrollment CA, served via `GET /api/crl` as PEM. `GET /api/revoked` lists all revoked entries. Configurable CRL validity period (`--crl-validity-days`, env `RUSTGATE_CRL_VALIDITY_DAYS`, default 7). Revocation reasons supported: `key_compromise`, `ca_compromise`, `superseded`, `cessation_of_operation`, `affiliation_changed`, `privilege_withdrawn`. (#411)
- **HA fencing tokens** — peer ownership claims now carry monotonically increasing fencing tokens (NATS KV revisions) to prevent split-brain in multi-pod deployments. During adoption, the fencing token is re-validated after the delay period to detect stale claims. The connection registry stores fencing tokens alongside connection metadata for observability. Backward-compatible: pods without fencing support interoperate via `Option<u64>` with `#[serde(default)]`. (#458)
- **Data package signature verification** — optional detached signature field on data package uploads. When `--package-signature-ca` is set, uploaded signatures are verified against the CA public key (ECDSA P-256 SHA-256 and RSA-PSS SHA-256). Enable `--package-strict-signatures` to reject unsigned packages. Signatures are stored in metadata, returned in upload/search responses, and served via `X-Package-Signature` header on download. Backward-compatible with existing unsigned packages. (#423)
- **Crypto agility strategy** — `docs/crypto-agility.md` documenting the approach to algorithm migration: feature-gated backends, TLS negotiation, backward-compatible rollout, certificate algorithm migration path, federation crypto alignment, and emergency algorithm disable procedures. (#456)
- **REST API reference documentation** — comprehensive `docs/rest-api.md` covering all HTTP endpoints: metrics/health probes, federation peer management, certificate trust store, enrollment/signing, data packages, and WebSocket transport. Includes request/response schemas, authentication requirements, rate limits, curl examples, and status codes. (#454)
- **Operational documentation** — troubleshooting guide, performance tuning guide, disaster recovery procedures, and four new runbooks (certificate rotation, CRL updates, WebSocket connection storms, TLS handshake failures) (#450, #451, #452, #453)
- **NATS JetStream pull consumer** — subscriber uses a durable JetStream pull consumer for real backpressure instead of buffered push subscriptions. Messages are explicitly acknowledged after processing; the NATS server retains undelivered messages in the stream until the consumer catches up. Configurable via `--nats-live-stream` (env `RUSTGATE_NATS_LIVE_STREAM`, default `RUSTGATE_LIVE`) and `--nats-max-ack-pending` (env `RUSTGATE_NATS_MAX_ACK_PENDING`, default 1000). (#415)
- **JetStream resumption checkpoints** — optional checkpoint file (`--nats-checkpoint-path`, env `RUSTGATE_NATS_CHECKPOINT_PATH`) persists the last processed sequence number so restarts resume from the checkpoint instead of replaying the entire history stream. (#413)
- **Federation replay buffer** — bounded per-peer ring buffer (`--federation-replay-buffer-size`, env `RUSTGATE_FEDERATION_REPLAY_BUFFER_SIZE`, default 1000) stores recent forwarded messages. On reconnection, missed messages can be replayed from the buffer before resuming the live stream. Set to 0 to disable. (#409)
- **WebSocket per-connection rate limiting** — configurable frame-rate (`--ws-max-frames-per-sec`) and byte-rate (`--ws-max-bytes-per-sec`) limits per WebSocket connection. Clients exceeding limits have frames dropped; sustained violations trigger disconnection (`--ws-max-violations`, default 10). All disabled by default for backward compatibility. (#422)
- **Session circuit breaker** — disconnects slow sessions after consecutive send failures exceed a configurable threshold (`--circuit-breaker-threshold`, default 10, env `RUSTGATE_CIRCUIT_BREAKER_THRESHOLD`). Prevents a single backpressured client from degrading group routing for other sessions. Set to 0 to disable. (#420)
- **Per-group access control** — optional JSON-based ACL configuration (`--group-acl-file`, env `RUSTGATE_GROUP_ACL_FILE`) enforces per-group send and receive permissions at router dispatch. Groups listed in the ACL file restrict which certificate CNs may send to or receive from the group. Groups not listed operate in open mode (any member can send/receive), preserving backward compatibility. (#416, #417)

## [0.6.0] - 2026-04-04

### Changed

- **Standardized environment variable prefixes** — all environment variables now use the `RUSTGATE_` prefix for consistency and to avoid collisions with other services. `NATS_*` → `RUSTGATE_NATS_*`, `FEDERATION_*` → `RUSTGATE_FEDERATION_*`, `ENROLLMENT_*` → `RUSTGATE_ENROLLMENT_*`, `METRICS_BIND` → `RUSTGATE_METRICS_BIND`, `WEBSOCKET_BIND` → `RUSTGATE_WEBSOCKET_BIND`, `DATA_PACKAGE_DIR` → `RUSTGATE_DATA_PACKAGE_DIR`. `OTEL_*` vars unchanged (OpenTelemetry standard). **Breaking change** — update deployment configs, Helm values, and shell scripts. (#392)
- **WebSocket codec migration** — WebSocket transport now uses the unified `Codec` trait (`Arc<dyn Codec>`) instead of the legacy `WsEncoding` enum and `MessageEncoder` trait. Removed `WsEncoding`, `MessageEncoder`, `RawEncoder`, `CotEncoder`, and `TakProtobufEncoder`. The `WsServerConfig.encoding` field is replaced by `WsServerConfig.codec`. (#391)

### Added

- **Pluggable codec architecture** — unified `Codec` trait in `rustgate-core` replaces the separate `Framing`/`Encoding` dispatch. Each wire protocol (`TakXmlCodec`, `TakProtobufCodec`, `RawLineCodec`, `RawLengthPrefixedCodec`) implements a single trait covering framing strategy, payload decode/encode, and protocol detection. Session handling uses `Arc<dyn Codec>` instead of enum-matching, simplifying the read/write loops and enabling future custom codecs. Existing `--framing`/`--encoding` CLI flags are preserved via a factory function. (#184)
- **Server certificate hot-reload** — `--cert-reload-interval` enables periodic polling of the `--cert` and `--key` files. When changes are detected (via SHA-256 digest comparison), the server certificate is atomically swapped for new TLS handshakes without restart or downtime. Audit events emitted on rotation success/failure. (#291)
- **OpenTelemetry distributed tracing** — optional `otel` feature flag adds OTLP trace export via `--otel-endpoint`. Key spans instrumented: TLS handshake, message routing, federation forwarding, NATS publish/subscribe. Works with Jaeger, Grafana Tempo, and any OTLP-compatible backend. (#281)
- **Dynamic TLS config refresh** — trust store certificate changes (add/remove via REST API or filesystem) take effect on the next TLS handshake without server restart. Inbound V1 uses a `DynamicCertVerifier`, outbound V1 uses a `DynamicPeerConnector` that rebuilds `ClientConfig` on generation change. (#192)
- **Circuit breaker metrics and audit events** — Prometheus counters for circuit breaker trips/recoveries and structured audit events for state transitions (#289)
- **External Secrets Operator support** — optional ExternalSecret templates for NATS, enrollment, and federation secrets with configurable `secretStoreRef` and `refreshInterval` (#275)
- **Anti-replay detection** — sliding-window filter drops messages with duplicate `(uid, time)` pairs within a configurable window, preventing message replay attacks. Configurable via `--replay-window` (default 300s, 0 to disable). (#284)
- **Comprehensive documentation** — configuration reference completeness (plaintext, rate limiting, CRL, archive, federation V2/HA, OTel sections), STRIDE threat model, graceful degradation policy, post-quantum TLS roadmap, expanded NIST 800-53 control mapping (CP, IR, RA, CA families), module-level docs across all source files, XML-to-protobuf field loss documentation, metrics endpoint security warning, position cache TTL semantics (#261, #256, #263, #279, #280, #290, #295, #308, #311)
- **FIPS-enabled release binaries** — release workflow produces FIPS 140-3 binaries (aws-lc-rs) for x86_64-unknown-linux-musl and aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu alongside standard binaries. aarch64 FIPS uses gnu target because aws-lc-fips-sys is incompatible with musl cross-compilation. CI validates FIPS cross-compilation for both targets. (#366)

### Fixed

- **Timestamp parsing panics** — prevent panics when CoT and protobuf parsers encounter malformed timestamps with out-of-range values (#371)

### Security

- **Quarterly advisory review** — upgraded async-nats 0.46→0.47, resolving RUSTSEC-2025-0134 (rustls-pemfile unmaintained) and RUSTSEC-2026-0049 (rustls-webpki CRL matching). RUSTSEC-2026-0042 (aws-lc-sys) was previously resolved. All advisory ignores removed from deny.toml — zero known vulnerabilities. (#239)

## [0.5.0] - 2026-04-01

### Added

- **TLS handshake concurrency limiting** — configurable semaphore to bound concurrent TLS handshakes and prevent resource exhaustion (#345)
- **CRL freshness validation** — warn on stale CRLs whose `nextUpdate` has passed; configurable via `--crl-warn-stale` (#347)
- **Configurable TLS version minimum** — `--tls-min-version` flag to enforce TLS 1.3-only mode; key usage validation on client certificates (#348)
- **Per-connection WebSocket rate limiting** — reuses `--client-message-rate` and `--client-burst` config for WebSocket sessions (#346)
- **NATS mTLS support** — client certificate authentication for NATS broker connections via `NatsTlsConfig` (#342)
- **Sensitive data masking** — `mask_cert_serial`, `mask_ip`, `mask_credential` utilities for structured log fields (#343)
- **Security audit events** — CRL reload, enrollment certificate issued, rate limit triggered, TLS handshake failed audit events (#343)
- **Identity validation** — `Identity::new()` returns `Result` with validation errors for empty CN/fingerprint (#334)
- **Structured fuzzing** — `Arbitrary` trait implementations behind `fuzz` feature flag for `RoutableMessage` and `CotEvent` (#341)
- **Fuzz targets** — JWT parse, CSR parse, NATS wire event, WebSocket frame, CRL parse, and protobuf roundtrip fuzz targets (#344)
- **Image digest pinning** — Helm deployment supports `image.digest` for immutable container references (#340)
- **NetworkPolicy** — Helm template for network-level pod isolation, enabled in production values (#340)
- **Integration tests** — 150+ new tests across core types, proto codec, server CLI/TLS, and feature gates (#359, #361, #362, #363)
- **1100+ tests** — up from 887 in v0.4.0

### Fixed

- **Position cache memory exhaustion** — bounded cache size prevents unbounded growth (#316)
- **JWT revocation cache memory exhaustion** — bounded cache with LRU eviction (#315)
- **ClientThrottle integer overflow** — prevent token refill overflow on long-running connections (#317)
- **Federation forward lock contention** — snapshot client list to reduce hold time during message forwarding (#318)
- **Helm image tag** — corrected to match workspace version (#319)
- **Workspace dependency inheritance** — enforced for socket2, prost-build, tikv-jemallocator (#320)
- **Certificate field sanitization** — escape JSONL output and bound NATS token length (#322)
- **Enrollment rate limiter pruning** — periodic cleanup of expired entries; URL-decode query parameters (#321)
- **JWT HMAC implementation** — replaced custom HMAC with `hmac` crate; enforce minimum secret length (#323)
- **Silent message drops** — added structured logging for dropped messages in router and federation (#324)
- **Async archive I/O** — moved archive writes off the async event loop; removed `block_on` from federation tests (#325)
- **Dead metrics code** — removed unreachable metrics paths; added `Display` impls; fixed CRL serial number formatting (#330)
